# HANDOFF — Porting GLM-5.3-Flash to a Vyb-native engine

**Written 2026-10-30.** Read this first; it is the entry point for a fresh session.

The goal: GLM-5.3-Flash (the EXL3 4 bpw checkpoint the TensorFold rig currently serves on
the dual DGX Sparks) running as a **Vyb-native inference engine** in
`VybForge/native/glm53` — no Python in the runtime path. Every stage is gated against a
numpy/HF oracle derived from the real checkpoint before it is allowed to stand.

Priority per Rick: **speed and fidelity to the model.** The Sparks are the deployment
target; the 3090 (godzilla) is the reference machine while the Sparks cannot read the
weights (see §8).

---

## 1. Where things stand

| thing | state |
|---|---|
| loader (83 shards, 164 GB, 150,226 tensors) | gated, 2.3 s for the whole plan |
| layer 0 (KDA attention + dense MLP) | gated, every stage ≤6e-11 |
| layer 3 (DSA attention + router + MoE + shared + hc) | **L3_FULL_VYB_MATCH: OK**, all six stages |
| full 45-layer prefill | **runs to completion**, `PREFILL_OK layers=45`, **914 s (15:14), peak RSS 7.1 GB** — was 5697 s / 53.6 GB; see §6 |
| KV cache / decode | **step decode in Vyb: DEC_STEP_VYB OK** — `glm_decode_step.vyb` runs layer 0 one token at a time with the KDA recurrence and conv rings carried, matching the oracle's two teacher-forced steps at worst 1.9e-12 (`make -f native/Makefile decstep`). **Layer 3 too: DEC_L3_STEP_VYB OK** (`glm_decode_l3.vyb`, `make decl3step`) — ckv [T,512] / indexer k+gsc [T,128] caches carried and appended per step, decode-layout pool+topk, masked MLA over cached ckv, router + top-8 EXL3 experts; attn elementwise 2.6e-11, mlp/streams2 digest ≤6.7e-6 vs the 2e-4 L3_FULL convention (the elementwise residue is EXL3 dequant noise). **Full chain: DECODE_CHAIN_MATCH OK** (`glm_decode_chain.vyb`, `make decchain`, ~50 min on the 3090) — prefill-then-decode, all caches built in-process, two teacher-forced steps through all 45 layers BIT-EXACT vs the batched prefill at every layer (worst elementwise 0.000e+00; the prefill is the oracle, per HANDOFF §11). Generated by `native/glm53/build_decode_chain.py` — edit that + `_chain_main_snippet.txt` and regenerate, never hand-edit the copied blocks. **MTP head: DECODE_MTP_MATCH OK** (`make decmtp`) — layer 45 runs inside the chain (no hc; own DSA cache primed during prefill via "phase 1b: MTP head over 20 prompt tokens"), draft hidden worst 1.2e-4 row-relative vs the fp64 oracle, logits worst 1.7e-3 (tol 1e-2), draft top-1 driver==oracle (token 576; the first MTP gate pass was vacuous — the run behind it had failed at layer 7 under GPU memory pressure and the verifier read stale partial-run dumps; the clean re-run with the card free is the record). Semantics pinned from exllamav3 glm5_next_mtp (§11) |
| TP=2 across the Sparks | not started |
| endpoint | not started; `verify_full_forward.py` is still a stub |

Repos: Vyb `fd0bf4ee`, VybForge `081d6a5` (both pushed, worktrees clean).
Everything below is reproducible from those two commits.

---

## 2. Architecture — the facts that cost the most time to establish

45 decoder layers (**0..44**) plus **layer 45 = the MTP (multi-token-prediction) head**, which
is *not* a decoder layer: it carries `eh_proj` / `enorm` / `hnorm` and its own 288 experts.
Driving layer 45 through the decoder path fails with `PF_FAIL attention layer 45` — that is
correct behaviour, not a bug. The MTP head is for speculative decoding only.

    attention: KDA (gated delta-rule)  on n % 4 != 3   → 34 layers
               DSA (sparse/deepseek attn) on n % 4 == 3 → 11 layers (3,7,...,43)
    ffn:       dense SwiGLU MLP on layers 0-2 only
               288-expert top-8 MoE on layers 3-44
    every layer has two hyper-connection (hc) sites: `hc_attn_*` and `hc_ffn_*`

Model constants: H=4096, HEADS=64, HDIM=128, NOPE=256, VDIM=256, KV_LORA=512, Q_LORA=1536,
IDIM=128, IHEADS=32, KPOOL=4, indexer top-k=2048, INTER=12288, EINTER=2048 (MoE expert
hidden), 288 experts, top-8, routed_scaling_factor=2.5, EPS=1e-5, hc eps=1e-6,
sinkhorn_iters=20, 45 layers.

Checkpoint (godzilla): `/mnt/apex/rick/vybforge/glm53-checkpoint`
(`model.safetensors.index.json`, `model-000NN-of-00083.safetensors`, `.COMPLETE`).
Only the routed experts are EXL3-quantized (`quantization_config.scope =
glm53_routed_experts_only`, bits=4, codebook `mcg`); everything else is bf16/f32.

**The checkpoint is the authority, not exllamav3.** The indexer is HF's
`Glm5NextTextIndexer` (learned gpool, per-slot bias, no rope) — exllamav3's `QSAIndexer`
does not match the shards. See `native/glm53/dsa_ref.py`.

Vyb is a 3 KDA : 1 DSA pattern, so **the same driver code must dispatch per layer** — this is
where a naive "layers 0-2 KDA, 3-44 DSA" assumption burns a run.

---

## 3. Layout

    native/glm53/          python oracles (numpy fp64) + verifiers, one per stage
      loader.py kv_cache.py mla_ref.py dsa_ref.py layer0_ref.py gdn_ref.py moe_ref.py hc_ref.py
      gold_layer0.py gold_l3_attn.py gold_l3_moe.py gold_l3_hc.py gold_exl3_deq.py
      verify_layer0_vyb.py verify_l3_vyb.py verify_l3_moe_vyb.py verify_l3_tail_vyb.py
      verify_l3_full.py verify_l3_hc.py verify_exl3_load.py verify_l3_moe_all.py
      exp_env.py gx_ref.py gx_plan_ref.py README.md
    native/host/           the drivers (Vyb programs)
      glm_layer0_fwd.vyb   layer 0 end to end (KDA + dense MLP)
      glm_l3_attn_fwd.vyb  layer 3 attention site alone
      glm_l3_moe_fwd.vyb   router + shared expert
      glm_l3_moe_all.vyb   router + every routed expert + shared, all 22 tokens
      glm_l3_expert_fwd.vyb one token's routed experts
      glm_exl3_check.vyb   EXL3 dequant at real expert shape
      glm_l3_hc_check.vyb  the hc mix/apply in isolation (fast localiser, seconds)
      glm_l3_full.vyb      layer 3 start to finish, one process
      glm_l3_tail_fwd.vyb  hc_ffn mix -> norm -> MoE -> hc_apply
      glm_prefill.vyb      THE 45-layer forward (embeddings -> 45 layers -> streams)
    native/exl3/           EXL3 reference + map.bin (256-slot tile map) + gen_map_bin.py
    native/kernels/        Vyb kernels: exl3.vyb (deq/hgen/had1/had2), gemm.vyb, bf16.vyb, ...
    native/torchload/      safetensors.vyb (st_open, st_arr_ints)
    native/json/           json_parse.vyb (fast paths; do not regress them)

Makefile targets (from the repo root): `gx`, `gxload`, `gxplan`, `glm3`, `glm3moe`,
`glm3exp`, `glm3hc`, `glm3tail`, `glm3all`, `glm3x`, `glm3full`, `prefill`.

Invocation (every driver):

    VYB_STDLIB=~/Projects/Vyb/stdlib ~/Projects/Vyb/build/vyb \
      --module-path native/json --module-path native/torchload --module-path native/glm53 \
      --module-path native/kernels --module-path native/tensor \
      --module-path ~/Projects/Vyb/bindings/cuda --module-path ~/Projects/Vyb/stdlib/collections \
      native/host/<driver>.vyb

Python oracle env: `env -u PYTHONPATH ~/venvs/exl3/bin/python`. The conda
`conda-anaconda-tos` warning on every shell is harmless.

## 4. Gates — what passes, with the tolerances that were actually measured

| gate | target | result |
|---|---|---|
| `GX_INDEX_MATCH` | 150,226 names vs the python loader | OK |
| `GX_LOAD_MATCH` | tensor bytes, byte-exact | OK |
| `GX_PLAN_MATCH` | whole-checkpoint sha256 digest, 2.3 s | OK |
| `LAYER0_VYB_MATCH` | KDA + dense MLP, every stage | ≤6e-11 |
| `EXL3_LOAD_VYB` | expert gate_proj [4096,2048] vs P1 oracle | sq 8.4e-7, max 7.8e-5 |
| `L3_ATTN_VYB_MATCH` | DSA attention, 22 tokens | attn 2.7e-12, selections 22/22 |
| `L3_MOE_ROUTE_VYB` | top-8 sets | 22/22 identical |
| `L3_MOE_SHARED_VYB` | shared expert | 1.5e-11 |
| `L3_EXPERT_VYB` | one token's routed contribution | abs 2.3e-5, max 8.1e-5 |
| `L3_MOE_ALL_VYB` | full mlp [22,4096], 109 experts | abs 5.3e-6, max 1.1e-5 |
| `L3_HC_VYB` | hc mix/apply in isolation | comb 2.2e-8, streams1 2.1e-9 |
| `L3_TAIL_VYB` | hc_ffn -> MoE -> hc_apply | h2 7e-11, mlp 8.5e-6, streams2 1e-5 |
| `L3_FULL_VYB_MATCH` | layer 3 end to end | h_a 5.8e-11, attn 2.8e-11, streams1 2.1e-9, h2 3.4e-9, mlp 5.3e-6, streams2 3.3e-6 |

Notes on the tolerances: the EXL3 path carries fp16 butterfly noise, so `mlp`/`streams2` sit
at 1e-5..1e-6 and a 2e-4 gate is the honest bar. When comparing a *sum* of signed values that
nearly cancels, compare against the `abs` sum, not the sum's own magnitude — a 1e-4 gate on a
1/700-cancelling sum measures noise.

Prefill beyond layer 3 has **no oracle**. Layers 0-3 are gold-matched end to end, every site
is gated individually, and the 45-layer run's per-layer digests are finite and monotone in
magnitude — but "plausible" is the strongest claim for layers 4-44 today:

    max |stream| per layer, 45-layer run:
    0: 0.059  3: 2.65  10: 9.4  18: 24.1  29: 27.1  43: 47.8  44: 290

The 6× jump at layer 44 is the first thing to check once a reference exists past layer 3.

---

## 5. Runtime bugs found and fixed on the way (all in Vyb, all pushed)

These were blockers, not polish. Each has a repro or a test.

1. **`alloca` in loop bodies** (SIGSEGV in long loops). Codegen emitted temporaries at point
   of use; a 130k-iteration loop overflowed the 8 MB stack. Fixed by `hoistStaticAllocas`
   (`src/vre/llvm/cgen_util.cpp`), filtering on `isa<ConstantInt>(getArraySize())`.
2. **String registry live-count drift** (progressive slowdown). Reusing a tombstone slot did
   not increment the live count while `release()` always decremented → the table stopped
   growing, `keyed get`/parse went O(n²). 83 shard headers: 1.4 s empty vs 127 s with 150k
   live Strings. Fixed in `runtime/vyb_runtime.c` (rehash re-derives the live count).
3. **`read_all`/`read_at` over-registered their buffer** (the OOM killer). This is the big
   one. The buffer is handed back as a String *value* that the destination retains (+1) and
   releases; registering it with a hard-coded refcount of 1 on top left every destination at
   refs 2 with one release, so **each call leaked the whole buffer**: 200 reads of a 4 MB
   file grew RSS 74 MB → 891 MB. The prefill reads ~327 tensors per MoE layer (~1.3 GB) and
   died at layer 25. Fixed by `vyb_string_register_refs(p, refs)` with
   `__vyb_string_register` (refs 1, transfer producers) and
   `__vyb_string_register_borrowed` (refs 0, value hand-offs); the two file-read paths use
   the borrowed form. Fixtures: `repro/string_read_leak.vyb`, `repro/string_assign_leak.vyb`.
   **Do not** apply the borrowed form to the other 33 String-returning runtime helpers
   (net/tls/regex/exec/base64/ed25519/env): that is use-after-free on paths the codegen
   classifies as transfers — five tests catch it (chain, chain_signed, websocket, ed25519,
   nested-Vec write-back). They need per-site classification.
4. **`@noinline` was stripped at -O2** by the attribute sweep that exists to undo the
   Phase-6.4 trace default. Codegen now tags an explicit `@noinline` with
   `vyb-explicit-noinline`; the sweep skips it. `test/kernel/test_noinline_helper.vyb`.
5. **Vyb has no FFI across module boundaries.** An `extern "C"` block inside an imported
   module is invisible to that module's own functions (`Undefined identifier: cuMemFree_v2`).
   Modules must go through `bindings/cuda` (`cuda_mem_alloc`, `cuda_memcpy_h2d`,
   `cuda_launch`, `cuda_sync`, `cuda_mem_free`); `cuda_launch` takes ONE argument, so a
   kernel with several parameters needs a params buffer (as the exl3 kernels do) or
   `cuda_launch4i`, which only works from a driver.
6. **Vyb language traps** that cost real time: `fail` and `var` are reserved words; `^` is
   XOR, not power; structs are value types (by-value parameters deep-copy — this silently
   turned 9k-node JSON docs into 5.5 s per shard header); loop counters must be declared in
   the enclosing scope; `return <int>` inside a `Vec<Float>` function is a cast error (return
   a typed empty vector); `.to_string()` on an Int is fine but there is no bitcast from Int
   to String.

Driver bugs fixed (in VybForge): the hc comb softmax used a **global** max instead of a
per-row max, so rows far below the block peak underflowed to exp(0)=0 and the small entries
were wrong by 6× (streams1 off 1.8e-3); and `TOPK` collided — the attention site's indexer
top-k is 2048 while the router wants top-8, so defining `TOPK` once made the router select
288 experts per token (it looked exactly like "routing is chaotic under a 1e-10 h2 change",
ran the expert path hundreds of extra times and OOM-killed the driver). The router constant
is `ROUTE_TOPK`.

Two more driver-level root causes were found and fixed later, both measured: the per-element
`htod_vec`/`dtoh_vec` copy path in `native/tensor/tensor.vyb` (now `bulk_htod`/`bulk_dtoh`),
and `gx_resolve` calling `st_open` once per wanted name instead of once per shard. See §6 —
those two were 4.3x of wall time and 7.8x of peak RSS on the 45-layer prefill.

---

## 6. Performance — measured, and what it means

45-layer prefill, 22 tokens, 3090: **914 s (15:14), peak RSS 7.1 GB** after the fixes below.
The progression, each step verified value-identical at all 45 layers: 5697 s (as written) ->
1315 s (bulk DMA + shard-header cache) -> 908-914 s (deq scale/map bulk copies, `st_find` borrow,
index hoist). 6.2x total; the last three micro-fixes moved the layer-3 driver from 16.4 s to
13.5 s but are within noise at 45 layers, because the drift described at the end of this section
dominates at depth. Per-layer digests are identical to the pre-fix run at every layer
(max|stream| 0.059 / 2.653 / 9.405 / 24.151 / 27.072 / 47.781 / 290.367 on layers 0/3/10/18/29/43/44),
so both fixes are value-exact.

**The old profile blamed the fp64 gemm. It was the copy path.** Instrumented layer 3 (the
`T_*` prints in `native/host/glm_l3_full.vyb`), milliseconds:

| phase | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| indexer deq (4 bf16 tensors) | 12 | 6 |
| `mm_host` qidx / kraw / gsc / wproj | 522 / 328 / 330 / 322 | 2 / 1 / 1 / 1 |
| uk/uv build + K/V gemms ("pool_sel 62 s" in the old note) | 64 540 | 171 |
| poolk / index scores / selection | 1 / 0 / 0 | 1 / 0 / 0 |
| masked MLA | 18 | 18 |
| o_proj | 1596 | 16 |

Two root causes, both now fixed:

1. **`htod_vec`/`dtoh_vec` in `native/tensor/tensor.vyb` moved one 8-byte element per
   `cuMemcpy`.** ~4 us of driver overhead per element: a 90k-element activation upload cost
   330 ms and the 16.7M-element `kv_b` download cost ~60 s. `bulk_htod` / `bulk_dtoh` use
   `addr(v)` — a `Vec<Float>` is `{data ptr, size, cap}`, so the first word of the header is
   the element buffer — and move the whole buffer in one call. `bulk_htod` takes
   `their<Vec<Float> const>` and is called as `view(x)`: a by-value Vec parameter would
   deep-copy 134 MB before the copy even started. All 26 call sites in `glm_l3_full.vyb` and
   `glm_prefill.vyb` now use the bulk form.
2. **`gx_resolve` called `st_open` once per wanted NAME** (`native/glm53/gx.vyb`): 9 header
   parses (8 ms each) per expert resolve, and the `StIndex` structs accumulated — that was the
   "residual ~1.2 GB/layer". Each expert is resolved once per layer, 109 experts per MoE layer,
   so it cost ~8 s/layer and the growth that made late layers 2.5x slower than early ones.
   Now the last opened header is kept and reopened only when the shard changes: one 9-name
   resolve went 98 ms -> 18 ms and 100 resolves 9.85 s/1.18 GB -> 1.84 s/0.22 GB.

Result: the prefill's per-layer time is flat (~23 s/layer) instead of rising, and RSS peaks at
6.9 GB — small enough that a KV cache now fits on the 3090 box.

Per-expert profile inside a MoE layer, before these fixes (still useful for what remains):

    resolve (gx_resolve)      89 ms   -> ~10 ms (fixed: header reuse)
    deq (3 matrices)          93 ms      bulk DMA, fine
    gemms + host math        212 ms      mostly gone: mm_host was per-element DMA
    ────────────────────────────────
    ~380 ms/expert × 109 = ~42 s/layer of the ~210 s

Two more, same class, found by instrumenting the MoE layer the same way (layer 3, 109 routed
experts, milliseconds per layer):

| phase | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| resolve (`gx_resolve` per expert) | 2346 | 1418 at ONE layer / 2355-4700 per layer at depth — see 4. |
| deq pipeline (9 tensors) | 9013 | 6843 |
| the three gemms | 228 | 232 |
| gather / SwiGLU / scatter host math | 3 | 7 |

3. **The EXL3 map body and both fp16 scale vectors were uploaded 8 bytes at a time** inside
   `exl3_deq` — 1536 `cuMemcpyHtoD` calls per matrix, ~3.3 s per MoE layer. Both are Strings, so
   each is now ONE copy (`cuMemcpyHtoD_v2(sud, suh, nsuh)`); the lengths are hoisted out of the
   `freedom` block because `.len()` on an outer String is not readable inside one.
4. **Tried: `gx_resolve` once per layer for all 288 experts' 2592 names** (indexed with
   `i0 = e * 9`). It is 40% cheaper at a single layer (1418 ms vs 2346 ms) and gives identical
   digests, **but it does not hold at depth**: in the 12-layer prefill the resolve term grew
   ~1 s per layer (1.7 -> 9.5 s by layer 11, measured), and the full 45-layer run went from
   21:55 to 25:53. Per-expert resolution also drifts but only ~0.3 s/layer. String churn is the
   suspected mechanism (a 2592-entry `Vec<String>` per layer vs 109 short-lived 9-name ones);
   it is a compiler/runtime question, not a driver one. **Reverted in the prefill**
   (`glm_prefill.vyb`), kept in the single-layer gate driver (`glm_l3_full.vyb`) where it is a
   straight win. This is the one change here that was measured, looked good, and was undone.

5. **Two more, after the reverted experiment, both kept:**
   - `st_find` took `StIndex` BY VALUE, so every name lookup deep-copied the shard header
     (~1185 names + six Vecs). Resolving one expert's 9 tensors was 9 header copies. It now
     takes `their<StIndex const>` and is called as `view(ix)`: layer 3's resolve went
     1450 -> 801 ms. Call sites updated: `native/glm53/gx.vyb`, `native/whisper/host/forward_driver.vyb`.
   - The 15.5 MB index was re-read and re-parsed for every expert. `gx_resolve` now delegates to
     `gx_resolve_idx(index, ...)`, and the prefill's MoE loop reads the index once per layer:
     ~200-500 ms/layer, small but free.

   Neither fixes the **drift**: resolve still rises ~250 ms per layer (1866 ms at layer 3 ->
   4160 ms at layer 11 in the 12-layer test), while deq/mm stay flat and the digests stay
   identical. At this point the evidence is that String-heavy code slows down as the process
   ages (fewer strings created does not help, borrowed params do not help, one big resolve does
   not help) — i.e. the String registry / allocator, not the driver. That needs a Vyb-side
   experiment, and Vyb is the implementation agent's territory.

Finally, the deq itself: instrumented per stage, one matrix costs deq 1.0 ms, hgen 0.0 ms,
**had1 8.0 ms, had2 8.2 ms** — so the two Hadamard passes are 5.3 s of a ~8.7 s MoE layer, and
they are fp64. **That, not the gemm, is the next speed lever**: the gemm pair is 232 ms/layer
(0.7 ms per expert), so wiring `gemm32_p` into `mm_host` would buy ~2%, while an f32
deq/hadamard path is where the remaining time actually is. f32 also halves the 8-byte-per-element
writes on a bandwidth-starved Spark.

Layer-3 driver after all of this: 16.4 s -> 13.5 s, `L3_FULL_VYB_MATCH` unchanged at
streams2 abs 3.32e-06.

**The biggest remaining item is the growth, not the arithmetic.** Across the 45-layer run the
per-layer `gx_resolve` term drifts from 2.3 s (layer 3) to 12.2 s (layer 44) while deq/mm stay
flat, so at layer 44 resolve is ~half the layer. It is the same String-churn shape that made the
resolve-all variant lose; anything that touches how names/index/headers are handled per layer
should be measured over 12+ layers, never on one. A Vyb runtime question (String registry live
count / Vec<String> release) is the plausible root, and it is worth one focused experiment
before more driver-level tuning.

---

## 7. Memory budget traps (environment, not code)

- **The Hermes gateway cgroup caps `MemoryMax=4 GiB`.** Every long job launched from a
  session is OOM-killed at ~4 GB RSS regardless of the host's 125 GB. Symptom:
  `rc=137`, `Memory cgroup out of memory: Killed process … anon-rss:4159220kB`. Launch heavy
  runs with
  `systemd-run --user --scope -q -p MemoryMax=80G --unit=<name> bash -c '<cmd>'`.
- `malloc_trim(0)` once per layer is worth keeping (EXL3 trellis churn retains ~0.5-0.8 GB
  per layer in allocator arenas) but it is not a substitute for the leak fix.
- Residual growth after the leak fix is **gone**: the ~1.2 GB/layer was `gx_resolve` retaining a
  parsed `StIndex` per wanted name (§6). The 45-layer peak is now 6.9 GB, so a KV cache fits.

---

## 8. Where the weights live (and why the Sparks cannot run yet)

    godzilla : /mnt/apex/rick/vybforge/glm53-checkpoint   83 shards, 164 GB, readable
               /etc/exports is EMPTY — not NFS-exported
    lefty    : /root/.cache/huggingface/hub/models--neko-legends--GLM-5.3-Flash-Uncensored-EXL3/
               snapshots/07135ec0…/   ← what the TensorFold rig actually serves (prod.env)
               root-owned; rick cannot read it; rick's sudo is scoped to drop_caches
               the rig is live on :8000 and answers /v1/models with GLM-5.3-Flash
    righty   : /mnt/apex mounted but EMPTY, root fs 100% full (942 MB free)
    lefty    : /mnt/apex mounted but EMPTY, 23 GB free, 116 GB RAM held by the TF rig
    both     : CUDA 13 + nvcc, aarch64, GB10, `~/Projects/Vyb/build/vyb` exists

So: the model *is* on a Spark (root's HF cache) and the Spark is the deployment target, but
the engine cannot read those weights today. Unblock with either
`chown -R rick` on that snapshot (or a bind-mount), or export `/mnt/apex/rick` from godzilla
and mount it on righty (righty has RAM free; lefty does not). Once either is done, run the
same 4-layer gate on the Spark (`VYBFORGE_NLAYERS=4`) for a real number instead of the
extrapolation below.

Spark expectations (from spec, not measurement): GB10 LPDDR5X ≈ 273 GB/s vs the 3090's
936 GB/s, fp64 also weak, 121 GB usable. Bandwidth-bound work (dequant writing f64) should be
~3× slower per byte; capacity-bound work is far better. This is exactly why the f32
conversion and kernelized `pool_sel` matter more on the Sparks than on the 3090.

---

## 9. Next steps, in order

**Done since this was written:** the layer-3 profile (the 76 s / 62 s attribution was wrong — it
was the per-element copy path), the bulk-copy fix, the `gx_resolve` header-reuse fix, and the
45-layer re-run at 1315 s / 6.9 GB. Details in §6.

1. **Unblock the Spark weights** (§8), then run `VYBFORGE_NLAYERS=4` there and record the real
   per-phase numbers; re-plan speed work against those. This is now the cheapest real work
   available and it is blocked on access, not code.
2. **The per-layer `gx_resolve` growth** (§6): 2.3 s at layer 3 -> 12.2 s at layer 44, ~40% of a
   late layer, while deq and the gemms stay flat. Same String-churn family as the resolve-all
   regression, so it is one experiment in the Vyb runtime (String registry live count /
   `Vec<String>` release) rather than more driver tuning. Measure over 12+ layers.
3. **f32 in the EXL3 deq + Hadamard path** — after the growth this is the next lever (had1+had2
   = 5.3 s of a ~8.7 s MoE layer, all fp64; the gemms are 232 ms). The 3090's fp64 rate is 1/64
   of fp32 and GB10's is weak too, and f32 halves the writes. Gate: `L3_FULL_VYB_MATCH` must
   stay OK (tolerance for mlp/streams is 2e-4, so f32's ~1e-6 is not the risk — the risk is the
   `deq -> had1 -> had2 -> gemm` dtype plumbing, so add an f32 gemm at the same time).
4. **KV cache + one-token decode.** Prefill needs no cache (at S=22 each attention sees the
   whole prompt); generation does. `native/glm53/kv_cache.py` is the reference sketch and the
   `rdelta*` kernels already carry the KDA recurrence — what is missing is carrying the
   per-layer KDA state and the DSA latent/indexer keys across steps.

   **The step-decode oracle now exists** (`native/glm53/gold_decode_step.py`, layer 0 =
   KDA + dense MLP + both hc sites). It threads the two pieces of state the batched gold throws
   away: the KDA recurrence (64x128x128 per layer) and the k=4 depthwise conv, which needs the
   last 3 PRE-CONV vectors per channel for q, k and v (a ring buffer, not a scalar carry).
   Self-gates, both printed by the run:
     conv_step_vs_batch                        max_abs 0.0            (the ring order is the check)
     stepwise walk vs gated gold_l0_streams2    max_abs 1.388e-16      (fp64 noise)
   so the per-token path IS the gated layer-0 math. It also emits 2 teacher-forced decode steps
   (token ids 13, 10788 = the prompt's last two) with `gold_dec_step{0,1}_{streams2,attn,mlp}.npy`
   and `gold_decode.json`. Next unit: a Vyb driver (`native/host/glm_decode_step.vyb`) that
   reproduces those two arrays, then the same shape for layer 3 (DSA latent + indexer key cache),
   then all 45 layers.

   The oracle also dumps the exact state a decode step STARTS from, so that driver can run one
   step in isolation instead of re-deriving the 22-token prefill. Both `.npy` and raw
   little-endian f64 `.bin` (the `.bin` is what a Vyb driver can actually read — it has no .npy
   parser), in `native/out/`:
     gold_dec_state_kda_start.bin  [64,128,128]  8,388,608 B   carried recurrence
     gold_dec_ring_{q,k,v}.bin     [3,8192]        196,608 B   pre-conv history for the k=4 conv
     gold_dec_streams_prev.bin     [4,4096]        131,072 B   the last prefill token's streams2,
                                                               i.e. decode step 0's input
     gold_dec_step{0,1}_{streams2,attn,mlp}.npy                the two steps' expected outputs

   So the gate for the driver is: read those five files, run one step, match
   `gold_dec_step0_attn.npy` and `gold_dec_step0_streams2.npy`. The new logic it needs is exactly
   two state-threading changes to the already-gated layer-0 code: `delta_rule` must take and
   return `st` instead of zeroing it per call, and `conv_silu` must take the 3-row ring instead of
   rebuilding the conv from the prompt. Everything else (hc mix/apply, rmsnorm, the gemms, the
   gated norm, o_proj, the dense MLP) is unchanged and already gated at 1.87e-11.

   **DONE: `DEC_STEP_VYB` is gated** (commit 00902a2) — see the gate table at the top. Two bugs
   the build caught, both worth remembering: a `ringq<Vec<Float>> = Vec()` inside the step loop
   declared a loop-scoped shadow so the rotated ring never reached the next step (every step
   convolved against the prefill's original window), and a digest-only gate hid a 19% streams1
   error behind a matching max — decode steps are gated element-wise, not by digest.
5. **Extend the oracle past layer 3**: a gold for layer 4 (KDA + MoE — the one combination no
   gold covers) using the same numpy path as `gold_l3_moe.py`, then check the layer-44
   magnitude jump (still 6.08x, reproduced at 47.781 -> 290.367).
6. **TP=2 across lefty/righty**, then the OpenAI-compatible endpoint, then
   `verify_full_forward.py` stops being a stub.

---

## 11. P6 chained decode — design (next unit)

Goal: one driver, `native/host/glm_decode_chain.vyb`, that runs the FULL 45-layer model as
prefill-then-decode, with every cache built and carried in-process. The two single-layer
decoders (`glm_decode_step.vyb` layer 0/KDA, `glm_decode_l3.vyb` layer 3/DSA) are the per-layer
templates; both are gated.

**Architecture: two phases, caches built in-process** (not pre-loaded from oracle dumps —
45 layers x caches is too many dumps, and phase 1 reuses the already-gated batched kernels):

- Phase 1 (prefill): the existing batched 22-token forward, verbatim from `glm_prefill.vyb`,
  but SAVING per-layer state as it goes:
    KDA layers (34): recurrence state [64,128,128] + conv rings [3, 3*8192] per tensor q/k/v
    DSA layers (11): ckv [22,512] + indexer k [22,128] + gsc [22,128]
- Phase 2 (decode): one token at a time through all 45 layers, dispatching per layer
  (DSA at 3,7,...,43), appending to the DSA caches and updating the KDA state.

**The gate is free and exact**: the prefill IS the oracle for stepwise decode. Each token's
per-layer output under causal attention is identical whether computed batched or stepwise, so
`DECODE_CHAIN_MATCH` compares the decode walk's per-layer, per-token streams against the
prefill's own arrays element-wise. `glm_prefill.vyb` must dump per-token per-layer streams
(currently it dumps whole-sequence digests) — one added `dumps()` per layer, or a raw f64 dump
the verifier reads. Tolerance: fp64 noise (~1e-11), same as the single-layer gates.

**Weight resolution**: the drift trap (§6) forbids resolving all ~12k names up front. Resolve
each layer's ~26 main tensors once at start (1200 names, one pass), and resolve MoE experts
LAZILY per used expert inside the step (9 names, ~20 ms x 8 experts x 42 MoE layers ~ 7 s/step —
acceptable for a gate; hoisting the per-layer expert resolve to once-per-layer is the later
speed pass).

**Scale check** for the teacher-forced gate (22 decode steps): ~8 experts x ~83 ms deq x 42
MoE layers ~ 29 s/step, so ~11 min for the full 22-step walk on the 3090. The single-layer
drivers ran ~30 s including loads, so the per-step overhead is dominated by EXL3 dequant — the
f32 deq/hadamard lever (§9 item 3) is what makes generation-time speed real.

**MTP head after this**: the checkpoint layout is now pinned from the shards (index query,
`model.language_model.layers.45.*`, 3481 tensors): layer 45 is a FULL DSA-style decoder layer
— q_a/q_a_ln/q_b/kv_a/kv_a_ln/kv_b/o_proj plus the complete DSA indexer (wq_b, wk, k_norm
w+b, weights_proj, kpool ape/gate) — plus MoE (mlp.gate + bias, shared_experts, 288 EXL3
experts) and the MTP-specific `eh_proj.weight`, `enorm.weight`, `hnorm.weight`. Two things the
old §2 note got wrong: it does NOT lack attention, and it has NO hyper-connections (no
hc_attn_fn/hc_ffn_fn/base/scale) — so the hc mix/apply sites are skipped and input_layernorm
consumes the stream directly. `...layers.45.shared_head.norm.weight` (the model's final norm)
is also stored under this prefix. MTP decode shape: enorm(enorm'd hidden) + hnorm(embed of the
drafted token) -> eh_proj (2H -> H) -> the layer -> draft logits; pin the exact composition
against the checkpoint's own MTP usage (the config's num_nextn_predict_layers) before wiring.

**MTP semantics — PINNED from the shipped implementation** (exllamav3
`architecture/glm5_next_mtp.py` + `modules/arch_specific/qwen3_5_mtp.py` + `modules/rmsnorm.py`;
config: num_nextn_predict_layers=1, index_share_for_mtp_iteration=true, tie_word_embeddings=false,
vocab 154880, eh_proj BF16 [4096, 8192] = [H, 2H] out-in, hnorm/enorm BF16 [4096]):

1. Trunk state: layer-44 streams2 [4,H] -> MEAN over the 4 streams -> trunk final norm
   (`model.language_model.norm.weight`, exists, eps 1e-5) -> hnorm (RMSNorm with
   constant_bias=1.0, i.e. scale = x_norm * (w + 1.0) — pinned from modules/rmsnorm.py:84).
2. e = enorm(embed(next_token)) with the same (w + 1.0) scale.
3. concat [h, e] (2H) -> eh_proj (2H -> H) -> MTP input.
4. Layer 45 as a PLAIN residual block, no hc: input_layernorm -> DSA attention (MLA + indexer
   mode "full", NO rope, select_hq_bits=2, its OWN kv cache built by running the MTP layer over
   the prompt — the trunk prefill must push every token through steps 1-4 to prime it) ->
   residual -> post_attention_layernorm -> MoE -> residual. The shipped impl lets layer 45's
   own indexer score instead of reusing the trunk's top-k (index_share_for_mtp_iteration), and
   notes the two agree below index_topk context.
5. shared_head.norm (plain weighted RMSNorm, constant_bias 0) -> lm_head (untied, borrowed from
   the trunk) -> argmax = the draft token. The shipped impl computes the norms in fp16 — the
   exllamav3 draft is only a SEMANTIC reference (top-1 agreement); the numeric gate is our own
   fp64 oracle vs our fp64 driver, element-wise tight like the other gates.

Gate design: extend the chain generator so phase 1 (batched prefill) also runs steps 1-4 per
token and layer 45 batched (dumping the MTP hidden per token), and phase 2 runs the MTP layer
per decode step appending to its cache; the driver's final draft logits are compared against
`native/glm53/gold_mtp.py`, a numpy fp64 oracle that reads the chain's dumped layer-44 hidden
(chain<N> dumps, already full precision), applies steps 1-5 with real weights, and emits
logits + top-1. `DECODE_MTP_MATCH` = element-wise logits/hidden agreement with the oracle;
top-1 agreement with exllamav3 is the semantic smoke check.

**P12 — endpoint on the 3090**: status 2026-11-13:
- **GLM_TOKENIZER_ROUNDTRIP: OK** — `native/tokenizer/tokenizer.vyb` now reads the GLM
  artifacts via `VYBFORGE_TOKDIR` (extracted to `artifacts/glm53-tokenizer/` by
  `native/tokenizer/extract_glm_tokenizer.py`: vocab 154,820 / merges 321,649 / 36 added
  tokens). Two GLM-vs-Qwen differences are handled: the pre-tokenizer regex is
  `\p{N}{1,3}` (env `VYBFORGE_DIGMAX=3`; Qwen is `\p{N}`) and `model.ignore_merges=true`
  (env `VYBFORGE_TOKIGNORE=1`: a whole pre-token found in the vocab is emitted directly,
  no merges). Gate: `make tokglm` / `verify_glm_tokenizer.py` — 24/24 ASCII prompts
  exact vs the `tokenizers` reference, encode->decode round trip 27/27; the two
  non-ASCII probe prompts (CJK, emoji ZWJS) diverge exactly as documented for Qwen
  (no Unicode \p{L}/\p{N} in the Vyb segmenter). Defaults unchanged (Qwen gate still OK).
- **Generation loop**: `VYBFORGE_GEN` in the chain driver (edit
  `_chain_main_snippet.txt` + regenerate). CAPT raised 22->38 (cache rows per DSA slot:
  20 prefill + 2 teacher-forced + up to GENMAX=16 generated). After each decode step:
  mean-collapse of the layer-44 streams -> `model.language_model.norm` -> chunked
  lm_head argmax = the next token (fed back for the following step; greedy-via-target);
  the MTP draft (layer 45 on the same trunk state + current token -> shared_head.norm ->
  lm_head) is computed alongside and logged, no acceptance logic. Per-step full-logits
  digests (`gtrunk=`/`gdraft=`) + `GENTRUNK`/`GENDRAFT` lines go to the chain dump; the
  walked ids land in `VYBFORGE_GEN_OUT` as `PROMPT <csv>` / `GEN <csv>` (the GEN line is
  the full greedy continuation: nsteps+gen tokens). Gate: `verify_glm_gen.py` (run A vs
  run B determinism + draft agreement rate).
- **Server**: `native/host/glm_chat_server.vyb`, localhost:8091 (8090 is taken on
  godzilla), single-client (sequential accept), chat_server.vyb pattern: encode -> exec
  the chain driver with `VYBFORGE_L0_IDS` + `VYBFORGE_GEN` -> parse `GEN` -> decode ->
  reply. MINUTES per request (full prefill re-run: the end-to-end curl below took ~55 min),
  documented in its header. The prompt length is dynamic: the chain driver now takes any
  prompt of 3..CAPT-2 ids (prefill R = len-2; the gated 22-token prompt gives the original
  R = 20 exactly — per-step token/cache indices use a separate `rid`, since phase 2 sets
  the batched-row global R back to 1).

**P12 gate results (2026-11-13, 3090, hyperqwen stopped for the runs, restored after):**
- `GLM_TOKENIZER_ROUNDTRIP: OK` — 24/24 ASCII prompts exact vs `tokenizers`, round trip
  27/27; CJK/emoji probes documented as divergent (no Unicode classes in the Vyb regex).
- `GLM_GEN_GATE: OK` (`verify_glm_gen.py decchain_genA.txt decchain_genB.txt`) — the
  default 22-token prompt ("The capital of France is Paris, and … divided for decades."),
  VYBFORGE_GEN=4: greedy continuation ids 1283,576,6722,315,9621,374
  (" after The capital of France is" — greedy repetition, expected for a base model with
  no chat template); run A == run B exactly over all 6 steps (ids AND full 154880-wide
  logits digests); MTP draft top-1 agreed with the trunk argmax on 5/6 steps
  (disagreement at t=4: trunk 9621 vs draft 279, logit gap ~0.4).
- `DECODE_CHAIN_MATCH: OK` re-stamped on a VYBFORGE_GEN run's dump (worst 0.000e+00) —
  generation mode preserves the gated chain bit-exactly.
- `DECODE_MTP_MATCH: OK` re-stamped after a default (gen=0) run: `gold_mtp.py` regenerates
  from the chain's own dumps (nsteps now 2, top1 576), worst elementwise 3.06e-03
  (tol 1e-2); the gen-mode draft agreed (576 at t=1 in run A too).
- **End-to-end**: server up on :8091, `curl 'http://localhost:8091/chat?q=The%20capital%20of%20France%20is%20Paris%2C%20and%20the%20largest%20city%20in%20Germany%20is%20Berlin'`
  (15-token prompt) → 55 min → continuation ids 15147,304,9851,30 → "_{ in Germany?",
  draft agreement 2/2 on that request. Artifacts: `native/out/chat_reply.txt`,
  `glm_chat_gen_ids.txt{,.log}`, `glm_chat_chain.txt`.
- Costs measured: full run (prefill 20 + MTP priming 20 rows + 6 decode steps) ≈ 75 min;
  per decode step ≈ 8-10 min; a server request (15-token prompt, GEN=2) ≈ 55 min.
- Make targets: `tokglm`, `decgen` (default-prompt gen run + verify), `glmchat`.
- Next: speculative acceptance (draft-then-verify — the draft is already computed per
  step), f32 deq/hadamard (the minutes-per-step lever), resident weights in the server
  process to kill the per-request prefill.

**TP=2 / endpoint (owner decision)**: TP=2 applies ONLY to the GLM-5.3-Flash model and can
ONLY run on the dual Sparks — godzilla's single 3090 has no second device, so TP=2 is
unreachable there by construction. Therefore it must be RUNTIME-configurable, never compiled
in: the driver reads `VYBFORGE_TP` (0/1 = single card, the default and the only mode that
exists on the 3090; 2 = layer-sharded across lefty+righty) plus a layer->rank map, with the
split purely at the driver level (no kernel or layer-code changes; the per-layer structure the
chained decode already has is what makes the shard a loop-boundary change). The Qwen/ridge
path stays single-card regardless. The endpoint, by contrast, runs fine on the 3090 at TP=1 —
chain + MTP + tokenizer behind a localhost-only OpenAI-compatible listener, same shape as the
existing :8888 Vyb-native server. Still blocked for any Spark run: weight access (§8 — the
checkpoint is root-owned on lefty, /etc/exports empty).

**Allocator regression bisected + pooled (2026-11-14)**: the 914 s -> 1276 s prefill
regression was bisected to Vyb `7ff7b9e0` ("fix(codegen): free a discarded Vec<scalar>?
call result") — the leak fix made every hot-loop free a real libc free, and multi-MB
buffer frees route through mmap/munmap (deq 6.8 -> 13.8 s/layer, resolve 2.3 -> 6.2
s/layer; `fd0bf4ee` and `c3e1d76b` both profile at the 914 s baseline). A large-block
pool (`__vyb_pool_alloc`/`__vyb_pool_free`, side-table ownership, double-free
absorption) was implemented in the Vyb repo, branch `fix/pool-alloc-regression`
(d209b613, pushed): prefill4 deq 13753 -> 7304 ms / resolve 1796 -> 1134 ms (baseline
7088/1056), 45-layer prefill stable, suite 1358/1358 + ctest 4/4. NOT yet merged to
main; the build-release tree builds it, `build/vyb` is untouched. TWO open findings
from the work: (1) ~~the generated code has a latent double-free/use-after-free~~
DISPROVEN — the depth crashes were the pool's own class-exhaustion bug (bucket walk
overran to a 16-GiB class; clamped), and four consecutive 45-layer runs with the
reclaim enabled complete clean with zero absorbed double frees; the real fix is in
the pool, already on the branch (00e9ec26 adds a VYB_NO_DISCARD_FREE diagnostic knob
and a DOUBLE FREE tripwire);
(2) a depth-dependent slowdown remains even pooled (deq/resolve inflate with layer
index; 45-layer wall 1249-1274 s vs the 914 s leaky baseline) — partly cold shard
reads on the first pass, partly adjacent to the known String-registry resolve drift;
its root cause is its own investigation. THE TRANSFER STRAND'S SITE — first
hypothesis DISPROVEN: the gate diagnostic (RECLAIM-vec-elem, a83e69a6) proves
reclaimOwnedStructAt's Vec<struct> branch DOES emit the per-element owned-
field reclaim for Vec<JNode> (known=1 owned=1) — that site is NOT the strand.
The strand is elsewhere: the remaining +1/element/resolve retain still
unidentified. THE SITE-TAGGED TRACE IS BUILT (a83e69a6+ on the branch): 12
emitStrSiteMarker call sites in the codegen (1=decl-init 2=param 3=struct-
field 4=assign 5=expr-payload 6=deepcopy 7=? 8=return-transfer 9=match-arm
10=vec-push 11=vec-growth 12=vec-copy) + __vyb_str_site in the runtime +
the JIT symbol wired. PARSE-STAGE RESULT: net +450K refs/iter; the candidate
retain sites: 8 return-transfer (600K retains), 9 match-arm (300K), 10
vec-push (300K), 3 struct-field (300K); the aggregate cannot disambiguate
which release is missing — NEXT: the per-pointer lifecycle attribution (the
strand = the pointer whose RET count exceeds REL by the strand) to name the
exact site. NOTE: the full stage SEGFAULTS with the markers present (open;
the parse stage is clean). RESOLVED + REGRESSION (0bcb3af5): the strand =
site 8, the return-transfer's borrow-retain (every stranded pointer carried
exactly one unmatched site-8 retain) — the borrow's +1 assumed the scope
cleanup releases the returned binding, but the return-path cleanup
keepAlives the moved binding, so the +1 stranded; the fix skips the retain
for bare moved identifiers. RESULT: the microbench full stage live=6 FLAT
over 3 iterations (was 5,942->11,883->17,824) — THE STRAND IS ELIMINATED.
BUT the 45-layer glm_prefill SEGFAULTS early on the build (one str_stats
line, live=10, then crash): the moved-identifier return needs a guard
refinement (some flow still under-counts a ref). THE CRASH STRING IDENTIFIED (the RETIRE content print): the raw
model.safetensors.index.json text ('{\n "metadata": {\n "total_size": 1756...')
— the site-8 move frees it, then a stale pointer's byte-compare loop reads it
(the faulting instruction: movzbl (%rax,%rcx,1) at the JIT address; frame #1
NULL). This is the registry slot-reuse/stale-read class — the same one as the
documented runner abort (Rick's design territory). The strand fix itself is
CORRECT (the microbench live=6 flat). NEXT: either (a) the registry's
defense-in-depth (the slot generation/ticket so a stale release/pointer
cannot kill the new string), or (b) the codegen's stale-read guard (the
return-move set must exclude bindings still read after the return's CFG
position — currently impossible statically; favors (a)). Rick chose (a) — INSTALLED:
the retired-buffer quarantine (vyb_strq_ring, 4096 entries, retired registry
buffers held mapped; the eviction frees the oldest) + the pool's
foreign-branch absorb (a foreign free of a quarantined pointer returns
without freeing). RESULT: the prefill's crash moved from the site-8 segfault
to a resolve-enter tcache double free with ZERO QUAR-ABSORB hits — the
double-freed block is freed by TWO RAW POOL-FOREIGN FREES (both from the
JIT'd code, gdb bt #9 = the JIT address, no registry involvement): the
site-8 fix's ref accounting exposed a pre-existing double-free (a buffer
whose ref was 2 under the borrow-retain is now 1, freed early, then freed
again by the second site). NEXT: instrument __vyb_pool_free's foreign
branch (print the pointer + the caller marker per free) and diff the two
frees of the aborting block to name both codegen sites, then fix whichever
site double-frees. DONE — THE FINDING (ff3.log): the FREE-F instrumentation
works (the earlier install silently no-oped on an indent mismatch); the
aborting block is freed TWICE BY THE SAME JIT'D CALL SITE (0x...9051e x2,
no intervening alloc), and the site sits in a function whose adjacent frees
are ~21B apart — the signature of reclaimOwnedStructAt's per-field buffer
frees; the aliasing source: a MOVED PAYLOAD whose fields share one buffer
pointer (the extraction/move aliases two fields, the reclaim frees both).
NEXT: (1) name the function: gdb "info symbol" on the JIT'd address at the
abort, or map the offsets via the .ll's function order; (2) DONE + RESOLVED + NEW STATE: the extraction copy (copyOwnedValueForBinding,
#345's rule) applied at the optional arm; ALL free paths now route through
the quarantine (the foreign branch, the over-cap path). THE PREFILL'S
RESOLVE CRASH IS FIXED (it runs past resolve-enter, live ~18.8K stable).
REMAINING: (a) PF_FAIL attention layer 1 (the digest mismatch, before the
LAYER0 gate prints); (b) the pool's retained counter reads 3.65e17 (garbage).
BOTH are consistent with ONE BUFFER OVERFLOW introduced by this session's
ref changes — the prime suspect: copyOwnedValueForBinding's sizing at the
extraction (the copy's buffers under-sized). NEXT: audit
copyOwnedValueForBinding's per-field sizing (the Vec buffers' byte counts,
the String copies' len+1), or run the prefill's pre-CUDA stages under ASan. SHARPENED (the q4 log):
the attention failure is DOWNSTREAM — the real failure is **L0_FAIL resolve
21** (the layer-0 resolve digest is 21 vs the pinned 360448 — the copied ix
is nearly EMPTY), then "attn len 0 layer 1". The deep copy's sizing LOOKS
right (generateVecDeepCopy: alloc = cap*elemSize, copy = size elements, the
cap-preserving fix already in its comment) — so the wrongness is the
copy's CONTENT or the SIZE fields: instrument generateVecDeepCopy/
generateStructDeepCopy to print (the src size/cap, the dst size/cap, the
digest of the copied bytes) at the extraction, run the driver's L0 gate,
and diff. Suspicion: the JNode/struct-element per-element deep copy inside
generateVecDeepCopy (line ~1673) returns a value the copy loop stores
wrongly, or the copy's {ptr,size,cap} triple misorders fields. PROBED
(__vyb_vec_copy_probe, committed): the SHAPE IS CORRECT — 22,805 copies,
sane sizes (the doc's 8,902-elem JNode array at 56B/elem, cap-preserving),
no size/cap misorder. THE WRONGNESS IS AT THE CONTENT LEVEL. NEXT: extend
the probe to a verify (memcmp src/dst after the copy loop — a
__vyb_vec_copy_verify(src,dst,size,elemsz) printing the first mismatching
byte offset) to localize which field's copy corrupts; then read the
per-element copy loop (~1673) against the mismatch. DONE: 22,803 copies
verified, ZERO mismatches — the extraction deep copy is BYTE-PERFECT and
exonerated. The L0_FAIL resolve 21 cause is among the remaining session
changes: (i) the site-8 return-transfer fix, (ii) the quarantine free-
routing (the foreign + the over-cap paths now hold blocks instead of
freeing — a freed-then-held block's address is NOT reusable, which changes
the heap layout AND may break code that observed the reuse), (iii) the
extraction copy (exonerated on contents). BISECT PLAN: (1) build with the
site-8 fix alone (revert the extraction copy + the quarantine routing
temporarily), run the L0 gate; (2) if the L0 passes, add the quarantine
routing and re-test; (3) the first failing step is the culprit. The L0
PASSED on the pre-session baseline (the e2bab515-era build, the 1308 s run
with the borrow-retain). BISECT VERDICT: **the site-8 fix is the culprit** —
with the site-8 fix + the quarantine routing (no extraction copy) the L0
still fails resolve 21; with the site-8 fix alone the tcache abort returns
(the routing was masking the latent double free). THE COMPLETE MECHANISM:
the return's surgical unwind pops the scopes WITHOUT releasing the
non-temp bindings, so exitToFunctionBaseline finds them popped empty — the
borrow-retain at site 8 (+1 for the caller) is thus never matched by the
returned identifier's release: pre-site-8 the +1 strands (the drift);
post-site-8 the ref drops to 0 one release early (the resolve reads
freed data -> the digest 21). THE CORRECT DESIGN: a full unwind of the
popped scopes under the needsCleanup discipline (the temps + the locals +
the returned identifiers all released; the borrowed reads excluded via
keepAlive) — the blanket unwind failed before because it did not respect
that discipline. NEXT SESSION: implement the needsCleanup-disciplined full
unwind in visit(ReturnStatement), verify the microbench (live flat AND the
L0 gate green), then the 45-layer acceptance. DONE: the full unwind + THE
PAIRED RETAIN+RELEASE (the borrow-retain restored; the unwind releases the
moved identifier: 2 -> 1 for the caller -> 0 at the caller's teardown):
**L0_FAIL resolve 21 IS GONE — the resolve gate passes** (the failure moved
downstream: PF_FAIL attention layer 0). REMAINING: (a) the layer-0
attention's failure — ROOT VISIBLE: the attention's input fraw is ALL
ZEROS (the census: fraw n=180224 sum=0 abs=0 max=0), so log_decay(0)
produced the garbage (gdec max=2.7e12) and delta zeroed -> attn len 0.
The fraw zeros = A STALE-BUFFER READ: the embedding's result buffer was
freed (a raw free -> quarantined, held mapped) before the read, so the
stale pointer now returns the stale page's zeros instead of segfaulting —
the quarantine converted the crash into silent garbage. NEXT: with

**ROOT CAUSE FOUND (the s intermediates): THE ARITHMETIC IS CORRECT.**
The sum runs 0 -> -0.0003 -> 0.0126 -> 0.0599 -> 0.398 -> 0.429 -> -1.247
-> **-3.35685 at i=90111** — sane and smooth — but the census prints
-3692488466944. THE FLOAT to_string FORMATTING IS THE BUG: the mantissa
is right but the decimal exponent is shifted by ~13 for values ~1e0
(the small values like -0.000312805 format correctly; -3.36 does not).
The whole memory-corruption theory for the census is DEAD — the x data,
the clone, the reads, and the accumulation are all correct. THE FIX: the
f64->string dtoa in the runtime (the decimal-exponent computation for
the ~1e0 range). CORRECTION: the runtime's __vyb_float_to_string is
snprintf("%g") — correct. So the digest's s is CLOBBERED between the
loop's end (-3.35685, the last ONSET) and the return's string-build (the
to_string receives -3.7e12). The ratios differ per number (not a constant
formatting shift), so these are genuinely different values in the s/a
locals at build time. NEXT: probe the s's value between the two loops
and just before the return's to_string (the ONSET-style prints) to catch
the clobbering write. **REVERSED — THE CENSUS WAS NEVER GARBAGE:** the
census line is di(hv).to_string() where di(v) = (v * SCALE40) as Int —
it scales by 1099511627776 BY DESIGN. The "garbage" -3692488466944 is
exactly -3.35685 * SCALE40. The digest's arithmetic, the x data, the
clone, the reads: ALL correct; the census: correct by design. THE REAL
DISCREPANCY: the scaled digest is **21 vs the pinned 360448** — a factor

x's values' scale — the deq_bf16's z param (the driver passes z=0 for
the embedding deq; the other calls pass strides/offsets) or the read's
region. DONE: the z param EXONERATED (native/kernels/bf16.vyb: z>0
transposes, z=0 is the direct non-transposed write — correct for the
embedding deq) and the deq itself is a plain bf16 conversion. THE
REMAINING SUSPECT: the read's REGION — the raw bytes dequantize to ~1e-2
but the file's embeddings should be ~6e-6, so the read is hitting the

the data_offsets in the index are relative to the data segment (after

read_at uses it as an absolute file offset, the read lands ~1600 rows
early/late. NEXT: verify whether gx_resolve's off already includes the

clone: correct); (2) the digest's function entry: print v.len() as the
first statement — if it prints 180224, the size field was already

**2026-11-15 (evening) — the x-scale trail was a PHANTOM; the real bug
found and half-fixed.** The x digest (x n=90112 sum=-3692488466944 ...)
is byte-identical in EVERY log including the Oct 30 PASSING 45-layer
run (prefill45_bulk.log) — the embeddings were always correct and that
digest IS the historical pin. "L0_FAIL resolve 21" is a resolve COUNT
(nf=21 of want.len()=24 in kda_attn_site), not a digest value; the
"1600x scale" theory is dead. Two real bugs, in order:

1. **FIXED (Vyb 5520f9de, branch fix/return-path-cleanup-v2)**: a
   call-result STRUCT assigned to a binding was stored shallowly — the
   callee's cleanup reclaims the returned struct's owned field buffers,
   so `g<GxLoc> = gx_resolve(...)` aliased freed storage (G9PROBE:
   found=25, off=garbage, path=binary junk, from field index 9 on).
   Fixed by adding the CallExpression arm to the struct deep-copy gates
   at cgen_decl.cpp:376 and cgen_expr.cpp:1259 (mirrors 50e1f2c6's Vec
   arm). With it: **the L0 gate's layer 0 passes fully** (attn len
   360448, all digests sane).
2. **REMAINING → RESOLVED (Vyb 4ae941da, same evening)**: the layer-1 stale
   want-name strings were the stale-RELEASE kill (a late release matched the
   re-registered slot at a recycled address and retired the NEW string). Fix:
   __vyb_string_release absorbs any release of a quarantined pointer (a mapped
   retired buffer cannot be a live string, so the match is stale BY
   DEFINITION — the identity check needs no generation token); the
   quarantine grew to 65536 entries with a held-byte budget (VYB_STRQ_MB,
   default 512) and an O(1) shadow hash set for the hot release path.
   **RESULT: the L0 gate is GREEN at NLAYERS=2 — both layers complete (attn
   len 360448, zero FAILs, exit 0; native/out/glm53/prefill_genfix.log).**
   Per-directory suite runs are green (base64 1/1, arrays 9/9). The full-run
   suite still decays after ~180 tests — the runner's own stale-READ bug
   (its limitPrefix string goes stale → malformed `timeout` args → every
   later child fails with "Try 'timeout --help'"); the runtime fix moved the
   runner's healthy span from 4 to ~184 tests. The runner bug lives in
   test/run_tests.vyb's own string handling — separate from the registry.

**FOLLOW-UP (same evening, after the L0 gate went green)**

3. **The suite's 9 real regressions — FIXED (Vyb 4dff29ec)**: e2bab515's
   return-path cleanup POPPED the compile-time scopeStack at every return;
   codegen continues after a return inside a match arm (the other arms, the
   join blocks), so sibling paths registered `__recv_struct_tmp` with no
   active scope and their cleanups never fired (btreemap's
   `bt.get(10) else "?"` returned garbage; archive/chain/http/https/closures
   red). The cleanup now walks the scopes WITHOUT popping and RESTORES
   needsCleanup so each exit path frees exactly once; the return path frees
   ONLY the match temps (the blanket all-vars form double-freed the closure
   escape tests). ALSO: run_tests.vyb's opt() returned the borrowed argv
   element whose return-transfer freed the caller's binding — the runner's
   full-run decay at ~180 tests (the malformed `timeout` args); opt() now
   returns a fresh buffer and takes argv as a const borrow.
   **RESULT: the suite is 1358/1358 GREEN** (first full pass since the
   ownership work began); the L0 gate re-verified green on the same build.

4. **THE 45-LAYER RUN IS BLOCKED by a ~30 GB/layer raw-heap leak**, bisected:
   the Oct 29 binary (build-merge-check) + the current driver completes
   NLAYERS=2 at 1.56 GB peak, exit 0; f624030c (extraction copy + all free
   paths routed through the quarantine) peaks at 6.5 GB for 2 layers, and
   the leak continues at HEAD (~1 GB/s of anonymous arena growth measured
   live: 17 GB → 78 GB in 70 s during layer 1-2; the kernel OOM-killed the
   45-layer run at 83.7 GB anon-rss — exit 137, twice). The string registry
   is SMALL during the growth (live ~43k), so it is NOT the strand — it is
   raw buffers never freed. The handoff's open suspect stands: a heap
   corruption from the extraction-copy change (the garbage
   retained_pool=3.65e17 counter is the tell; generateVecDeepCopy's shape
   was verified byte-perfect earlier, so the corruption is elsewhere in the
   free-routing/sizing). The 2-layer run COMPLETES green under a 100 G
   systemd scope; 45 layers cannot fit. NOTE: session-launched jobs are
   OOM-killed at the gateway's 4 GiB cgroup — heavy runs need
   `systemd-run --user --scope -p MemoryMax=80G`.
   NEXT: instrument __vyb_pool_alloc/free byte counters (or run the 2-layer
   gate under heaptrack/valgrind) to name the leaked allocation's call site;
   the bisect already bounds it to f624030c's two changes.

**LEAK ROOT-CAUSED (same night, LD_PRELOAD malloc counter — see
~/.hermes/cache/scratch/malcount.c)**: it is NOT a leak and NOT the
registry/pool/quarantine. The 2-layer run issues **4.9M allocations
totaling 373 GB (186 GB/layer, ~12 GB/s churn)** and frees them all
(freeGB == allocGB) — the 83 GB RSS is glibc main-arena fragmentation
from that churn (the arena cannot release interleaved free space; one
29 GB contiguous anon mapping observed). Neither pool_alloc/free (<5000
calls per 2 layers) nor quarantine pushes (<1000) carry the traffic —
the churn flows through RAW malloc, i.e. the runtime's Vec/String
operations and the by-value parameter deep copies the compiler's own
warnings flag every run: rms_rows 2.8 MB, delta_rule q/k/g 3x1.4 MB,
log_decay 1.4 MB, digest 2x1.4 MB, PER CALL. mallopt(M_MMAP_THRESHOLD=64K)
did NOT help (the deep copies are interleaved with long-lived small
allocations, so the arena fragments regardless). VYB_STRQ_OFF=1 (a
quarantine bypass switch now in the runtime) coincided with a 1.7 GB
2-layer run but the quarantine provably holds almost nothing — the A/B
was confounded; do not trust it.
   NEXT: convert the hot by-value Vec params in glm_prefill.vyb (the ones
the deep-copy warnings name: rms_rows, log_decay, delta_rule's q/k/g,
digest, kda_attn_site's streams) to `their<Vec<Float> const>` + view()
at the call sites — the churn should drop from 186 GB/layer to ~nothing
and the 45-layer run becomes possible on godzilla without a scope. The
45-layer attempt with VYB_STRQ_OFF=1 reached layer 3's MoE and failed
ALL_FAIL deq expert 287 (the stale-pointer class returning with the
defenses off) — keep the quarantine ON once the churn is fixed.

**THE CHURN FOUND AND FIXED (VybForge 3330519)**: the 186 GB/layer was
the driver's OWN dumps() — an O(n^2) repeated-concat dump (out = out +
di(v.get(i)).to_string()) over the 180224-element qnvals/chain dumps:
~360 GB of churn PER CALL, which fragmented the glibc arena to the 83 GB
OOM. dump_to() now streams the identical content in 32 KB chunks
straight to PREFILL_OUT (open_append + write_str per chunk). The hot
by-value Vec params (rms_rows, log_decay, delta_rule's q/k/v/g,
kda_attn_site's streams) are now const borrows. RESULTS: the 2-layer
gate runs in 12.6 s (was 2.5-6 min) at 2.7 GB peak (was 83.6 GB); the
45-layer run passes layers 0-2 and layer 3's attention with bounded
memory (4.6 GB peak) — host-side memory is SOLVED.
   NEXT (the new, bounded failure): the 45-layer run dies at the MoE
expert deq — `ALL_FAIL deq expert 22` (run3) and `expert 28` (the
VYB_STRQ_OFF run), i.e. exl3_deq returns 0 for a specific expert
index, deterministically, with host memory clean. The expert loop
frees dWg/dWu/dWd per iteration (cuMemFree_v2), so suspect device-side
accumulation or a device alloc/launch failure inside exl3_deq (the
DEQFAIL probes cover deq_bf16 only — instrument exl3_deq's allocs and
launch rcs the same way). Also verify on a clean GPU (a leftover vyb
held 17 GB after one run). The 2-layer gate does not reach the MoE
(dense L0-2) — the MoE gate needs NLAYERS>=4.
   INSTRUMENTED (EXL3FAIL probes in exl3_deq, glm_prefill.vyb): the
failure is `cuMemAlloc_v2(loc(fd), kn*8)` then `loc(od), kn*8` —
DEVICE-MEMORY EXHAUSTION in the MoE expert loop (~17 GB held on a
24 GB GPU; the failing expert index varies per run: 22/28/51 — it is
whichever expert hits the exhausted pool). The expert loop frees
dWg/dWu/dWd per iteration, so something else accumulates device
buffers across experts/iterations (mm_host's dA/scr? the deq staging?
an unfreed buffer in the expert path). NEXT: instrument the expert
loop's device high-water (print cudaMemGetInfo.free per expert) and
diff the alloc/free sets; the leak is bounded to one layer's expert
loop (~23 experts × leaked bytes = ~17 GB → ~700 KB-1 MB leaked per
expert iteration).
   REFINED (DEVMEM probe per expert + cuMemGetInfo_v2 extern added to
glm_prefill): expert 1's deq completes with only 2.57 GB device used,
then the NEXT expert's qd alloc (cuda1, ~29 MB trellis) fails — NOT
out-of-memory. So cuda1 is a non-memory CUDA error whose rc is
discarded by the `!= 0` check. NEXT: capture the rc at each EXL3FAIL
site (`rc<Int> = cuMemAlloc_v2(...) as Int` + print). DONE — **rc=700 =
CUDA_ERROR_LAUNCH_FAILED, a STICKY error**: an earlier expert-path
kernel faulted asynchronously (illegal access — the stale-buffer
family), poisoning the context; every later CUDA call then returns 700

**Resolve-depth drift ROOT-CAUSED (2026-11-14, evening)**: the drift is a string
leak, measured deterministically by `repro/str_leak_micro.vyb` (isolation in
seconds, not per 20-min run). `st_open` leaks ~5,930 registry strings per
1185-tensor header parse, and `gx_resolve_idx` re-opens the SAME shard per call
(~109 calls/layer) -> ~646k leaked strings/layer -> the registry table grows to
64M slots -> every string op turns cache-miss-heavy (resolve 2.3 -> 11 s/layer).
Root cause: json_parse returns JsonDoc by value and function-returned structs
never get return-transfer cleanup (the codegen's isOwnedStruct is 'resolved
lazily at cleanup / return-transfer time' and never resolves for returned
values) — the doc's ckey keys and nested JNode.sval strings all leak. A Vyb-
level json_free cannot fix it (strings reachable from Vyb code are retained
copies; the doc's own refs are only reachable via codegen cleanup). Fix target:
src/vre/llvm/cgen_ownership.cpp return-transfer cleanup for structs owning
Vec<String> / Vec<struct-with-String>. Expect the 45-layer wall to drop from
~1250 s toward ~950 s after the fix. SECOND finding parked with it: assigning a
module-level struct (an st_open memo attempt) crashed glibc — global struct
reassignment appears to release values whose strings are still shared; repro
recipe in the gx.vyb parked note.

**FOLLOW-UP (same evening)**: the optional-payload cleanup fix LANDED in Vyb
main (13293775, LSan-clean, suite 1358/1358) — real memory is now released —
but the registry LIVE-COUNT growth persists (+11,856/resolve iteration on the
fixed build, LSan clean). So the drift is NOT unreleased memory: registry
slots never RETIRE. Likely path: strings registered via the borrowed variant
(refs=0) released once — fetch_sub gives 0, the retire condition is old==1,
so the slot stays live forever while the buffer is freed elsewhere. Fix
target: runtime/vyb_runtime.c __vyb_string_release — retire the slot when
refs reach <= 0 (or make borrowed registrations not count as live). The
microbench verifies in seconds; the 45-layer wall is the acceptance test.

**RESOLUTION STATUS (next morning)**: two fixes landed and the drift's
remaining half is precisely bounded. (1) The JsonDoc fix (13293775, on main)
releases the doc's memory — correct, LSan-clean. (2) The surgical
return-path cleanup (fix/return-path-cleanup-v2 a7d1f9f1) frees the
match-scrutinee temps at return — suite 1358/1358, the two earlier failures
fixed by scoping the unwind to the temps only (the blanket unwind broke
borrowed my<Struct> returns and helper-chain strings; main() is exempt —
its serialization tail reads the returned storage). REMAINING: the strand
persists at +5,928/resolve with the temps freed — the remaining half is the
st_open -> caller StIndex transfer: each Vec<String> element gains +1
stranded ref per resolve (the return transfer's retain vs the local
teardown's release, off by one). The traced evidence: ~6.9 RETs vs ~5.6
RELs per string per resolve; the ghost refs grow +1/resolve. Next: audit
the struct-return transfer's element retain (cgen_expr_call / the return
path in cgen_stmt) against the caller's StIndex teardown release. The
traces (VYB_STR_TRACE=1) are on main's runtime now. ACCEPTANCE MEASURED:
the 45-layer wall is UNCHANGED (1308 s vs 1270-1276 pre-fix; resolve mean
6,705 vs 6,012) — the match-temp fix freed real memory but the wall impact
persists, confirming the StIndex-transfer strand is the dominant half. The
branch fix/return-path-cleanup-v2 — the return-path cleanup is now
COMMITTED (e2bab515: the match-scrutinee temps freed at return, keepAlive
excludes moved identifiers/select-arms/member bases, main() exempt, the
unwind stops at the function baseline). Suite 1356/1358: the my_struct
corruption FIXED; ONE failure remains — test_main_exit_code's runner output
is garbled (a returned string's storage freed early somewhere in the
runner's helper chain — the runner is test/run_tests.vyb, string-heavy;
the unwind's keepAlive covers its visible shapes). THE RUNNER TRACED
(runner_trace_abort.log in repro/): the runner ABORTS with a tcache double
free, and the trace shows the mechanism: a binding's CLEANUP fires multiple
times (the vyb binding cleaned 4x — the cleanup IR emitted at multiple block
exits), and a late release arrives after the address's re-registration —
__vyb_string_release matches the re-registered slot and kills the NEW string
at the same address -> the tcache double free. THE FIX REQUIRES the
registry's slot semantics under address reuse (the release must verify the
slot's generation/identity, or the cleanup must not fire multiple times) —
compiler-author territory (the registry is Rick's design). The WIP stays on
diag/str-registry-trace; main is clean and releasable. NOT merged to main yet —
merge after the last failure is fixed; the 45-layer acceptance (~1308 ->
~950 s) follows.

**DEEPEST LAYER (traced, next morning)**: the drift is not one leak — it is
PARTIAL CLEANUP COVERAGE for match-scrutinee temps. One 15.5 MB json_parse
executes ~450k token-level matches but emits only ~30 match-temp cleanups
(VYB_STR_TRACE now logs OPTREG <loc> at registration, OPTPAYLOAD at reclaim,
CLEANUP per temp). The un-covered matches strand their payload strings
(refs end 1-4; 59,281 of 59,300 generations never retire in the 2-iteration
micro). Also verified this session: gx_read_index is clean; the leak is
entirely inside json_parse/st_open; LSan is masked by the program-exit sweep.
The instrumentation for the coverage audit is all on
branch diag/str-registry-trace (OPTREG locs + OPTPAYLOAD + CLEANUP +
REG/REL/RET). The audit: per OPTREG site, count OPTPAYLOAD/CLEANUP firings
vs executions; the uncovered sites are the fix targets (likely the
match-inside-loop / match-as-tail-statement paths where the scope-exit
emission is skipped).

**MECHANISM v3 (final, next morning)**: coverage is actually COMPLETE for
every OPTREG site (per-site CLEANUP counts all >= 1) — the real defect is the
CLEANUP's TIMING: the doc's strings retire ONE FULL ITERATION LATE (trace:
iteration N's strings release after iteration N+1's REGs). The match-temp's
cleanup IR lands in a CFG block that executes a full loop iteration after the
binding's scope exit (match-end/loop-latch placement), so in a loop every
snapshot catches one full iteration of strings stranded, and in the 45-layer
driver the releases only happen at program exit (the table grows to 64M slots
mid-run; RSS stays bounded because the buffers are freed late, not never).
MINIMAL REPRO: VYB_LEAK_STAGE=small VYB_LEAK_ITERS=3 repro/str_leak_micro.vyb
-> live 14/20/26 (+6/iteration, deterministic, seconds). FIX: emit the
match-temp payload reclaim into the match's own end block (or the binding's
scope-exit block), not wherever cleanupVariable's insert point lands after
the loop back-edge. The registry itself is exonerated completely.

**DRIFT ROOT CAUSE v2 (traced, same evening)**: full-lifecycle tracing
(VYB_STR_TRACE=1 logs REG/RET/REL per registry op) shows the growth is
STRANDED RETAINS, not unretired slots of freed memory: each header string's
refcount gains exactly +1 per gx_resolve_idx call (REG 1 -> RET 2 -> REL 1,
then +1 per resolve forever), so ~5,928 refs strand per resolve and slots
never reach the retire threshold. The strand is in the st_open ->
caller StIndex transfer: the return path (or the caller's local StIndex
teardown) retains every Vec<String> element once per call without a matching
release. Repro: repro/str_leak_micro.vyb (shrunken: 10 iterations, 1 name) —
+5,928 registry live per iteration, each header string +1 ref/resolve. The
registry itself is correct (LSan-clean; strings legitimately alive mid-run —
the earlier t_struct3 "leak" was a main-scoped-binding misread). Fix target:
the compiler's struct-return transfer for Vec<String> fields (cgen_expr_call
return path / cgen_ownership release balance).

**Deployment capacity/bandwidth math (measured + derived, 2026-11)**: the engine is weight-
streaming — compute is nearly free, and a token's speed = aggregate memory bandwidth / bytes
touched per token. GLM-5.3-Flash stores 164 GB but touches only ~27 GB/token (8 of 288 experts
fire; attention + shared + dense always touched). The storage-vs-touched distinction is what
makes sparse MoE scale: a 1 TB 4-bit MoE warehouse stores 6x more but only multiplies the
TOUCHED bytes if the active set widens. Measured/derived per-token ceilings, best config each:

| touched/token | godzilla 3090 (24 GB VRAM + 128 GB host cache + local NVMe) | dual Sparks (242 GB pool @ 546 GB/s aggregate) | margin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 27 GB (GLM-5.3-Flash) | 0.64 tok/s | 18.2 tok/s | 28x |
| 60 GB | 0.31 tok/s | 9.1 tok/s | 29x |
| 120 GB | 0.14 tok/s | 4.5 tok/s | 32x |
| 230 GB | 0.04 tok/s | 2.4 tok/s | 60x |
| 500 GB | 0.014 tok/s | 0.005 tok/s | ~1x, both unusable |

The Sparks win any model whose touched set fits their 242 GB pool, because their fast tier
(LPDDR5X against the compute, 546 GB/s aggregate) is 10x wider than godzilla's (24 GB VRAM,
then host RAM at ~18 GB/s, then NVMe at ~6 GB/s). The topology rule: keep the touched-per-token
set inside the pool; the NVMe warehouse can grow almost without limit (only the touched slice
streams). A dense 1 TB 4-bit model is dead on both (all 1 TB touched/token -> 0.01 tok/s).
Checkpoint placement measured: apex NFS read 16.5-87 MB/s (the 914 s prefill was over half
apex-NFS stall); local NVMe copies on godzilla (/usr/export, nvme1n1, 765 GB free) and lefty
(/srv/models, 1.9 TB SSD, 1.3 TB free) remove it — 6.1 GB/s reads. lefty->righty CX7 (measured
1272 MB/s) serves TP=2's cross-rank tensors; a direct godzilla-lefty RJ45 caps at 1 GbE (lefty's
spare ports) and is not worth pulling.

---

## 10. Working agreements that keep this moving

- Gate every change: `L3_FULL_VYB_MATCH` (3 min), `L3_HC_VYB` (seconds) and `L3_TAIL_VYB`
  (4 min) catch most regressions; the full Vyb suite is 1357 tests, ~2.5 min, and
  `test/suite_count_check.vyb` fails if a documented suite count drifts.
- One oracle per claim. If a gate passes on synthetic data, it has not been shown to pass.
- Vyb's `main` ruleset blocks direct pushes: disable ruleset `22855500`
  (`gh api -X PUT repos/rickenator/Vyb/rulesets/22855500 --input off.json`), push, re-enable.
  `git add -A` has swept build directories into commits twice — `.gitignore` now covers
  `build-qt/` and `native/`; check `git show --stat` before pushing.
- Text-slice assembly of a big Vyb driver from another driver's functions is a trap: slice to
  a function's closing brace, not to the next function you remember, and expect a copied
  prelude to re-zero parameters and to arrive untyped.
- Reports want HOST + FULL path per artifact; apex NFS (`/mnt/apex/rick`) is the landing zone.

1. (CORRECTED) No host repro to extend — all pass. Go straight at the device side: run the gate 5x
   on the same binary to estimate the flake rate, then instrument the expert immediately before
   the OOM with a print of every cuMemAlloc_v2 SIZE argument (a garbage size — e.g. a corrupt
   `kn`/`tb`/`mb` — is the remaining hypothesis; rc=2 = OUT_OF_MEMORY with cuMemGetInfo showing
   free memory only if the requested size itself is absurd). RESULT: sizes are CONSTANT and sane
   across all 129 deq calls (tb=4194304, kn8=67108864, mb=4112, krows2=4096/8192) — the
   garbage-size hypothesis is DEAD. So the device genuinely fills: each expert's ~200 MB of deq
   staging is freed early in the run (experts 1-21 exactly neutral) and stops being freed later,
   or the driver retains the freed pool; the loop's own frees are all present in the source. NEXT
   remaining levers: (a) a cuMemGetInfo print INSIDE exl3_deq (after each of its frees) for the
   experts around the growth onset — one of the nine frees may be a no-op because the block was
   reallocated (double-free-then-reuse) or the free list's pointer differs from the alloc; (b)
   VYB_POOL_TRACE / a device-alloc counter in alloc_dev/cuMemFree_v2 pair to name which of the
   nine never balances; (c) run the gate under cuda-memcheck (compute-sanitizer) — DONE:
   /usr/local/cuda-12.8/bin/compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck gives ZERO invalid accesses and ZERO
   double frees; the ONLY errors are the three CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY from cuMemAlloc_v2. So the
   code's device accesses are clean and the device genuinely exhausts — which leaves the FREE
   CALLS as the suspect: cuMemFree_v2's rc is IGNORED at every call site (the `cuMemFree_v2(x)`
   statements discard the CInt). A free that returns an error (invalid/duplicate pointer) leaves
   the block allocated with no diagnostics. NEXT (first thing next session): capture and print the
   rc of every cuMemFree_v2 in exl3_deq and around the expert loop's dWg/dWu/dWd frees; the first
   non-zero free rc names the block whose free is a no-op. This is a ~10-line change and one 4-min
   gate run. RESULT: all six frees return 0 (success) up to the OOM at expert 117 — the frees are
   NOT the no-op. Combined with memcheck (clean accesses), sane sizes, and a memory-neutral early
   loop, the holder is OUTSIDE exl3_deq: the remaining suspect is the driver's own device-buffer
   helpers feeding the loop — `bulk_htod` / `bulk_dtoh` / `alloc_dev` in native/tensor/tensor.vyb
   (and the gather's per-expert upload). Audit those for alloc/free balance (bulk_dtoh allocates a
   device staging buffer per call; if it leaks for rows>1 the curve grows only for multi-token
   experts — matching the observed onset). That is the FIRST thing to check next session; the
   gate's own accounting is now fully exonerated.
   FINAL DATA (this session): (1) **the gate COMPLETED all 288 experts once** (MOE_ALL_OK, exit 0)
   with the PREALLOC probe in place - the gate is achievable, the failure is marginal/layout-driven;
   (2) the PREALLOC fd line shows cuMemGetInfo free dropping **exactly 67 MB per deq call**
   (23442882560 -> 23375773696 -> 23308664832), i.e. one kn*8 staging block per deq never returns
   to the pool, so 288 experts x ~200 MB drains the card to the OOM edge; (3) the loop's
   dWg/dWu/dWd frees all return 0 and the FDPTR addresses show the allocator RECYCLING freed blocks
   - so the drain is one specific 67 MB staging alloc per deq (candidates: od, td2 or fd) whose
   free path succeeds yet does not release (or which is allocated twice). NEXT: print
   cuMemGetInfo after EACH of the nine allocs and after EACH of the six frees inside exl3_deq for
   one expert - the alloc/free line pair that does not cancel names the block. Then the 45-layer
   run.
2. (SUPERSEDED — no host fix needed; keep `generateStructDeepCopy` unmodified.)
3. Verify: gate 5x → `MOE_ALL_OK` each time; then full suite → the 45-layer acceptance.

# SESSION 2026-11-16 (later) — THE MoE DEVICE LEAK WAS A GPU CO-TENANT. BLOCKER CLOSED.

## The answer, in one line
**There is no device leak in the MoE gate.** The card was not filling from our code — it was
filling from a **crash-looping vLLM container on the same 3090**
(`hyperqwen-abliterated-single-1`, image ghcr.io/syv-ai/hyperqwen, `restart=unless-stopped`,
**1054 restarts**), which re-attempts engine init every ~30-60 s and holds **15-22 GB of 24 GB**
while doing it. Every "nondeterministic" MoE OOM (expert 12 / 20 / 50 / 96 / 117 / 130, and the one
run that passed all 288) is that container's restart phase landing on our gate.

## How it was found (the instrument that finally named it)
1. `dmem()` in `native/host/glm_l3_moe_all.vyb` = a `cuMemGetInfo_v2` bracket around each deq.
   Printing device-free after every alloc/free site (9 allocs + 8 frees) showed the deq is
   **exactly balanced**; the only net change per deq is `-kn*8 = -67108864 B` = the returned fd
   block, which the caller frees (that was the "67 MB per deq never returns" from the last
   session — it is the RETURN VALUE, not a leak).
2. The remaining jump was between two probes with **no driver code in between**: -14.99 GB between
   expert 10's end and expert 12's start, -6.95 GB later. So an external process took the memory.
3. A per-process VRAM sampler (`nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps=pid,process_name,used_memory` once
   a second, run alongside the gate) named it:
   `VLLM::EngineCore` 14420 -> 15660 -> 22086 MiB appearing mid-run, then dying. The container's own
   log: `torch.OutOfMemoryError: CUDA out of memory. Tried to allocate 960.00 MiB ... Process
   3641187 has 21.41 GiB memory in use` -> `RuntimeError: Engine core initialization failed`.

## Why it never self-heals
Its first real crash was 11-02 05:49 (`AsyncLLM output_handler failed`). Since then every restart
attempt races the PREVIOUS EngineCore process, which still holds ~21 GB while draining, so the new
attempt OOMs at 45-50 MB free and dies -> restart. Two weeks of a 1-minute crash loop that quietly
sabotages every multi-minute GPU run on godzilla.

## Verified after `docker stop hyperqwen-abliterated-single-1`
- `make -C native glm3all` equivalent, **5 consecutive runs, all `MOE_ALL_OK experts=109`**,
  `L3_MOE_ALL_VYB: OK` (mlp max rel 1.07e-05) on the final one.
- Device use over a full run: **peak 1.69 GiB, final 1.69 GiB, flat** (was 23.5 GiB at expert 20
  before). Per-deq net: **327/327 = -67108864 B exactly, zero exceptions**
  (`DEQNET fd=-67108864 expect=-67108864` x327).
- Vyb suite: **1358/1358 PASS** (build-release/vyb).
- The real driver: `glm_prefill.vyb` reached **`layer 3 done`** — the first time the actual prefill
  got through layer 3's MoE — with its own DEVMEM probe flat at 2.58 GiB across all 288 experts.

## Cost of the acceptance run (new, measured)
One MoE layer = **~14 min** (288 experts x 3 deqs x ~0.9 s), so the full 45-layer prefill
(43 MoE layers) is a **~10-hour job**. The old `timeout 3000` command kills it at layer ~6.
Launcher for the long variant: `tools/run_prefill45.sh` (timeout 43200, local ext4 checkpoint
`/usr/export/...`, detached via `setsid nohup`), log `native/out/prefill45.log`, ends
`PREFILL_OK layers=45`.
`tools/run_glm3all_vram.sh` = the gate + a per-process VRAM sampler; keep it, it is what proved
co-tenancy. **Any device-memory diagnosis on godzilla must now check `nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps`
before believing a curve.**

## Still open
1. The ~10 h 45-layer acceptance run (launch decisions belong to Rick — it owns the 3090).
2. `hyperqwen-abliterated-single-1` is **stopped** (was crash-looping since 11-02). Restarting it on a
   clean card should work; if it OOMs again at engine init it needs its own fix, not a GPU quarantine.
3. Merge `fix/return-path-cleanup-v2` -> Vyb main (unchanged from the previous session).
