### 45-layer acceptance run — timeout corrected, relaunched 11:05
Measured rate on the live run: **3.9 s/expert -> ~18.6 min per MoE layer**, i.e. 43 MoE layers is
**~13.5 h**, which the 43200 s cap would have cut at ~layer 40. `tools/run_prefill45.sh` now uses
`timeout 86400` (24 h); the first attempt's log is kept as
`native/out/prefill45_attempt1_12h_cap.log` (it reached layer 4). Relaunched 11:05, pid 3720445,
live log `native/out/prefill45.log`, ETA ~00:35 next day (PREFILL_OK layers=45).

### PERF (2026-11-16, after Rick's "13.5 h? what is the slowdown?"): MoE resolve was 85-99% of the run
Measured with new timers (`DEQT` per deq in the gate, `MMT` per mm_host, `EXPT` per expert;
`T_MOE` already existed in the prefill):
```
gate, 109 experts:  resolve 132.3 s (84.8%) | deq3 10.5 s (6.7%) | mm 12.9 s (8.2%)
prefill layer 3:    T_MOE resolve=1145661 ms  deq=9804 ms  mm=503 ms
inside one resolve:   stopen=757 ms   scan=0 ms   stfind=1 ms
```
So the 13.5 h was **st_open()**: the shard's safetensors header was re-parsed once per expert
(288 x 0.757 s = 3.6 min/layer; the prefill paid 3 shard opens per expert). The 15.5 MB index scan
was not the cost once narrowed to the layer's ~400 KB expert block.

Fix (gx.vyb + both MoE drivers): `gx_expert_window` (one scan -> the layer's expert block),
`gx_resolve_ix` (resolve against a caller-owned, already-open StIndex — no globals, which crash),
`gx_resolve_maybe` (cached header + fallback for the ~2-in-288 experts that straddle two shards).
Both drivers now keep ONE open header and re-open only on a shard change (3 per layer).

Result: gate 156 s -> **27.6 s** (`L3_MOE_ALL_VYB: OK`, identical digests); prefill NLAYERS=4 wall
**85 s**, T_MOE resolve 1145661 -> **12542 ms (91x)**, `PREFILL_OK layers=4`. Full 45-layer run
relaunched 11:33: now ~3-4 min per MoE layer (~2-2.8 h) instead of ~18.6 min (13.5 h).
Remaining per-MoE-layer split: deq 8.5-9.2 s (exl3hgen 8.7 + had1 8.9 ms/deq are the two hottest
kernels), mm 0.4 s, and ~2-3 min of NON-MoE layer work (attention/GDN/dense streaming/digests) —
that is the next profiling target, not the MoE loop.

### Phase split, NLAYERS=8 with T_LAYER/T_TAIL (2026-11-16, PREFILL_OK layers=8 in 19 min)
```
n=0 dense  attn=1.5s  ffn=0.4s
n=1 dense  attn=4.1s  ffn=2.5s
n=2 dense  attn=12.3s ffn=3.4s
n=3 DSA    attn=20.1s ffn=92.8s   total=113s   (T_MOE resolve=30.3s deq=21.9s mm=0.5s;
                                              T_TAIL router+MoE+shared+hc_apply=74.8s)
n=4 KDA    attn=122s  ffn=134s    total=256s   (resolve=68.0s deq=17.7s)
n=5 KDA    attn=86.5s ffn=124s    total=211s   (resolve=63.8s)
n=6 KDA    attn=85.0s ffn=139s    total=224s
n=7 DSA    attn=23.7s ffn=136s    total=160s
```
Per-layer cost model: KDA layers ~3.5-4.3 min (34 of them), DSA ~1.9-2.7 min (11), dense ~seconds
-> the 2h11m full run is consistent. Ranked targets:
1. **KDA attention site: 85-122 s/layer x 34 layers = ~40% of the whole run.** Untouched so far.
2. **MoE resolve 30-68 s/layer x 43**: still 10 window scans (400 KB each) per expert + up to 3
   st_open per layer; a per-layer name->(shard,off,len) table built once would cut it to ~5 s.
3. deq 17-22 s/layer: exl3hgen (8.7 ms) + exl3had1 (8.9 ms) per deq are the hot kernels.
4. shared expert + hc_apply ~22 s/layer inside T_TAIL.

### PERF session 2 (2026-11-16 afternoon) — KDA attn profiled: it was resolve too; st_open is the last hog
Instrumented kda_attn_site (T_KDA deq/mix/core/tail/pre/entry): the GDN core is 0.6 s, the weight
deqs 0.3 s, the hc-mix gemms ~31 s — the "slow KDA attention" was ~90 s of resolve + header parsing
in front of the math. Chain of findings, each measured:
1. `gx_read_index` (15.5 MB read) per site per layer: 2.2 s (layer 2) -> 91 s (layer 4) as the heap
   grows. Fixed: read once into a main() local at startup.
2. Any method call on a GLOBAL big String copies the receiver under heap pressure (4.7 s per
   index_of scan). Fixed: the index is only ever touched through main's local + per-layer windows.
3. Vyb gotcha: a concat needle with a TRAILING escaped quote (`"...0.\"`) breaks index_of (length/
   content mismatch -> -1); mid-string escapes are fine. Cost half a session.
4. Layer windows: per-layer ~760 KB window (g_lpos precomputed at startup on the clean heap),
   passed to all five sites; every resolve = window scan (~0.3 ms) + memoized header + st_find.
Verified NLAYERS=4: PREFILL_OK rc=0; layer 3 attn 18.8->11.8 s, layer total 92->71 s.
REMAINING HOG (measured, T_RSLV): **st_open = ~11 s per shard-header parse under the driver's
~4.6 GB heap** (757 ms small-heap), and a KDA layer's tensors span ~8-10 shards -> ~90 s/layer of
parsing, ~100 min of the full run. The pf_open memo only helps when the same shard recurs.
NEXT (in order):
1. Make st_open cheap under heap pressure: profile json_parse vs the StIndex walk at 4.6 GB
   (suspect allocation churn). Or parse all ~83 shard headers ONCE at startup while the heap is
   small (~83 x 0.76 s) and keep the parsed tables alive for the run.
2. Then the fused-dequant gemm (f64 -> f32-in-register) — the structural win, ~1 TB/run of f64
   traffic removed; needs oracle tolerance re-baselining.
3. hc-mix gemms ~31 s/layer and shared+hc_apply ~22 s/layer are the next host/kernel items after that.
Full-run projection if (1) lands: ~2h11m -> ~35-45 min.

### Tab-refactor attempt (paused 2026-11-16 evening) — design + Vyb gotchas, reverted to a992759
Goal: eliminate json_parse/st_open from the runtime path entirely (they degrade to ~5-11 s per
parse as live docs/heap grow — measured: same-header re-parse in a CLEAN program goes 121 ms ->
610 ms over 4 parses and plateaus; in the driver it grows unboundedly). Design: at startup walk
all 83 shard headers once (st_open is cheap on the clean heap), bucket every tensor's
`name shard off len dtype` record by layer into per-layer table strings, and make every site
resolve by pure table lookup. Implementation got ~80% there (resolver + builder + wiring) but hit
a wall of Vyb codegen/scoping quirks; REVERTED to the green a992759 state. The design is sound —
the next session should reimplement it knowing:
1. **Vyb substring shares the parent buffer**: `index_of` on a substring returns PARENT-buffer
   offsets, and a second substring built from those offsets is garbage. Never chain derived
   substrings; scan the original in place.
2. **A concat needle with a TRAILING escaped quote breaks index_of** (`"...0.\"` -> -1 despite the
   text existing); mid-string escapes are fine. Build needles without trailing escapes.
3. `String::index_of` takes exactly ONE argument (no start offset).
4. `vec.set(i, <call expression>)` fails codegen in program-level functions ("Argument codegen
   failed") — bind the value to a local first... and locals declared inside a nested if-block are
   INVISIBLE to the following statements in the same function ("Undefined identifier") — hoist
   every working local to function scope (declare at the top, assign inside).
5. Bare `Vec<...>` globals are unsupported ("Undefined Vec variable"); struct globals at PROGRAM
   level work (gx_memo_probe: 200 reassignments clean), struct globals in an IMPORTED module break
   sibling-module sema (json_parse fails "Trait JParserOps is not defined").
6. `gx_shard_path(...)` calls also fail codegen inside such resolvers — inline the path
   concatenation instead.
With those known, the table builder + resolver are ~100 lines of straightforward Vyb. Expected:
startup ~60 s (83 shard walks on the clean heap), runtime resolve ~0.3 s/layer, full run ~35-45 min.

### Tab refactor LANDED (2026-11-16 evening, commit 8898deb) — runtime JSON parsing eliminated
The paused design was reimplemented in one clean pass with all six gotchas pre-applied
(function-scope locals, no call-exprs in Vec::set, no nested substrings, quote-free needles,
space-separated records, main-local tables). It works:
- Startup: one walk over all 83 shard headers (~0.15-0.76 s each, clean heap) buckets every
  tensor's `name shard off len dtype` record by layer into 45 table strings (main-local
  Vec<String>; bare Vec globals are unsupported). Non-layer tensors (embed_tokens) go to all
  buckets.
- Runtime: every site resolves via gx_resolve_tab(tab, dir, want) — one index_of on the layer's
  ~300 KB table + a forward char_at walk. NO json_parse, NO st_open at runtime.
- Verified: NLAYERS=4 PREFILL_OK, layer digests 0-3 byte-identical to the 45-layer gold;
  T_MOE resolve 1,145,661 -> 1,191 ms (963x); layer 3 total 92 -> 19.7 s; layer 4 (KDA)
  235 -> 44 s.
- 45-layer validation run in flight at commit time (prefill45_tab.log, prefill_vyb_45_tab.txt).
Remaining per-layer costs (layer 4 example): attn ~32 s (hc-mix gemms ~31 s of it — the actual
kernels are ~1 s), MoE deq ~9 s, ffn ~12 s. Next targets: the hc-mix gemm path and deq kernels.

**45-LAYER VALIDATION (2026-11-16 21:16): PREFILL_OK layers=45, rc=0, ~35 min wall
(20:41->21:16) — all 45 layer digests byte-identical to the 2h11m gold run.**
Session total for this pipeline: 13.5 h projected -> 2h11m -> **35 min (~24x)**, with

hc-mix gemms ~31 s, MoE deq ~9 s, ffn ~12 s) are genuine math/kernel work — the

green, digests byte-identical (prefill6_hoist.log). Effects: T_MOE deq 17.6 -> 8.4 s

time that driver is gated — do not change them blind.

### NEXT (option 2, scoped): f32 dequant+transform — the remaining ~8.4 s/layer
The MoE deq is now the biggest slice (~8.4 s/layer x 43). Root cause: had1/had2 are f64
MAC loops on a 3090 (f64 = 1/64 rate), and od/td2/fd round-trip kn*8 f64 bytes per matrix.
Vyb supports a real Float32 surface type (src/vre/llvm/cgen_expr.cpp:50) plus ld_f32n/
st_f32n/shfl_idx_f32/ld_shared_f32, so the plan is implementable:
1. f32 kernel variants in native/kernels/exl3.vyb: hgen writes H as f32 (st_f32n,
   half buffer), a FUSED deq+had1 that computes What via the mcg decode in-register into
   shared memory (one thread per (b,c), 128 consumers per row read shared), then had2f.
   Keep the f64 kernels — add new names, do not break the existing gate.
2. Driver: f32 buffers in a new exl3_deq32 path; consumer contract changes (mm_host gets
   f32 weights) -> this touches EVERY downstream digest.
3. Oracle re-baseline: new numpy gold with explicit tolerance (the skill: gate in exact
   mode only where decisions are decisive; pick tolerance from the numpy-vs-f32 spread,
   not by eye), regenerate the 45-layer gold, token/top1 check stays decisive.
4. Acceptance: full 45-layer run, wall target ~8-10 min.
Budget a full session; do not start it unless there is time to finish the re-baseline.

### OPTION 1 integration attempt: numerics PROVEN, perf regressed — driver change reverted (2026-11-17)
The f32 path was wired into glm_prefill (exl3_deq32 host fn: f32 deq -> had132/had232 ->
exl3cvt32 widen back to f64 -> unchanged host readback; tail_site MoE deqs switched).
NLAYERS=6 result (native/out/prefill6_f32.log):
- NUMERICS: PASS. f32 digests vs f64 gold: rel ~1e-7 (dense layers bit-identical, KDA/DSA
  layers sum rel 5e-8..2e-7). Exactly the predicted f32 noise. The semantics are sound.
- PERF: REGRESSED on KDA layers — T_MOE deq 60.7 s (was 8.4 s with f64+hoist), while the
  DSA layer IMPROVED (deq 3.5 s vs 7.7 s). Root cause (measured): exl3_deq32 does 4
  launches + 4 syncs + 10 allocs/frees per deq and allocates kn*12 device bytes vs kn*8;
  under the 4.6 GB heap the per-call launch/alloc/sync overhead explodes (~280 ms/deq x
  216 deqs), swamping the f32 math win. Same under-pressure pattern as st_open/hgen.
  DSA layers have fewer deqs per layer, so they netted a win.
- glm_prefill.vyb REVERTED to the f64+hoist state (HEAD). The kernels (exl3deq32/
  hgen32/had132/had232/cvt32) STAY — they are correct and additive.
NEXT (the real fix, before re-attempting integration):
1. Persistent per-size device arena in glm_prefill: allocate od32/td2/fd32/fdc/hd ONCE
   (max kn across the run is known per shape class), reuse across all deqs — removes
   ~10 allocs+syncs per deq. This is the same lesson as had_hoist: never alloc/free
   per-call under heap pressure.
2. Drop the cvt round-trip: bulk_dtoh f32 variant (host reads raw f32 bytes into a
   String via one cuMemcpyDtoH_v2, convert on host in the digest loop) or keep cvt but
   fuse it into had232's epilogue.
3. Re-measure with T_MOE deq as the metric; gate NLAYERS=6 (digest rel ~1e-7 vs gold is
   the expected PASS band; byte-identical is NOT expected on MoE layers).

### Arena attempt 2: scales fixed, cvt kernel faults — reverted, standalone probe next (2026-11-17)
Second integration attempt with a full per-size device arena (kn = ntiles*256 =

Findings, each measured:
1. Arena pointers DO propagate out of the freedom block fine (ARENA print showed valid
   handles) — the earlier cross-block suspicion was wrong.
2. FIRST bug (fixed): the sud/svd arena slots were sized 4 KB/16 KB but nsuh = krows*2
   = 8192 for cw=2048. cuMemcpyHtoD_v2 FAILS (not overflows) when the count exceeds the
   allocation's registered size — the runtime validates. Rule: arena slots must be sized
   to the TRUE max transfer, undersizing surfaces as a mysterious HtoD failure.
3. SECOND bug (open): after the scales fix, exl3cvt32 faults with CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS
   (sync=700) on the first expert, despite gid<kn, S=arE (kn*4), D=arF (kn*8) all in
   bounds. The fault poisons the context (next trellis HtoD fails htod1). Cause unknown —

### cvt fault bisected: kernel is bit-exact and clean in isolation (2026-11-17, later)
Standalone probe (native/host/glm_cvt_probe.vyb, log native/out/cvt_probe.log): the f32
pipeline + exl3cvt32 on expert 0 gate_proj runs rc=0, NO sync=700, and the cvt-widened
f64 digest is BYTE-IDENTICAL to the f32-path digest from check32 (gp0 sum/abs/sq/max/v0-v7
all equal). Conclusions:
- exl3cvt32 is correct and bit-exact; the Float32->Float cast was NOT the fault (also
  note: the explicit `x as Float` form compiled fine in the kernel; both forms work).
- NEW gotcha (confirmed): a struct-field assignment made INSIDE a freedom block does NOT
  propagate out — cvtbox.p read as 0 in main after being set inside freedom (the same
  class as the nested-if local gotcha, now for struct fields). Function-RETURN values and
  plain locals DO propagate. Route cross-block values through returns.
- Therefore the sync=700 in the glm_prefill arena attempt is specific to the arena layout
  inside that driver, not the cvt kernel. NEXT (cheap bisect): run the probe with
  ARENA-style buffers (alloc once in main, pass pointers in) instead of per-call allocs —
  if the 700 reproduces, shrink the arena set one buffer at a time; if not, the fault is
  an interaction with glm_prefill's other device state (suspect: an arena address
  colliding with the driver's own long-lived buffers, or kn*8 fdc vs the probe's
  4096*2048*8 — identical sizes, so more likely address placement).

### Bisect result: arena + cvt is CLEAN in the probe — fault is glm_prefill context state (2026-11-17)
The probe was converted to arena-style buffers with EXACTLY the glm_prefill sizes/shape
(native/host/glm_cvt_probe.vyb, native/out/cvt_probe2.log): alloc-once in main, pointers
passed in, zero per-deq allocs. Result: rc=0, NO sync=700, cvt digest byte-identical to
the f32 reference again. So arena layout + exl3cvt32 are jointly exonerated.
The fault therefore lives in the INTERACTION with glm_prefill's context state at the time
of the first f32 deq: thousands of prior alloc/free cycles, ~4.6 GB live heap, and the
driver's own long-lived buffers (the f64 path's per-call allocs run through the same
allocator continuously). NEXT bisect (cheap, in the probe): before the cvt call, stress
the context the way the driver does — a loop of alloc/free of ~64 MB blocks (say 200
iterations) plus a couple of large live allocations — and see if the 700 reproduces. If
it does, the culprit is allocator state (pool fragmentation around the arena); if not,
port the arena into glm_prefill incrementally (arena for f32 buffers only, f64 path
untouched) and gate NLAYERS=4 — the failure there was reproducible in ~90 s.

### Startup-warm-up experiment: warm-up works, protection does NOT (2026-11-17, final)
The startup-call bisect ran (NLAYERS=4): exl3_deq32 at startup, BEFORE layers 0-3,
SUCCEEDS (STARTUP_DEQ dw0=valid, cvt writes the 64 MB arena fdc cleanly) — and then the
run continued GREEN (PREFILL_OK, deq 2.5 s). BUT a second experiment — startup warm-up
call + fd/fdc switched to arena for the whole run — FAILED at layer 3: the warm-up cvt
write to the arena fdc is clean, then layers 0-3 run, then the layer-3 cvt to the SAME
arena slot faults sync=700. The warm-up does not protect.
CONCLUSION (sharpened): the fault is not first-use and not deterministic per-buffer —
the intervening GPU activity (layers 0-3: dense ffn mm_host/dev_alloc churn + DSA
attention) INVALIDATES the startup-allocated arena mapping for writes. Fresh cuMemAlloc
buffers at the same moment work. This is a Vyb RUNTIME/codegen-level device-pointer
LIFETIME question: suspect the generated code or vyb_runtime auto-freeing/reclaiming
cuMemAlloc'd pointers held in long-lived locals (see the allocator-pool note in the
vybos-development skill: "generated code also frees pointers born elsewhere") once
subsequent allocations churn. The evidence set for the compiler investigation: this
matrix + the probe (clean in every isolated configuration) + the startup warm-up result.

### ROOT CAUSE FOUND — CORRECTED (2026-11-17, final): the frees were EXPLICIT, not compiler-generated
CORRECTION of the section below: the "freedom-block exit auto-frees loc-held pointers"
theory was WRONG. The shim + IR dump showed the cuMemFree_v2(arF) came from an EXPLICIT
call: the probe's own `freedom { cuMemFree_v2(dW) }` after the readback, and in
glm_prefill the f64-path's per-expert `cuMemFree_v2(dWg/dWu/dWd)` frees (legitimate for
per-call buffers, fatal for arena-backed ones) plus the warm-up's `cuMemFree_v2(dw0)`.
No compiler change is needed; the VarDecl ownership path was correctly ruled out.
The useful durable lesson is only the operational one: when a returned pointer is
arena-backed, the caller must not run the per-call-buffer teardown on it. (See the ARENA
VERDICT section — the arena itself then measured WORSE at depth, so fresh-per-call stands
on performance grounds too.)
---
(The section below is the superseded WRONG theory, kept for the record.)
### ROOT CAUSE FOUND: freedom-block exit auto-frees loc-held pointers born elsewhere (2026-11-17)
The pointer-validity matrix pinned it exactly (native/out/cvt_probe11.log):
- dW (returned) == arF; PRE-HTOD-E (32 MB arE) rc=0 — still valid; PRE-HTODF (64 MB arF)
  rc=1 — INVALIDATED, immediately after the first exl3_deq returns. ALLOC-TIME HtoD to
  every arena slot rc=0 (all valid at allocation).
MECHANISM: the generated code auto-frees `loc`-held pointers at `freedom`-block exit.
exl3_deq's freedom block ASSIGNS `fdc = fdca` (the arena pointer, born in main's freedom
block) — at block exit the cleanup frees it as if the block had allocated it. The
allocator is size-thresholded (large-block pool): >= 64 MB goes back to the driver
(cuMemFree), smaller blocks stay pooled — which is why 32 MB arena buffers (fd/arE)
survive and the 64 MB fdc dies, why fresh-per-call fd/fdc is green (allocated INSIDE the
block = legitimately owned, pool reuse makes the next call green), and why the startup
warm-up did not protect (the warm-up call's OWN freedom exit freed the arena fdc; the
layer-3 cvt then wrote freed device memory -> sync=700).
This is a Vyb codegen/runtime OWNERSHIP bug: a freedom block must not free pointers it
merely ASSIGNED from elsewhere. It sits in the same cleanup machinery the impl agent's
fix/return-path-cleanup-v2 merge (3b7f9f73, 2026-11-16) just touched — check whether that
merge introduced or exposed it (the f64 path never held a large foreign pointer in a
freedom local before the f32 work, which is why it never surfaced).
FIX DIRECTION: at the freedom-exit cleanup, free only pointers the block ALLOCATED
(birth-tracking: assigned-from-parameter/outer-scope pointers must be skipped), or mark
cuMemAlloc-born pointers explicitly. Evidence set: this probe + the 4-way matrix +
cvt_probe11.log. The 90 s repro stands (glm_cvt_probe.vyb).
INTERIM (current committed state is correct): fd+fdc fresh per call — the block owns
them, the auto-free is legitimate, deq ~3 s, 13:54 acceptance.
WORKTREE READY: ~/Projects/Vyb-cuda-fix (branch fix/cuda-cross-module-launch off
3b7f9f73, debug build) REPRODUCES the fault identically (cvt_probe_debug.log: dW==arF,
PRE-HTODF rc=1, PRE-HTOD-E rc=0). This is the investigation binary.
SEARCH NARROWED: the VarDecl ownership/cleanup path in src/vre/llvm/cgen_decl.cpp
(needsCleanup, ~L452-530) does NOT cover Int locals — fd<Int> is a plain Int and gets no
VarDecl cleanup, so the invalidation is NOT that path. Either the freedom-exit reclaim
emits a device free elsewhere, or arF is not "freed" but its mapping is dropped another
way (the decisive next probe: after the first exl3_deq, cuMemcpyHtoD to arF fails while
arE succeeds — check whether the failure is rc=1 INVALID_VALUE persisting, i.e. the
driver no longer recognizes the pointer, vs a sticky context error).

### MINIMAL REPRO ACHIEVED (2026-11-17, final): cross-module kernel launch between writes
The probe (native/host/glm_cvt_probe.vyb, native/out/cvt_probe7.log) now reproduces the
sync=700 in ~60 s OUTSIDE glm_prefill, with three ingredients:
1. exl3_deq #1: cvt writes the 64 MB arena buffer — OK.
2. Churn: 10 launches of bf16deq (a kernel from a DIFFERENT PTX module, native/build/
   bf16.ptx), each with its own 2 MB + 8 MB cuMemAlloc/cuMemFree — all rc=0.
3. exl3_deq #2: cvt writes the SAME arena buffer — cuda_launch4i returns 0 but
   cuCtxSynchronize returns 700 (illegal address). Context poisoned afterwards.
Previously exonerated: pure alloc/free churn (no launches) — clean; module loads without
execution — clean; store width, alignment, overlap, padding — all ruled out.
THE BUG SHAPE: launching kernel(s) from a second PTX module between two launches of a
first module's kernel breaks the first module's subsequent kernel writes to a buffer
allocated before the second module's kernels ran. Suspects, in order:
(a) src/main.cpp vyb_cuda_launch4i — the dlsym'd cuLaunchKernel wrapper (shared static
    CuLaunchFn; param packing); (b) cuModuleLoadData/module-handle handling across
    modules; (c) stream/context state per module. The repro program is glm_cvt_probe.vyb
    — run it with the VYBFORGE_GX_DIR env (see cvt_probe7.log command line), ~60 s.
NEXT: Vyb worktree (worktree add -b fix/<name> origin/main, cmake --preset debug), run
the repro against the debug binary, then instrument vyb_cuda_launch4i / module handling.
A fix lands as a PR; VybForge gates: glm_cvt_probe + glm3x + NLAYERS=6 prefill, then the
fd/fdc arena re-enable + full 45-layer run (~5-6 min target).

### SANITIZER VERDICT: arF is cuMemFree'd DURING the first exl3_deq (2026-11-17, definitive)
compute-sanitizer memcheck on the slimmed probe (cvt_probe_san2.log, rc=99, 13,033 errors):
- The faulting accesses are "Invalid __global__ write of size 4 bytes" at exl3cvt32+0x1d0,
  starting 896 bytes past the end of a 64 MB allocation — and memcheck's nearest-allocation
  record proves ARF IS NOT IN THE CUDA ALLOCATION TABLE at that moment (the writes land
  inside arF's printed range, yet memcheck only knows the 64 MB block below it).
- arF was valid at allocation time (ALLOC-TIME HtoD rc=0 for every slot) and the first
  cvt's sync returned 0 — so arF was freed DURING the first exl3_deq, implicitly: the
  probe's exl3_deq contains NO cuMemFree of arena buffers (verified by grep). The freer
  is the Vyb runtime/codegen: a spurious/deferred cuMemFree of a 64 MB device pointer
  held in a freedom-block local. The "cross-module launch" framing was WRONG — the
  bf16deq churn is irrelevant; the first exl3_deq alone invalidates arF (PRE-HTODF rc=1
  BEFORE the churn, cvt_probe11b.log).
- The digest garbage (sum=-807, abs=100107, mostly zeros) is the same bug seen from the
  read side: the 8.4M per-element DtoH readbacks from arF fail with INVALID_VALUE
  (100+ captured in cvt_probe_san.log) because arF is already freed.
CORRECTED BUG STATEMENT: a 64 MB cuMemAlloc'd device buffer, allocated in main's freedom
block and passed as a parameter into a function whose freedom block assigns it to a
local, gets cuMemFree'd by the runtime/codegen during that function's execution —
independently of any explicit free. 32 MB buffers in the identical position survive
(size-dependent path in the allocator or codegen cleanup).
NEXT (Vyb worktree, ~/Projects/Vyb-cuda-fix debug build has the repro): instrument
cuMemFree_v2 in the repro (log ptr+size on every free) — the log will show WHO frees
arF (the call site's backtrace under the sanitizer, or a VYB_CDBG trace in the runtime's
device-free path). Then fix the ownership rule: a device pointer assigned from a
parameter must not be freed by the callee's cleanup; only the allocating scope frees.
Gates: glm_cvt_probe clean (both digests real) + glm3x + NLAYERS=6 + full 45 run with
the arena re-enabled (~5-6 min target).

### ARENA VERDICT: sync=700 fixed, but the arena REGRESSES at depth — fresh-fd stands (2026-11-17, final)
With the arena frees removed (the actual sync=700 fix), the full-arena config runs GREEN
(NLAYERS=6, PREFILL_OK, STARTUP_DEQ warm-up + no arena frees) — the bug is closed. BUT the
arena does not deliver the projected win: T_MOE deq degrades erratically with depth
(3.3 s L3 -> 62 s L4 -> 16 s L5) versus a steady ~3 s with fresh-per-call fd/fdc at the
same depth. Zero per-deq allocations yet 4-20x slower per deq at depth — the per-deq cost
under a growing heap is NOT allocation-dominated, and the fixed-address arena buffers make
it worse (mechanism unknown; the fresh mapping per deq somehow behaves better).
DECISION: glm_prefill REVERTED to the committed fresh-fd configuration (13:54 acceptance,
digests <=1.4e-6) — it remains the best measured configuration. The sync=700 mystery is
CLOSED (explicit frees of arena-backed results — an integration bug, not a compiler bug);
no Vyb compiler change is needed. The residual optimization target is the depth-dependent
deq cost growth (1.8 -> 13 s across the run) — a fresh investigation, separate from the
closed sync=700 item. The probe harness (glm_cvt_probe.vyb) and all bisect evidence stay
committed for that follow-up.

### DEQ-GROWTH ITEM CLOSED: the growth dissolves under analysis (2026-11-17, final)
Analysis of the 13:54 acceptance log's 42 MoE deq timings: the sequence does NOT grow
with depth — first-10 average 8.2 s, last-10 average 3.9 s (ratio 0.5, FASTER later): an
early-run effect on the first few MoE layers, then steady ~3-4 s. The earlier "1.8 -> 13 s
across the run" reading came from min/max outliers, not a trend. No depth-dependent
mechanism exists to chase; no arena, no compiler change needed. The deq total is ~3.4 min
of the 13:54 run; the remaining wall time is attention/ffn streaming — genuine math at the
measured per-layer costs. The f32 pipeline is at its current optimum. The next real
optimization targets are the hc-mix/attention streaming and (structural) the fused
dequant+gemm, both out of scope for the closed sync=700 item.

### FUSED-DQ BUG LOCALIZED: correct at cw=2048, WRONG at cw=4096 (down_proj) — the probe's missing case
The probe now runs BOTH geometries (extended glm_cvt_probe.vyb):
  gate_proj  cw=2048 (tensors 0/1/2): MISMATCH f32-pairs=0 of 4194304      -> BIT-IDENTICAL
  down_proj  cw=4096 (tensors 6/7/8): MISMATCH f32-pairs=4192256 of 4194304, first=8192 bytes
                                      (= element 2048 = row 16, col 0)    -> WRONG
Rows 0..15 are exact; everything with kb16 = row/16 >= 1 is wrong. That is precisely
where the 16-row trellis tile index (tile16 = kb16*nbc16 + nb16, nbc16 = cw/16) starts
varying, so the defect is a trellis-stride / tmp-layout assumption inside exl3dq1s that
only holds when cw == 2048. The driver's gate and up deqs (EINTER=2048) are therefore
fine; the down deq (H=4096) is garbage -- which is exactly the full-run divergence
(L3 onward, abs 3.4e-1) and NOTHING else. The separate path (exl3deq32 + exl3had132)
with the same cw=4096 is correct (DOWN_SEP rc=0 and the fresh-fd driver gates green),
so the reference and the trellis layout are not in doubt: the bug is in the fused
kernel's cw=4096 indexing.
SUSPECTS, in order:
  1. tile16 = kb16*nbc16 + nb16 -- verify nbc16 is the trellis's true fast-axis stride
     by parameterising it (p+48) and testing stride 256 vs 128 at cw=4096; deq32 uses
     nbc = cw/16 and is green, so if 256 fails the difference is elsewhere in the pair
     (nb16 derivation vs deq32's tile->(kbv,nbv) direction).
  2. the T (tmp) block layout: dq1s writes T[block*8192 + b2*128 + d] (64-row blocks),
     exl3had132 reads T[(row/128)*16384 + (row%128)*128 + d] (128-row blocks). Both
     linearise to row*128 + d, which is only shape-free while the column-band count
     matches; at cw=4096 there are 32 column bands instead of 16 -- check whether the
     tmp is really a flat row-major [rows,128] or a per-(row-band, col-band) blocked
     buffer, and make dq1s agree with had232's actual convention.
  3. block ordering rb64 = block/nbc128 vs the row-band-major assumption used by the
     consumer.
FIX PROTOCOL: parameterise the disputed stride, probe both cw values until
MISMATCH=0 for BOTH, then re-integrate in glm_prefill and gate NLAYERS=6 against
prefill_vyb_45_tab.txt (expect 3.4e-8), then the 45-layer acceptance.
PROGRESS (cvt_probe37.log): suspect 2 CONFIRMED AND FIXED — exl3had132/had232's T
index is gid-linear (kbv*nbc128+nbv)*16384 + (row%128)*128 + d; dq1s was writing its
own 64-row block numbering (block*8192 + e2), which coincides with had132's convention
only for rows 0..63 of column band 0 — exactly the 6 spot elements the shared-kernel
gate checked (the earlier full-buffer scan ran on the NAIVE dq1, which is gid-linear
and was never wrong). Fix: dq1s phase 2 now computes gidT from row/col explicitly
(kbvT*nbc128 + nb128)*16384 + bT*128 + d. RESULT: gate cw=2048 STILL BIT-IDENTICAL
(0/4194304); down cw=4096 improved only 4192256 -> 4112384, first mismatch still
element 2048 (row 16). So a SECOND defect remains on the deq side at cw=4096: rows
0..15 exact, every kb16 >= 1 row wrong, while deq32 with the same tile formula
(tile = kbv*(cw/16) + nbv) is green in the driver. The formulas are textually
identical, so the difference must be in what the fused launch actually reads —
prime suspect now: the probe's fused launch reuses the pp2 staging and tq2 trellis
buffer from the gate section (only p+32 cw is overwritten for the down test) —
verify t6.len() == tq2's allocation and that gx_read(g,6) is really the down
trellis, by staging a FRESH tq3 for the down launch. If a fresh trellis buffer
fixes it, the driver integration must likewise use a per-tensor trellis staging
(the driver already does); if not, dump raw deq values (pre-had1) for rows 16..31

ELIMINATED (cvt_probe38.log): fresh trellis staging (tq3, own alloc, t6 staged,
p+0 repointed) — DOWN_MISMATCH unchanged (4112384, first=8192). The probe's trellis
reuse was NOT the cause; the defect is inside exl3dq1s's deq-side indexing for

while deq32's tile = kbv*(cw/16)+nbv is green in the driver at the same geometry;

NEXT EXPERIMENT (cheapest first): parameterise the trellis fast-axis stride
(p+48, default cw/16) and at cw=4096 try stride 128 and the transposed indexing
tile = nbv*(rows/16) + kb16 — one probe run per variant, gate+down must both be

ELIMINATED (cvt_probe39.log): transposed tile = nb16*(rows/16)+kb16 at cw=4096 —
WORSE (4128768 mismatches, first=0). Current-stride and transposed both fail; a
flat stride of 128 is impossible for cw=4096 (nb16 up to 255 would collide), so
the tile formula itself is NOT the bug — dq1s and deq32 use textually identical
tile/slot/deq math, yet disagree at cw=4096 from row 16. STRUCTURAL CONCERN TO
SETTLE FIRST (cheap, host-side): the kernel's phase-1 comment says "8192 threads
= 8192 elements" per 64x128 tile, but the launch is 256 threads/block with the
tile's rows covered by consecutive 256-thread CUDA blocks (each stages a row
pair into its own shared, phase 2 computes that same row pair) — this is
self-consistent ONLY if the grid covers (rows/64)*(cw/128) LOGICAL tiles while
`block = tid/8192` spans grid*256/8192 = grid/32 logical tiles. With grid =
ntiles/32 = 1024 CUDA blocks, block only reaches 31 -> only 32 logical tiles =
262,144 of 8,388,608 outputs computed; the rest of T is stale memory. The gate's
"bit-identical" verdicts rest on 6 spot elements (rows 0..1 — exactly the rows
the first CUDA blocks compute) and the full-buffer scan was run against the
NAIVE dq1, never the shared kernel. SETTLE BY: memset t3 to a sentinel before
the shared launch, count non-sentinel slots after — if coverage is 1/32, the
kernel needs an inner loop (32 iterations per thread over e2, or grid =
ntiles*256/256 with per-thread element loop) and BOTH prior A/B verdicts for
the shared kernel must be redone with full coverage. This also fully explains
the driver divergence pattern (large-scale wrongness from the first fused layer)
without any trellis-layout mystery.
NOTE: cuMemsetD8_v2 does NOT exist in the Vyb CUDA binding (only cuMemcpyDtoH_v2
and cuMemcpyHtoD_v2 — probe40 failed to compile); the sentinel test must instead
use a double-launch differential: alloc a second 32MB buffer, run the shared
launch with the gate trellis, bulk-copy t3 into it (DtoH t3 -> host String ->
HtoD into the copy; no DtoD binding), relaunch with a DIFFERENT trellis
(tq3/down already staged), then count pairs that changed — the changed count is
exactly the number of slots the kernel wrote. 4194304 pairs expected if coverage
is 131072 if it is grid/32.
RESOLVED (cvt_probe41/42/43): THE COVERAGE BUG IS CONFIRMED AND FIXED. Probe41's
full-buffer gate scan on the shared kernel: SHARED_MISMATCH 4063232 of 4194304,
first=1048576 bytes = element 262144 = exactly 32 logical tiles x 8192 — the
kernel computed 1/32 of the matrix and left stale memory elsewhere; the computed
1/32 was bit-perfect. CAUSE: grid was ntiles/32 = 1024 CUDA blocks x 256 threads
= 262144 threads, but each 64x128 tile needs 32 CUDA blocks (one row pair of 256
threads each; phase 1 stages and phase 2 consumes the same pair within one CUDA
block, so the design is self-consistent — it just needs the full grid). FIX:
grid = ntiles (32768 blocks) for the shared launch. RESULTS (cvt_probe43):
  gate  cw=2048, tmode=0: SHARED_MISMATCH 0 of 4194304  -> FULL-BUFFER BIT-IDENTICAL
  down  cw=4096, tmode=0: DOWN_MISMATCH 3670016 of 4194304, first=8192 bytes
        = element 4096 = row 32 (kb16=2) -> rows 0..31 now correct (was 0..15 at
        grid 1024), kb16>=2 still wrong. Transposed tmode at full grid: all wrong
        (probe42, first=0) — transposed eliminated for good.
The grid fix is HOST-SIDE ONLY (no kernel change). The correctness boundary moved
from row 16 to row 32 with the grid fix because the previously-"correct" rows 0..15
at grid 1024 were real computations and rows 16..31 were stale-coincidence; at full
grid rows 16..31 are computed and correct, so the residual cw=4096 defect now
starts at kb16=2 (tile 512+). REMAINING STEP for item 1: arbitrate the down
mismatch with the PYTHON GOLD (gold_exl3_deq.py) — dump the down td2 from BOTH the
separate path and dq1s and see which one agrees with the oracle; if the separate

down launch currently runs tmode=0 (tmD staged 0 in cvt_probe43 state).

inv-slot lookup, H read, accumulation) against the separate path across the whole

deq32's nbc) and nbc128 (=cw/128, gate-proven via the T layout), the disagreement
at cw=4096 kb16>=2 CANNOT be explained by the fused code alone. Two possibilities

CRITICAL REFRAME (end of session): the driver's fresh-fd green CANNOT arbitrate
deq placement correctness. The f64 gold was produced by the driver's OWN f64
pipeline, which uses the same nbc = cw/16 tile-placement convention as the f32
path — a systematic placement error CANCELS EXACTLY between the f32 candidate and
the f64 gold, and the gemm consumes the placement consistently, so every
driver-internal gate passes regardless of whether the placement matches the
checkpoint's true trellis layout. The ONLY true arbiter for "which trellis tile
holds which weights" is the python reference decoder (gold_exl3_deq.py). The
factor-8 relation (fused tile 512+k == deq32 tile 64+k at cw=4096, boundary
exactly at tile 512) is the fingerprint to test against it: run the gold decoder
on the down tensor and check which tile index it assigns to true (row 32, col 0)
— 512 (fused right, driver convention wrong at cw=4096) or something decoding to
deq32's od row 32 (separate right, fused wrong). If the FUSED side is right, the
residual 'divergence' was the driver convention all along and item 1's fused path
may already be correct — the NLAYERS=6 gate against the driver gold would then
legitimately fail while the fused path matches the TRUE model, and the decision
about which convention to ship belongs to Rick. DO NOT re-integrate until the
gold arbitration is done; this is a correctness question, not a performance one.
PERMUTATION SIGNATURE — CORRECTED (the probe45 gidp formula omitted had132's
nbc=32 block factor; the raw finding stands, the interpretation changes): the
probe found fused td2 gid 4096 == arD gid 131072. In had132's true layout
(gid = (kbv*nbc + nbv)*16384 + b*128 + d, nbc = cw/128 = 32 at cw=4096):
  fused slot gid 4096        = (row 32, col 0)     [kbv 0, b 32, band 0]
  arD    slot gid 131072     = block 8 = kbv 0, nbv 8, b 0 -> (row 0, col 1024)
So the TRUE signature is: fused td2(row 32, col 0) == separate td2(row 0, col 1024).
In deq terms: the fused dequanted tile 512 (kb16=2, nb16=0) for (32,0) while the
separate path's value at (0,1024) came from tile 8 (kbv=0, nbv=8). The fused slot
(32,0) holds data the separate path attributes to (0,1024) — a row/col-band
interleave between the two paths at cw=4096, NOT the row-group scatter first
reported. (The earlier "sep_row=(row%32)+(row/32)*1024" reading was an artifact of
the probe's wrong gidp — disregard it.) Candidates that fit an interleave:
  - the fused T write's (kbvT*nbc128 + nb128) block term vs had132's (kbv*nbc+nbv)
    — identical formulas, but the fused's kbvT = rowT/128 is computed from the
    64-row scheduling block; verify no 32-row vs 128-row confusion survives in
    gidT at cw=4096 (at cw=2048 nbc128=16 masks it);

LEAK REPRODUCED AND ISOLATED (alloc_probe3.vyb, committed): 200 x 32 read_at
drops -> str_stats live grows +32 entries +4.2MB PER ITERATION (live_bytes
exactly = the dropped read_at buffers), RSS 69 -> 112MB monotonic,
retained_pool=0 (the pool is not involved). read_all (plain String) recycles
cleanly; read_at (String? — an OPTIONAL unwrap) leaks EVERY buffer. ROOT CAUSE:
the ownership pass does not emit the scope-exit release for an optional-unwrapped
String — the read_at buffer is registered refs=0 (borrowed), the first holder
retains (refs=1), and the match arm's release never fires, so the buffer pins at
refs=1 forever. THE VYB FIX (next session, first task): in src/vre/llvm/
cgen_ownership.cpp (and/or cgen_stmt.cpp's match-arm handling), emit the release
for a String bound from a String? unwrap (the `s -> { ... }` arm of
`match (read_at(...))`) at the arm's scope exit — the same release the
plain-String match arm already emits. Repro: native/host/alloc_probe3.vyb (200
iters, +4.2MB/iter, live_bytes == dropped bytes exactly). IMPACT: EVERY gx_read
in every driver leaks its tensor bytes — the prefill's 45-layer runs leak ~54GB
(masked by the raised caps), the chain dies at 80G. Fix in Vyb, rebuild, rerun
alloc_probe3 (live_bytes must stay flat), then the chain gate ->
DECODE_CHAIN_MATCH, then the had_hoist mirror.

is dropped WITHOUT freeing (refs stay 1 forever). MINIMAL REPRO (next session,

PROBE7: THE LEAK REPRODUCED MINIMALLY AND DETERMINISTICALLY
(native/host/alloc_probe7.vyb): a wrapper fn does match(read_at(f,0,4MB))
{ t -> s = t } and RETURNS s; the caller binds s<String> = wrap_read(f) and
reassigns 6x. ALL 6 buffers: REG refs=1 -> 4 RETs -> 3 RELs (old=4,4,3) ->
refs=1 stuck, no RETIRE — one retain per buffer never gets its release. The
direct unwrap-to-binding shape (probe4) is FLAT; the WRAPPER-RETURN layer adds
the unbalanced retain (the return of a shared binding retains the return copy;
somewhere in that return->transfer->bind chain one retain lacks its release).
THE FIX PATH (next session): bisect with two probe variants — (a) the wrapper
assigning to an `our`/outer binding instead of returning, (b) probe4's direct
shape — the delta names the layer; then mirror the Vec call-result fix in the
ownership pass (the String return-copy's retain must pair with a release on
the path that consumes it). The 2MB-class cost is bounded (~4MB per full run)
but the SAME return-path shape likely underlies part of the ~296KB class.

unchanged). THE LEAK LIVES IN THE CALLED-FUNCTION MATCH/BIND ITSELF: the same
match(read_at){t -> s = t} shape is FLAT in main (probe4) and leaks 1 ref per
buffer inside any called function — the function-scope cleanup for the
io-intrinsic match locals (the match scrutinee temp's arm-exit release /
the s binding's scope-exit release) does not fully fire in a callee. NEXT:

armRetainedStringBindings releases + the function-body exitScope cleanup) —
the missing release emission is visible by diffing which release calls exist
in each; then fix the ownership pass for the function-body scope.

=================== RESIDUAL LEAK CLOSED (2026-11-18, overnight) ===================
ROOT CAUSE: the scrutineeIsIoIntrinsic check matched only the vyb_io_ runtime
symbol prefix — the imported io names (read_at/read_all/read_bytes*) never
fired the payload transfer in callees, so the callee's `s = t` kept BOTH the
stringOverwrite retain AND the payload-bind retain; AND the io path had
dropped the optional's inner-store compensating release. THE FIX (two edits in
cgen_stmt.cpp): (1) the check also matches the imported io names; (2) the io
path restores releaseStringValue(originalOptPayload). VALIDATED: probe7d 6/6
BALANCED (RET=2 REL=3 RETIRE=1 — was 3/2/0 leaking), the suite 1358/1358
green, AND the 3-layer prefill's final live set is FLAT 39.8MB (751,566 <1K
loader strings = 25.8MB one-time bounded + 44 legit <1M buffers) with ZERO
trellis-sized or ~296KB leaks — the ~300MB/layer growth is GONE. Also: the
VYB_STR_DUMP=1 live-set size histogram in vyb_str_stats (a leaking class
names itself), and the prefill calls vyb_str_stats at PREFILL_OK. Vyb landed
to main (81bab23c). The chain's RSS should now be flat-ish modulo the legit

=================== THE 3X PERF MYSTERY: SOLVED (the R split-brain) ==================
THE SERVE'S DECODE STEPS RAN THE ENGINE'S FN CALLS WITH R=20 WHILE THE SERVE'S
INTERMEDIATES WERE R=1-SIZED. `sv_R = 1` rebinds only the SERVE's module
global; the engine's site fns (hc_apply/moe_tail_step/attn_site_c) read the
ENGINE's own module-global R (still 20) — every decode-step fn call indexed
R=20 loops over the R=1 intermediates: out-of-bounds gets returning 0.0 down
a slow path (85us/call) at 20x the iterations = the 108s step layers and the
all-zero s2 digests (the phase 1 was R=20 on both sides — consistent — which
is why it matched the gate and masked the bug; the earlier draft 10788 was
computed from the R=20 mis-indexed state and was CORRECT ONLY BY THE
TEACHER-FORCED ARGMAX LUCK of the deterministic gate path — treat the
pre-fix serve outputs as untrusted). THE FIX: set_engine_R(n) in the engine
(an assignment inside the engine's own module scope) + the serve calls
set_engine_R(1) before the steps. VERIFIED: the step digests BYTE-IDENTICAL
to the gate's reference (l0s0 s2 n=16384 sum=-928573974546 v0=-7890726791,
exact), the dumps 55s -> 3s, the step layers ~25s (the gate's 9-35s range),
the full request ~45-75 min (was ~3.5h), the draft 10788 HTTP 200.
DIAGNOSIS TOOLING (committed): now_ms() in the engine (pure-Vyb /proc/uptime
parse, the rss_mb pattern) + the SEC * section timings in the serve's step
body + the DUMPTIME lines in the engine's dump_to. THE RESIDUAL: the phase-1
runs ~3x slower than the gate's phase-1 (13s vs 4s/layer, R=20 consistent on
both sides — a different, smaller cause; the next perf item). THE LESSON:
module-global config that fns read implicitly (R) must be rebound through the
OWNING module — an importer's assignment shadows.

(probe10g: 5us/call); the dump/digest formatting (probe10h/10k: 0.65s per
327680-element dump); the append file size (554MB target: 0.64s); the shim
local constants vs module constants (no pace change); the state-in-main move
(no pace change — but correct per probe10a). The real cause was semantic
(the R split-brain), not mechanical.

=================== THE EXTERN DEDUP LANDED; THE JIT MANGLE REMAINS ==================
The extern dedup is LANDED in module_registry.cpp: the carried extern blocks
are deduped by name (the first module to declare a foreign symbol carries it;
the later blocks carry only their new members) — glm_serve.vyb is SEMANTIC-
CLEAN with the state-in-main architecture. THE REMAINING: the JIT link fails
on 5 mangled cu* symbols (__pv_9554c5f9_cuCtxSynchronize, cuMemAlloc_v2,
cuMemcpyHtoD_v2, cuModuleLoadData, cuModuleGetFunction): the semantic binds
the serve's main's cu* references to MANGLED names while the carried extern
declarations are PLAIN — the declarations and the references disagree. THE
NEXT SESSION'S TASK: find which pass mangles the references (the ownership
pass or the codegen's module-qualified handling — the trace:
VYB_TRACE_MANGLE=1 on the serve shows the extern-carry entries for the cu*
from MULTIPLE modules: tensor, gx, glm_engine — the dedup filters the later
blocks' duplicates, so the mangle's source is the REFERENCE side: the
semantic/codegen binding of the serve's main's cu* calls to the mangled
names). The likely fix: the same module-NS fallback as the identifier
resolution, applied in the ownership/codegen passes — or the reference-side
dedup: the semantic's resolution of the cu* names to the FIRST contributor's
binding for ALL the passes consistently.
=================== THE #432 EXEMPTION LANDED; THE FN-SCOPE INSERT DEBUG NEXT ==================
The extern exemption is LANDED (the __extern_C namespace statements skip the
#432 per-module identity + the mangleBareStmt — the foreign C symbols keep
their exact names). THE REMAINING ERRORS (2): dsa_attn_site_c and
kda_attn_site_c undefined at the serve's nested callsites. THE STATE OF THE
ANALYSIS: the engine's fns are share'd (the exports ✓), granted into the
root's effectiveScope ✓, spliced-first (the reorder ✓ so they precede main in
the resolved body), and the #432 identity does NOT fire for them (the
privateMangled lacks their names — no cross-module collision). The plain-name
resolution SHOULD work at every step, yet main's nested callsites fail while
the same calls at main's TOP LEVEL work (probe9's a = f1() ✓ vs the nested
b = f1() ✓ — WAIT: probe9's NESTED call WORKED (b=42 ✓)! THE DIFFERENCE
BETWEEN probe9 AND the serve: the probe's module is tiny (1 fn); the engine is
5883 lines with 177 module-scope state declarations + 36 share'd fns + the
extern block. THE NEXT DEBUG: instrument the semantic's FunctionDeclaration
visit (the scope-insert site: the fn name + the scope address) and main's
visit — the diff between probe9 and the serve reveals which mechanism (the
scope-gate's hides on the fn, the module-NS fallback, or the visit order)
differs at scale. The instrumentation pattern: the [fnpresert]/[splice] trace
(VYB_TRACE_MANGLE) is already in place.
=================== THE FINAL MECHANISM: THE SPLICE SKIPS THE ENGINE'S FNS ==================
The pass-1 pre-insert (the constants + the top-level fns into the global scope
before the statement visits) FIXED ALL THE CONSTANT ERRORS — and the debug

body — the splice's carry path skips them for the whole-module import, while
the OTHER modules' functions (the io's hash_chars, the http's socket_open...)

splice loop (the `for (auto& importedStmt : importedRecord.module->body)`

=================== CONSTANTS FIXED; FNS' NESTED VISIBILITY REMAINS ==================
THE UPPERCASE SHARE BUG (found + fixed): my share pass matched only
lowercase-starting names — ALL the UPPERCASE constants (NSLOT, HC, H, HEADS,

THE SHARE'D FUNCTIONS still fail inside the nested scopes (dsa_attn_site_c,

=================== THE GAP IS UNIVERSAL: NESTED-SCOPE IMPORTED SYMBOLS ==================
The shim proved it: the constants shim (NSLOT/HEADS/KD) resolved those three,
then the NEXT constants surfaced (CONV_K/NDSLOT/CAPT/KV_LORA/IDIM/R/HC), and
worse — the ENGINE'S SHARE'D FUNCTIONS (dsa_attn_site_c, kda_attn_site_c) are
ALSO undefined inside main's nested scopes. THE RULE: imported-module symbols
(the constants, the externs, AND the share'd functions) are visible at the
importer's TOP-LEVEL statements but INVISIBLE inside the importer's NESTED
scopes (the while/match bodies — exactly where the http serve loop lives).

resolution — when the scope-stack lookup fails, fall back to the module

DEQ-CACHE STATUS: BUILT AND VALIDATED (4bd0f53) — deq_cache.vyb + probe6
(PROBE6_OK: save/load round-trip 0/1024 mismatches, the wrong-size load

is NE=288 with ~130-160 DISTINCT (layer, expert) pairs hit per prefill — a full

(3 x 4MB gx_read per expert off the apex NFS, ~130 experts/layer = ~1.6GB/layer
of network reads), not the deq kernels. THE THREE REAL OPTIONS:

      stays (~3s/layer), the reads go local;
  (c) the full deq'd cache as built: 1.1-1.4TB on the apex NFS (2.8T free) —
      the fastest runs (no deq, no reads, the pure h2d) but a huge footprint.
RECOMMENDED: (a) first (free), then (b) if the deq compute matters. The
deq_cache module as built supports (c) as-is and (b) with the same functions
(the trellis Strings need a String<->bytes shim — the gx_read returns String;
write_bytes takes Vec<UInt8> — a small conversion or a String-bytes io call).

/home/rick/vybforge-glm53-checkpoint (164G, 108 files, byte-identical: the

EXACT LEDGER (VYB_STR_TRACE, the A-loop buffers, address-tracked): each read_at
buffer: REG refs=1 (the owned birth ref) + 3 RETs (THREE retains: the optional's
inner store, the shallow-copy's retain, the s-assignment's retain) = 4 refs;
releases observed: REL old=4 (the compensating release) + REL old=3 (t's
scope-exit) = 2 releases in-window -> 1 ref LEAKED per buffer, deterministic,
32/40 tracked buffers unbalanced (the balanced 8 = the small digest strings).
THE FIX IS ONE OF (test each by rebuild + probe4 in minutes):
  (a) drop ONE retain: the shallow-copy's retain in copyOwnedValueForBinding
      (make the optional payload extraction TRANSFER the birth ref instead of
      shallow-copy+retain for io-intrinsic scrutinees), or
  (b) add the missing release: the s binding's own scope-exit or the birth-ref
      consumption at the first transfer (make the io String wrap consume the
      birth ref instead of retaining).
The ledger arithmetic is unambiguous: 1 birth + 3 retains - 2 releases = 1 leak
per read_at buffer; x 200 iterations x 32 reads = the chain's 80G. The fix that
makes probe4's live_bytes flat is the correct one; then the suite, the chain
gate, and the had_hoist mirror.
LEAK ROOT-CAUSED (VYB_STR_TRACE=1 on alloc_probe + the chain): the probe's 530MB
is POOL RETENTION by design (freed 4MB blocks recycle in the pool's buckets, cap
8GB) — the probe does NOT leak. The CHAIN's leak: ~58K live registry strings per
phase-2 layer — the phase-2 loop passes gx_read(...) tensor/trellis Strings as
INLINE TEMPORARIES directly into exl3_deq(...)/deq_bf16(...) calls; those
temporaries register (refs=1) and their release never fires at statement end.
The OLD chain never reached phase 2 (OOM at phase-1 L12), so this leak was never
exercised until the resolver port made phase 2 reachable. THE FIX (VybForge-side,
chain driver): bind every gx_read result to a REUSABLE String variable declared
once per loop scope (trel0/trel1/... for the 9 per-expert reads; tl0..tlN for the
~25 per-layer tensor reads) and pass the VARIABLE — the reassignment free path
(assign.old_string released after the store) is proven correct, so rebinding
bounds the live set to one buffer per slot. Same treatment for the per-expert
scales and the per-layer hc/ffn reads. After the fix: RSS per layer must be flat
(rss_mb probes in both loops); then rerun the gate (120G cap), expect
DECODE_CHAIN_MATCH OK, commit, and the had_hoist mirror re-application follows. REMAINING FOR ITEM 1 (mechanical, next session): re-apply the
glm_prefill integration (the reverted fused-path edits: fdqs/finv loads, persistent
mapdev/invbase, exl3_deq32 fused section, tail_site threading) with TWO fixes over
the reverted state: dqgrid = ntiles (NOT ntiles/32) and the T-layout gidT write
(already in the kernel — the driver only needs the grid fix); then NLAYERS=6 gate
vs prefill_vyb_45_tab.txt (expect ~3.4e-8), then the 45-layer acceptance and the
new gold. The reverted integration diff is in git history (commit a9a06ec era) and
the probe51 configuration is the reference for the staging pattern.
45-LAYER ACCEPTANCE (prefill45_fused.log, commit 1659b39 + tmode removal):
PREFILL_OK layers=45, ALL 45 digests within 1.42e-6 of the f64 gold
(prefill_vyb_45_tab.txt; worst L35) — the SAME band as the fresh-fd baseline.
ITEM 1's CORRECTNESS IS COMPLETE AT FULL DEPTH. OPEN: performance. The run's
layer totals sum to ~36.8 min vs the 13:54 baseline's ~13.9 min — but the
comparison is UNRESOLVED: the only full-45 log on disk (prefill45.log) shows
~6.8s deep-layer totals that match NEITHER the 13:54 profile nor the fused run,
and the fused run's ATTENTION times also grew with depth (attn does not touch the
fused path at all), so the slowdown may be environmental (the L4-style stall
recurring) rather than the fused deq. The NLAYERS=6 comparison showed the fused
deq equal-or-faster (L3 2603 vs 2894, L5 2943 vs 2962). NEXT: (1) identify the
true 13:54-era log (or rerun the fresh-fd config NLAYERS=6 back-to-back with the
fused config for a clean A/B); (2) if the fused deq is genuinely slower at depth,
profile T_MOE deq vs depth — the earlier depth-growth pattern (1.8s -> 13s) may
interact with the fused path's per-call trellis HtoD. Correctness is not in
question; only the wall-clock claim awaits a clean measurement.
A/B RESULT (same conditions, back-to-back 45-layer runs, Nov 17 late):
  fresh-fd: PREFILL_OK 45, layer-total sum 1688.7s (deq 1178.1s, attn 393.9s)
  fused:    PREFILL_OK 45, layer-total sum 2205.7s (deq 1558.0s, attn 506.4s)
  -> fused deq ~32% slower, overall ~31% slower, BUT BOTH configs run ~2x slower

  a degraded state, and the two runs' deep layers diverge wildly (freshfd L36+

### FUSED-DQ INTEGRATION: kernel proven, DRIVER INTEGRATION DIVERGES — reverted, open bug documented (2026-11-17 late)
The exl3dq1s kernel was integrated into glm_prefill's exl3_deq32 (fused dq1s replaces
deq32+had132; persistent mapdev/invbase built once at startup by exl3inv; 3 launches:
dq1s -> had232 -> cvt32; od never materialized). RESULT: NLAYERS=6 PREFILL_OK and MUCH
faster (L4 total 4.8 s vs 5.7 fresh-fd; deq 2.1-2.4 s vs 2.9-3.1) — BUT the layer digests
DIVERGE from the f64 gold starting at L3: abs rel 3.4e-1, sq 5.4e-1 (the fresh-fd config:
3.4e-8/6.3e-8). The divergence starts exactly at the first fused-deq layer.
CONTRADICTION: the probe proves the fused kernel's td2 output is BIT-IDENTICAL to the
separate path across the ENTIRE 8.4M-element buffer (MISMATCH f32-pairs=0 of 4194304,
cvt_probe35.log) for expert-0 gate_proj — yet the full driver run diverges. The map is a
verified bijection (256 unique (r,c), no duplicates, r/c in 0..15 — scanned map.bin
directly). The exl3inv build syncs clean. Everything analytically checks out.
OPEN BUG: the driver context differs from the probe in ways that matter — candidates:
(1) the probe tested ONE matrix; the driver runs 288 experts x 3 matrices back-to-back
sharing the arena — a state/race the single-shot probe cannot see; (2) the persistent
mapdev/invbase interact with later allocations; (3) an interaction with the driver's
other concurrent state. NEXT: in-driver A/B — dump the fused vs separate td2 for the
FIRST expert INSIDE glm_prefill (both paths in one run, same expert) and diff; if they
differ in-driver but not in the probe, the driver's arena/state is the difference; if
identical, walk experts until the divergence appears. glm_prefill REVERTED to the
committed fresh-fd green state (13:54, digests <=1.4e-6 verified) — the fused kernels +
probe + all evidence remain committed for the follow-up.

### SHARED-MEMORY exl3dq1s WRITTEN — gating blocked by probe-harness instability (2026-11-17 night, final)
The cooperative redesign is implemented: native/kernels/exl3dq.vyb (own module, own PTX
so its 32 KB shared doesn't tax exl3.ptx's kernels; compiled with --kernel-shared 32768;
note the flag CAPS at 49152 — the 64 KB f32 tile became a 64-row x 128-col tile = 32 KB).
Block = one 64x128 tile, 256 threads, ONE element + ONE output per thread (8192 threads
per tile — the initial per-thread k-loops double-covered the tile and wrote 1 MB past the
32 KB shared window; caught by memcheck as shared writes at 0x8480/0x8500), kernel_barrier,
phase 2 reads shared. fp16_to_f64 copied into the module (kernel files don't share fns).
GATING BLOCKED: the probe SEGVs inside the shared-gate block before FUSED_SHARED prints —
most likely the first exl3_deq's freedom exit frees the trellis String (born elsewhere —
the documented hazard) and the shared gate's trellis.len()/HtoD then reads freed host
memory (nondeterministic; the naive gate's identical read survived by luck). FIX NEXT
SESSION: in glm_cvt_probe.vyb, re-read trellis/suh/svh via gx_read FRESH inside the shared
gate block (or capture trellis.len() into an Int BEFORE the first exl3_deq), then gate:
T3 spot-compare vs arD (the compare code is in place, reading t2out/arD outside the
freedom block — Int assignments propagate, Vecs built inside a freedom block do NOT
survive its exit: build readback Vecs OUTSIDE freedom blocks).
Also verified this session: 4-byte DtoH (dtoh32) fails in the binding (returns 0.0
silently) — use 8-byte dtoh reads and pair-interpret, or spot-compare raw.
OPEN ANOMALY (san6, launched after the 1:1 fix + rebuild): 92 OOB shared writes at
exl3dq1s+0x16c0 (b ~ 66+, offsets 0x8480-0x8514+) persist — impossible under the 1:1
mapping if rem < 8192. Two candidates: (a) the probe loaded a STALE exl3dq.ptx (the
recompile silently skipped writing once — verify with cmp after rm); (b) Vyb Int division
ROUNDS instead of truncating: block = tid/8192 for the last tile's tids (tid up to
262143, 262143/8192 = 31.9995) would round to 32 -> rem = tid - 262144 = NEGATIVE ->
negative shared offsets (the PTX shows .shared.v2.f32 [%rd208+-4] — a negative-offset
vectorized access — supporting (b)). Verify: run a tiny Vyb program printing 262143/8192;
if it prints 32, division rounds — compute block with an explicit floor
((tid - rem)/8192 after rem = tid % 8192 via a mod intrinsic, or guard rem < 0).

### FUSED DEQ+HAD1 KERNEL: written, BIT-IDENTICAL, needs shared-memory redesign for the win (2026-11-17 night)
Item 1 of the fused-dequant direction is implemented and gated:
- `exl3dq1` (native/kernels/exl3.vyb): one thread per tmp element, dequantizes the packed
  slab IN REGISTERS per tap (mcg + fp16 pair + f32 narrow, identical math/order to
  deq32+had132) — the expanded What (od) buffer is NEVER materialized. 3 launches instead
  of 4 (dq1 -> had232 -> cvt32), od traffic eliminated.
- `exl3inv`: builds the map's (r,c)->slot inverse on device (256 threads; the map body's
  placement fields are u16 at slot*16+12/+14 — read them ALIGNED as single u16 loads; the
  initial hi/lo split read at +13 was misaligned (716) and poisoned the context).
- GOTCHA (cost cycles): Vyb's `ld_u16` compiles to a 4-byte ld.global.u32 — a u16 load at
  a 2-mod-4 address is MISALIGNED. The deq path's u16 loads are always 4-aligned by
  construction (base = 16-aligned + slot*16); any new u16/u32 table needs 4-byte entries
  (the inverse map uses u32 per entry, st_u32/ld_u32).
- GATED: `FUSED_DQ1 lrc=0 sync=0` and the produced td2 is BIT-IDENTICAL to the separate
  deq32+had132 path on spot checks across the first 6 f32 pairs (12 f32 elements,
  denormals included) — native/out/cvt_probe23-24.log. The probe's digest helper could not
  be used for the full-buffer compare (Int64 overflow in the 2^40 digest scaling on
  f32-pair-as-f64 values + 4-byte DtoH fails in the binding — dtoh32 returns 0.0 on every
  call; use 8-byte dtoh reads and raw spot comparisons).
- PERF: fused dq1 = 115 ms per matrix vs ~14 ms for the separate deq32+had132 — the
  one-thread-per-output design pays 128x redundant dequant (each output thread
  re-dequantizes its 128-col row strip; ~0.84 TOPS effective).
NEXT (the performance redesign): shared-memory cooperative version — per 128x128 tile,
cooperatively dequantize the What tile into shared memory (64 KB f32; sm_86 allows 100 KB
with opt-in), syncthreads, then the 128-tap MACs read shared. PREREQ: verify Vyb exposes
a barrier/syncthreads intrinsic (grep src/vre/semantic_internal.hpp; rpf_gemm128.vyb's
mma+shared path proves shared memory works — check how it synchronizes). Fallback if no
barrier exists: deq into shared via separate launch is meaningless (global round-trip
returns) — then the win must come from the mma path (rpf_gemm128 pattern) instead.
Also note: the probe's two-write repro section SEGVs (host) — its first exl3_deq's
freedom exit frees the trellis String (param born elsewhere); do not run that section
when gating (the fused-gate block runs before it and is unaffected).

copied from /mnt/apex/rick/Projects/VybForge/native/ where it was authored). Summary:
fused-dequant fp32 GEMM — weights stay packed (q4_K/q6_K/f32-slab, `wty` dispatch),
dequant happens in registers inside the matmul kernel (`gemm_dq_f32.vyb`, one thread per
output, reusing the bit-exact q4k/q6k block math), accumulation in f32, packed slabs

gate on blk.0 + full-layer forward). File plan: kernel in native/kernels/, driver in
native/host/, oracle compare in native/tools/, Makefile targets.
STALE vs current state (read before implementing):
1. Constraint 1 ("no shared memory") is OUT OF DATE — the compiler now exposes

   runs mma.m16n8k16 with shared A/B staging. Tiled/mma designs are back on the table.
2. The doc targets the Qwen3-4B q4_K/q6_K path; the GLM-5.3 path uses EXL3 — the GLM
   analog is the f32 transform kernels already landed (exl3had132/had232/deq32), and the
   open GLM item is fusing deq+transform, not deq+GEMM.

### ARCHITECTURE DIRECTIVE (Rick, 2026-11-17): the production-engine pattern
All production engines share one shape, and VybForge follows it ultimately:
- llama.cpp (CUDA): weights stay packed in their GGUF quant; each kernel dequantizes a
  tile to fp32 IN REGISTERS on the fly, matmul with fp32 accumulate; KV cache f16 by
  default. Nothing is ever materialized as an expanded weight copy — 63.9 MB packed
  stays 63.9 MB (vs the 807 MB f64 expansion).
- vLLM: fp16/bf16 compute throughout, fp32 accumulation inside GEMMs; int4/int8/FP8
  dequantized to fp16 in registers per-tile (Marlin: int4->fp16 direct). Never f64.
- TensorRT-LLM: same — fp16/bf16/fp8 compute, weight-only dequant in-kernel, fp8/fp16 KV.
- ExLlamaV2/V3 (the closest analog: lefty/righty DeepSeek EXL3 2.9 bpw, mixed-quant
  per-layer dequant): EXL3/QT -> fp16 in registers during the matmul, fp32 accumulate,
  fp16 KV cache.
UNIVERSAL PATTERN: packed storage, dequant-to-fp16/fp32 in registers inside the GEMM,
fp32 accumulation, f64 NOWHERE (f64 is scientific-computing/datacenter only). The
current VybForge shape (dequant once to an expanded buffer, then dense GEMM) matches
llama.cpp's CPU strategy, not its GPU one. The fused-dequant kernel (FUSED-DQ-GEMM
direction, PKD packed-resident) is the architectural step.
FP64/32 GOLD (prerequisite, already in place and git-tracked):
- f64 gold: native/out/prefill_vyb_45_tab.txt (f64-compute 45-layer digests)
- f32 gold: native/out/prefill_vyb_45.txt (13:54 f32 run digests, <=1.4e-6 of f64)
- element-wise: native/out/gold_l3_streams2.npy (layer-3 streams, f64 reference)
Any fused kernel gates against these within a measured tolerance (f32 band already
established: digests <=1.4e-6; element-wise TOL derived from the same run's spread).
=================== THE HOIST CONFIRMED: 2/2 GREEN ==================
The repeat run #2 of the hoisted serve: GREEN end-to-end (the draft 10788
HTTP 200, 20 rows, the P1bstep 20.2s, the digests identical, CHAIN_OK) —
2/2 green for the hoisted serve on the padded base (plus 3/3 for the
pre-hoist padded baseline). THE ENDPOINT: ~2.4 min/request, verified
reliable. The remaining: the shard walk 81s (the persistent index file),
the gemm kernel's N-tile guard in the compiler, the tokenizer port.
=================== THE SHARD-WALK CACHE: 79s -> 1ms ==================
The setup's shard walk (83 x st_open, ~79-83 s) now caches to
native/out/gx_tabs.cache (VYBFORGE_TAB_CACHE overrides; delete the file to
force a re-walk; the stamp embeds the checkpoint dir). THE SETUP: ~80 s ->
~1 s (S0cache hit, S1tab 1ms). GREEN end-to-end, the digests identical.
THREE runtime lessons landed with it: (1) a giant single write_str of ~13 MB
produced a byte-shifted file once (the per-tab small writes are exact;
write_str verified byte-exact at 1.14 MB and 10.26 MB in probes); (2) THE
LEN-PREFIX PARSER BUG: the advance must be nl+1+plen, NOT pos+1+plen (the
len-line width forgotten — the parse drifted ~5 bytes per tab and every
load failed); (3) a failed cache load MUST NOT mutate the live state
in-place — the partial tabs.set polluted the tabs and the subsequent walk
APPENDED onto the stale payloads (the doubled cache file). Parse into a
temp list; commit only when pos == the file length exactly.
=================== THE PHASE-1 PROFILE: deq32 IS THE LEVER ==================
The per-request SEC totals (cache6, green): the phase-1 ffn (the tail_site
MoE) 61.8s of the ~100s phase-1. The existing T_MOE timers split it across
the 42 MoE layers (~101-106 unique routed experts/layer): **deq=36.8s
(60%)**, mm=7.2s, resolve=5.5s. The deq = ~12,700 exl3deq32 calls (~3 per
hit expert x ~101 experts x 42 layers) at ~2.85 ms each; each deq writes
EINTER(2048) x H f64 (~84 MB) => ~29 GB/s effective on a ~900 GB/s part —
**the deq32 kernel is ~30x off memory bandwidth** (the trellis LUT +
hadamard decode is compute/latency-bound, the generated native/build/
exl3.ptx from native/exl3/ tooling). THE POTENTIAL: 36.8s -> ~2-4s at even
300 GB/s => the endpoint -> ~1.5-2 min. THE PATHS: (a) the deq32 kernel
optimization (the vectorized LUT fetches, the wider tiles, the f32 output
halving the writes if the f32 digest band is acceptable — the gate has both
the f64 and the f32 references); (b) the expert-deq LRU across the requests
if the serve goes multi-request (~101 experts x 3 x 84 MB = 25 GB/layer —
infeasible; only the shared-expert + the router weights are cache-sized);
(c) the resolve 5.5s -> ~0 via the same persistent-index treatment as the
tabs cache (the 15.5 MB index parsed once per process). The attention
(P1attn 15.2s) and the phase-1b (12.5-20s) are secondary.

The exl3deq32 kernel (native/kernels/exl3dq.vyb, the fused deq+column-transform,
32768 blocks x 256 threads, one output per thread): the phase-2 re-reads the
128-tap transform vector from the L2 PER OUTPUT — ~4.3 GB of L2 traffic per
8.4M-output call — the dominant cost (the kernel at ~30x off bandwidth).
THE 4-ROWS-PER-THREAD VARIANT (the tap loaded once per c2 serving the rows
b2, b2+16, b2+32, b2+48; the per-output c2-ascending order preserved):
**deq 36.3s -> 1.3s (28x)**, mm 7.2 -> 0.8s — BUT the DECODE_MTP gate went
RED (max_abs exactly 8388608 = kn at the gate A; the serve's draft 0 with
the logit -1e308 while ALL the digest lines stayed bit-identical — the MTP
layer's own output x2 is dump-file-only, never digest-gated). REVERTED to
the one-row phase 2; the serve is green again (the draft 10788, the digests
bit-identical). THE KEPT FIX: fp16_to_f64's exponent normalization — the
<=24-step multiply loops replaced with the one-multiply subnormal path and
the <=5-step binary exponentiation (the powers of two are exact in f64, so
the values are bit-identical) — verified: the digests unchanged.

=================== THE 4-ROW DEQ32: ROOT-CAUSED (CROSS-BLOCK SHARED) ==================
The 4-rows-per-thread variant was IMPOSSIBLE in the current kernel structure, and
the "28x speedup" was a mirage — the broken kernel read UNWRITTEN shared memory
instead of doing real work. THE MECHANISM: __syncthreads and shared memory are PER
LAUNCH-BLOCK (256 threads), and each launch-block stages ONLY its own 2 rows
(rem/128 = b2, b2+1) into its private 32KB shared window. A 4-row phase-2 reading
b2+16k reads rows OTHER blocks staged in THEIR shared — unreadable, so the
accumulators consumed unwritten shared garbage (NaN/huge f32s; the dumps showed
di() overflowing to INT64_MIN; DECODE_MTP max_abs = exactly 8388608 = 2^23).
Debug trail that cracked it: the check32 gate NEVER tested the fused dq1s (it
launches the old non-fused exl3deq32 directly) — both "check32 passes" were
meaningless for the dq1s; the serve-side T-buffer dumps (bulk_dtoh of the returned
fdc, tagged with the expert id) showed garbage from call #0; the wrapper chain
(dq1s->fd, had232->fdc, cvt32->od, return fdc) was verified launch-by-launch with
syncs passing, then a 3-way fd/fdc/od dump pinned the dq1s itself.
ALSO LEARNED (all measured, the GPU contention caveat: the ComfyUI process inflates
the deq ~2.6x when active — the comparisons must run on an idle GPU):
- A 2-rows-per-thread variant (rows b2, b2+1 = the block's own staged pair; the
  tap load serves two accumulators) IS CORRECT (the digests bit-identical) but
  NEUTRAL on speed: the phase-2 is MEMORY-LEVEL-PARALLELISM-latency-bound, not
  load-count-bound — halving the active warps halves the MLP and eats the saving.
  Both the if{while} wrapper AND the c2=128 zero-iteration trick failed to speed
  it up: the compiler branches correctly (the PTX even vectorizes the pair into
  ld.shared.v2) but the MLP is the limit.
- THE REAL 4-ROW REDESIGN (the next session, the expected ~2-4x, not 28x):
  (1) dq1s phase-1 deqs straight to GLOBAL memory (st_global.f32 to the T arena,
  gid-linear — the address math is identical to the current phase-2 stores), no
  shared staging, no barrier; (2) a NEW separate transform kernel, one thread per
  (row-group, d), reads FOUR fd rows per thread — four INDEPENDENT global loads
  per c2 iteration restore the MLP the thread-count reduction would otherwise
  lose — plus one ld_f32n tap per c2 (the H traffic /4); (3) it writes the fdc
  (f64) directly, the cvt32 retires. The chain becomes dq1s-deq-only -> had4x ->
  return fdc.
KEPT FROM THIS SESSION: the check32 gate now covers BOTH geometries (gp0 =
gate_proj cw=2048 AND dp0 = down_proj cw=4096, both vs the gold dequant digest) —
the cw=4096 path was previously untested by any gate. gold_exl3_deq.py emits dp0.
=================== THE DEQ-TO-GLOBAL REDESIGN: IMPLEMENTED, MEASURED, REVERTED ==================
The documented redesign was fully implemented and measured (the working tree, not
committed): the dq1s deq-only (the raw f32 straight to the td2 arena at the
gid-linear address, no shared/barrier), a new exl3had1x4 kernel (the 128-tap
transform, FOUR rows per thread, four independent global loads per c2 + one tap
load — the MLP preserved, the H traffic /4), the chain dq1s -> had1x4 -> had232
(fd->od) -> cvt32 (od->fdc) with all four param buffers pre-built and the kernels
launched back-to-back (one sync). RESULT: GREEN and bit-identical (the draft 576,
the digests unchanged) but deq 41.0s vs the 1-row's 36.2-36.4s — a ~13% REGRESSION,
reproduced 2x on an idle GPU. The per-stage timers: dq~1ms + had1x4~1ms +
had232+cvt~1ms per call. THE H/4 IS REAL (the had1x4's H reads ~1.07GB vs 4.3GB)
but the td2 round-trip (+67MB/call), the extra kernel stage, and the lost fusion
eat it. ALSO LEARNED: the intermediate cuCtxSynchronize calls are CHEAP (~us) —
the sync count was never the problem; the pipeline is work-bound.
VYB COMPILER QUIRKS found on the way (all worked around): a 5-term || chain fails
type inference; a 4-term string + chain fails; from<loc<CVoid>>(expr) mid-argument-
list breaks the parser's recovery (hoist to a typed var first — but ONLY the fn-
handle arg needs from<>; the param-buffer arg passes as the raw Int); the param
arena was 64B (enlarged to 256B for the 4 launch param sets).
CONCLUSION: the deq32 has NO easy win. The 1-row fused kernel stands as the best
known form (36.2-36.4s of the ~100s phase-1). The remaining ideas (the all-4-row
pipeline: the deq ALSO 4 rows/thread with 4 independent gather chains, the had232
4-row too) are documented but project to only ~1.5-2x with substantial complexity —
the deq32 optimization is CLOSED as diminishing returns unless a fresh angle appears.

=================== THE RESOLVE FAST-PATH: PARKED (VYB PARSER HOSTILE TO HashMap) ==================
The 5.5s resolve = ~4242 lazy nine-name expert resolves x ~1.3ms; the cost is
gx_resolve_tab's tab.index_of(key) — a naive ~120KB substring scan per name
(the per-layer shard tab). The fix attempt: a HashMap<String,String> name ->
"shard off len" map with O(1) lookups, built per-layer from the tail_site's own
~120KB tab (~10ms build) with a legacy-resolve fallback on any miss.
BLOCKED BY THE VYB PARSER — six syntax forms each hit a DIFFERENT misparse:
(1) module-global HashMap: member calls untypeable (the known Vec-global gap);
(2) their<HashMap<String,String>> param + statement-level nm.put(...): parsed as
a fn DECLARATION (dotted name + untyped params = "Expected parameter declaration");
(3) dummyB<Bool> = nm.put(...): the <Bool> typed-var triggers the generic-fn-decl
path, which then eats the FOLLOWING statements (the cascade);
(4) untyped dummyB = ...: "unexpected token IDENTIFIER" (a new var needs a type);
(5) if (m.put(...)) { }: "unexpected token KEYWORD_IF" right after a
HashMap<String, String>> declaration — the >> in the assignment LHS type is
lexer-ambiguous with shift, and the parser state corrupts the NEXT statement
(even though gx_plan.vyb's identical-looking HashMap<String, Int>> line works —
the difference is unresolved);
(6) untyped m = HashMap<String, String>(64): "unexpected token IDENTIFIER".
The working reference: gx_plan.vyb's LOCAL map with pos.put(ix.names.get(i), i)
statement form and pos.get(nm) else -1 — the exact same shapes fail once the map
crosses a share(all) boundary or a their<> param.
PARKED. The remaining path: a persistent per-shard SORTED name index
(gx_idx_<n>.cache, the tabs-cache pattern) + binary search in the tail_site — no
HashMap, no parser hostility, ~17 string compares per lookup (80x less scanning).
Or: fix the Vyb parser (the fn-decl vs member-call/statement ambiguity, the >>
lexer split in the type positions) — the compiler item.
=================== THE RESOLVE FAST-PATH: LANDED (779a47d) + THE PARSER FIX (Vyb d74284ca) ==================
The Vyb parser "hostility" from the parked note dissolved under a correct diagnosis: module
level only takes DECLARATIONS (all code lives in main()/fns) — most of the six repros were
probes violating that convention. The ONE real compiler bug: the aspect/bind receiver path
unwrapped ownership wrappers (their<>/my<>/our<>/view<>/borrow<>) for Vec inners only, so a
their<HashMap<K,V>> receiver dispatched its MapOps bind through the name-only Vec builtin path
("Vec::get index must be an integer"). Fixed in cgen_expr_call.cpp: the unwrap generalizes to
any non-primitive inner type (the wrapper alloca holds a T* view for structs, same as Vec).
Committed d74284ca on fix/return-path-cleanup-v2.

The fast path that finally landed (779a47d): tail_site builds a LOCAL HashMap (name ->
"shard off len") from its layer tab ONCE PER LAYER (~17ms) at layer scope — NOT per expert —
and each expert's nine-name resolve does O(1) lookups with a gx_resolve_tab fallback on any
miss. resolve 5.4s -> 0.68-0.70s per request (7.7x); deq/mm untouched; digests bit-identical;
draft 576; CHAIN_OK twice; serve RSS flat ~2GB. Two expensive lessons:
(1) a HashMap passed across a call boundary (borrow(nmap) into tail_site) DEEP-COPIES per
call — and the copies leaked: phase 2 ballooned 6GB -> 83GB and the serve was OOM-killed;
(2) a map built PER EXPERT (96x too often, inside the expert loop) also ballooned phase 2.
The map build must sit at LAYER scope and never cross a call boundary until the Vyb
their<>/borrow deep-copy semantics (and the leak) are fixed in the compiler.
Tab lines carry FOUR fields (name shard off len DTYPE) — the parser must accept nmF >= 2.
Gate status post-landing: exl3 P1 OK, check32 (gp0+dp0, run the script directly — no make
target) OK, glm0 OK, decstep OK 1.883e-12, decl3step OK 6.749e-6, DECODE_MTP_MATCH OK
(verify against the standing gold; the gold regen still gets rc=137 near expert 258 — the
verify passes with the existing gold npy), decchain still stale (pre-existing).

DEPLOYMENT CHECKLIST for any HashMap-in-loop change: (1) the build at the WIDEST loop scope,

GEMM32 PARAM GOTCHA: gemm32_r takes KSP at +48, NOT +32 (+32/+40 are legacy alpha/beta) — KSP at
+32 feeds garbage into the reduce -> CUDA 700 illegal address, which POISONS the context (every
later cuMemAlloc fails — looks like an OOM, it is not). gemm32_p: (A,B,SK,M,N,K,NBLK,KSP)@+0..56,
grid NBLK*KSP x 256 threads; A needs (M+4)*K*4 (padded rows read, never stored); SK = KSP*M*N*4.

plumbing enabler. Increments 2+: gate/up on f32 (needs the 4-byte DtoH binding question answered
