=== ROUND 94: bfgemm_p BATCHED COST CURVE — and the extrapolation that matters ===
Real weights (kv_a_proj_with_mqa: N=512, K=4096, 4.19 MB BF16), 3 timed reps after a warm-up:
    M=2    grid=2   gemm_ms=0.3333  ms/token=0.16667   bad=0  max=5.03e-08
    M=32   grid=16  gemm_ms=0.6667  ms/token=0.02083   bad=0  max=4.47e-08
    M=64   grid=32  gemm_ms=0.6667  ms/token=0.01042   bad=0  max=2.98e-08
    M=128  grid=64  gemm_ms=1.0000  ms/token=0.00781   bad=0  max=2.98e-08
Correctness holds at every batch size (max <= 5e-08 over the gate), and ms/token improves 21x from
M=2 to M=128. The curve is still falling at M=128, i.e. NOT yet bandwidth-bound.
EXTRAPOLATION TO ATTENTION (arithmetic, not measurement -- treat as an estimate):
  attention weights per layer = q_a 12.6 + kv_a 4.2 + q_b 50.3 + kv_b 33.6 + o_proj 134.2 = 234.9 MB
  measured cost at M=128 = 1.0 ms per 4.19 MB  ->  0.2387 ms/MB
  per layer = 234.9 * 0.2387 = 56.1 ms   ->  x45 layers = 2,524 ms per 128-token step = ~50.7 tok/s
  add the measured MoE at B=128 (2.43 s/step, ~52.7 tok/s) = ~4.95 s/step = ~26 tok/s
So attention ALONE lands right at the 45-50 bar, and attention+MoE together land ~26 tok/s -- BELOW the
bar. The honest conclusion is that the composed path is not yet at 45-50 and the batching thesis alone
does not get there.
WHERE THE HEADROOM IS: the kernel runs at 4.19MB/1.0ms = 4.2 GB/s effective, while this device streams
BF16 at 223.7 GB/s measured (p6r11_bfbw). That is a factor of ~53. So at these batch sizes the GEMM is
COMPUTE-bound, not bandwidth-bound -- which is exactly why ms/token kept falling with M and why raising
M further is the cheapest remaining lever (M=256/512 should keep improving until the 223 GB/s wall).
NEXT: extend the sweep to M=256 and M=512; if ms/token keeps halving, the bar is reachable by batch
size alone. If it flattens early, the kernel needs a compute fix (more ILP / fewer loads per MAC).
=== ROUND 95: THE BATCH CURVE PLATEAUS AT M=256 — batch leverage is exhausted ===
    M=2    0.6667 ms  0.33333 ms/token
    M=32   0.3333     0.01042
    M=64   0.3333     0.00521
    M=128  1.0000     0.00781
    M=256  1.6667     0.00651   <- plateau
    M=512  3.3333     0.00651   <- identical, no further gain
Gates clean at every size (bad=0, max <= 5.03e-08).
MEASUREMENT CAVEAT (important for anything built on these numbers): time_mono_millis has 1 ms
resolution and these runs are 1-10 ms over 3 reps, so every figure is quantised to a multiple of
0.333 and differences under ~30% are noise. The plateau itself is solid (M=256 and M=512 give the
identical quantised value); individual points below M=256 are not.
AT THE PLATEAU, 0.00651 ms/token per 4.19 MB of weights (0.001554 ms/token/MB):
    attention 234.9 MB/layer * 0.001554 * 45 layers = 16.4 ms/token  = ~61 tok/s
    + MoE at B=128 (18.97 ms/token, from the 2.43 s/step measurement)  = 35.3 ms/token = ~28 tok/s
So: attention ALONE clears the 45-50 bar with room; the COMPOSED path is ~28 tok/s and needs ~1.8x.
The kernel saturates at roughly 1.07 G MAC / 3.33 ms = ~322 GFLOP/s f32, against 223.7 GB/s of
bandwidth headroom -- it is compute-bound, so more batch will not help. Raising GFLOP/s (independent
accumulators per thread, fewer loads per MAC, f32 vectorisation) is now the only remaining lever.
NEXT: measure the plateau more precisely (>=20 reps so 1 ms quantisation stops swamping the result),
then decide between kernel compute work and measuring the composed path end to end.
=== ROUND 96: bfgemm8 (8 rows/thread) — 1.67x, and the MoE becomes the bottleneck ===
Same algebra, same TRANSP addressing, same KSP split; 8 independent accumulator chains per thread and
half the bf16 loads per MAC. CERTIFIED by both standing gates first:
    T all8: 36 72 0 0 0 0     (micro-test still exact)
    gates at every M: bad=0, max <= 3.68e-08
COST CURVE (ms/token, bfgemm_p -> bfgemm8):
    M=128   0.00781 -> 0.00521
    M=256   0.00651 -> 0.00391
    M=512   0.00651 -> 0.00391
    M=1024  (not run) -> 0.00391   <- new plateau, still exactly 0.00390625
    at M=1024 the kernel sustains 2.15 G MAC / 4 ms = ~537 GFLOP/s f32 (up from 322).
So the ILP change bought 1.67x and moved the plateau from M=256 to M=256 (value 0.00651 -> 0.00391).
UPDATED PROJECTION (arithmetic, on the plateau value 0.00390625 ms/token per 4.19 MB):
    attention 234.9 MB/layer * 0.0009323 * 45 = 9.86 ms/token = ~101 tok/s   (comfortably past the bar)
    MoE at B=128                              = 18.97 ms/token
    composed                                  = 28.8 ms/token = ~34.7 tok/s  (still short of 45-50)
THE BOTTLENECK HAS MOVED: MoE is now 66% of the composed cost. The next 1.5x has to come from the MoE
path (mvnodB / the fused decode matvec + expert gather), not from attention.
NOTE the timing caveat still applies (1 ms resolution); the plateau value repeats exactly at 256/512/
1024 which makes it trustworthy, but the small-M points are noise.
=== ROUND 97: ILP ON THE MoE MATVEC MAKES IT WORSE — the MoE's cost is the DECODE, not the dot ===
Applied the winning attention pattern (4 independent output columns per thread, X loaded once and
reused) to mvnodB as mvnodB4. Measured both through p6r10's batch sweep (P6_MVK selects the kernel):
    B        mvnodB ms   ms/token      mvnodB4 ms   ms/token
    8        0.60        0.0750        0.70         0.0875
    32       0.80        0.0250        1.30         0.0406
    64       1.00        0.0156        2.00         0.0313
    128      1.30        0.0102        3.40         0.0266     <- 2.6x WORSE
mvnodB4 is neutral at B<=4 and progressively worse above that. Reason: the 4-column mapping concentrates
all 128 columns into 32 threads, so 224 of the kernel's 256 threads sit idle, and the dot was never the
bottleneck in the first place. It is now REMOVED from the production path (the kernel stays in
exl3mv.vyb as a documented dead end).
WHY THIS MATTERS: it contradicts the "more ILP will help the MoE too" assumption and confirms the
original diagnosis from the gather spike (round 64: real_gather_ms=0.65 vs nogather_ms=0.05, ratio 13x).
The MoE's time is in DECODING the packed EXL3 weights (4-bit nibble extraction, tile lookup, LUT
gather), not in the multiply-accumulate. Any further MoE gain has to come from making the decode
cheaper per weight (fewer bit ops per element, better LUT locality), NOT from restructuring the dot.
LESSON: ILP restructuring is not a universal lever. It helped bfgemm8 (dense, 1-accumulator-per-row
starvation) and hurt mvnodB4 (latency/occupancy-bound with 256 threads already active).
CURRENT COMPOSED ESTIMATE: attention ~101 tok/s (bfgemm8 plateau), MoE ~52.7 tok/s (B=128, 2.43 s/step)
-> composed ~34.7 tok/s. Still short of the 45-50 bar; the MoE decode is the wall.
=== ROUND 98: DECODE BROKEN DOWN -- it is only 0.16 ms; the MoE's cost is the DOT/ATOMICS ===
native/kernels/dparts.vyb splits EXL3 decode into cumulative stages (tile lookup -> nibble assembly ->
hash -> LUT), each doing mvnodB's exact tile/thread mapping, each with an anti-DCE store, timed at 50
reps (0.02 ms resolution):
    tile  0.060 ms   (37%)   the inv[] slot lookup + address arithmetic
    nib   0.120 ms   (+0.060) nidx/pp loads, byte loads, nibble shifts, w16 assembly
    hash  0.120 ms   (+0.000) the multiply-xor is FREE
    lut   0.160 ms   (+0.040) the two 65536-entry f32 gathers
So decoding one expert projection = 0.16 ms total. THIS CONTRADICTS MY OWN PREVIOUS CONCLUSION
(round 97 said the decode was the wall): at B=128 mvnodB costs 1.3 ms per launch, of which only 0.16
is decode -- the other ~1.1 ms is the DOT.
AND THE DOT IS PATHETIC: 2048 outputs x 128 tokens x 8 MACs = 2.1 M MACs in 1.1 ms = 1.9 GFLOP/s. The
inner loop is 8 MACs then `atomic_add_f32` into T -- so there is roughly ONE ATOMIC PER 8 MACs, and the
atomics are to global memory, contended across the 8 CTAs that k-split each output.
NEW HYPOTHESIS (testable, cheap): the MoE's cost at batch is ATOMIC-BOUND, not arithmetic-bound. The
test is a variant that PLAIN-STORES instead of atomically adding (numerically wrong, but it measures
the ceiling exactly). If that variant runs in ~0.2 ms instead of 1.3 ms, atomics are ~85% of the cost
and the fix is a real reduction (each CTA owning exclusive outputs, or a separate reduce pass) rather
than micro-optimising the decode.
REMINDER OF THE LARGER PICTURE: attention ~101 tok/s (certified bfgemm8), MoE ~52.7 tok/s,
composed ~34.7 tok/s vs the 45-50 bar. The MoE dot is where the remaining 1.5x lives.
=== ROUND 99: ATOMICS REFUTED, AND THE REAL MoE WASTE FOUND: 36x OF DEAD WORK PER LAUNCH ===
(mvnodB_st: plain store instead of atomic_add_f32 at B=128 -> 1.4 ms vs 1.3 ms. IDENTICAL within
noise. The atomics hypothesis is dead; mvnodB_st stays in exl3mv.vyb as a documented timing artifact
and must never be used for results -- its k-split races by construction.)
WHAT THE MEASUREMENT ACTUALLY REVEALED:
  - one mvnodB launch does 128 x 2048 x 4096 = 1.074 G MAC in 1.3 ms = 826 GFLOP/s. That is FASTER than
    bfgemm8's 537 GFLOP/s, i.e. the MoE dot is not slow at all. My round-98 estimate of 1.9 GFLOP/s was
    simply wrong (I forgot the k-split replication when counting work).
  - the waste is elsewhere: with B=128 tokens and top-8 routing, there are 128*8 = 1024 assignments
    spread over <=288 experts = ~3.6 tokens per expert. But the kernel multiplies ALL 128 tokens by
    EVERY expert's weights. Useful fraction 3.6/128 = 2.8% -> **36x of the MoE's work is dead**.
  - cross-check: 288 experts x 3 projections = 864 launches x 1.3 ms = 1.12 s/step, the same order as
    the measured 2.43 s/step (the rest being Hadamard passes, the gate, and scheduling).
IF THE MATVEC WERE COMPACTED TO THE ASSIGNED TOKENS (the gather that every production MoE does):
    MoE 2.43 s/step -> ~0.03 s/step = ~0.2 ms/token
    composed with attention (9.86 ms/token) = ~10.1 ms/token = **~99 tok/s**, comfortably past the bar
THIS IS NOW THE SINGLE HIGHEST-VALUE CHANGE IN THE PROJECT -- worth far more than any kernel
micro-optimisation: it is not making the arithmetic faster, it is not doing 36x of it.
IMPLEMENTATION SHAPE: the router already produces the top-k assignment. Build, per expert, the list of
token indices routed to it (a small scatter), then launch the expert matvec over only those tokens
(nin reads gathered by index; cw outputs scattered back to their token rows). At B=128 the per-expert
batch is ~4, so the launch count stays the same but each launch is ~32x cheaper.
=== ROUND 100: THE 36x CLAIM IS WRONG — PRODUCTION ALREADY COMPACTS; THE REAL GAP IS THE DEQ PATH ===
I said I would read the production MoE path instead of trusting my synthetic sweep. I did, and it
refutes round 99:
  - glm_engine.vyb:3235-3250 collects each expert's (token, lane) pairs into toks/lanes,
    nt = toks.len(), and skips experts with nt == 0.
  - :3359 feeds that nt straight into the matvec: mm_host32(sub, nt, H, dWg, EINTER, ...).
  So production ALREADY does the per-expert gather. There is no 36x of dead work; my figure came from
  p6r10's synthetic sweep, which deliberately runs all B tokens against one expert. Round 99's
  conclusion is WITHDRAWN.
WHAT THE READ FOUND INSTEAD, and it is the bigger prize:
  - production dequantizes every expert projection to f32 first (exl3_deq32f -> dWg/dWu/dWd, 12.6 MB
    packed -> ~32 MB f32) and then runs the f32 GEMM (mm_host32 -> gemm32_p/gemm32_r).
  - the FUSED matvec (mvnod4 single-token, mvnodB batched) reads the PACKED weights and does the
    matvec directly, measured at 0.600 ms per expert-projection vs 13 ms for deq+gemm -- 21x cheaper.
  - that fused path is a prototype: grep for mvnod in glm_engine returns NOTHING. It was never wired in.
So the single highest-value change is to replace exl3_deq32f + mm_host32 with the fused matvec inside
the MoE tail. Production already hands it a compacted nt, which is exactly the shape the fused kernel
wants. Two known traps to respect when wiring it: (1) call `mvnod4` for the single-token case, NOT
`exl3mv1s4` -- byte-identical bodies resolve differently (the entry-identity defect in this handoff);
(2) mvnodB4 and mvnodB_st in exl3mv.vyb are dead ends/timing artifacts and must not be used.
SCOPE FOR THE CHANGE: wire ONE projection (gate) behind a flag, verify it bit-for-bit against the
existing deq+gemm output on a real expert, then extend to up/out. Do not swap all three at once.
=== ROUND 101: FUSED-PATH WIRING — SCOPED, NOT STARTED (and why that is the right call) ===
I read the wiring surface instead of starting it. The change is bigger than one step and I am stopping
short of it deliberately, at the end of a long session, rather than leaving a half-built edit inside a
certified production engine.
WHAT IT NEEDS (all inside tail_site, glm_engine.vyb:2923, 4729 lines):
  1. device upload of the expert's trellis + su + sv (f16) + LUT -- the deq path does this internally
  2. the Hadamard build (had_hoist32/hdg32 exists) and handles for exl3xhat, exl3yhat, mvnodB, which
     are NOT in the engine at all (grep: only had_hoist32 matches)
  3. sub is a host Vec<Float> (f64): upload -> cast32 -> xhatB (batched, params +0 x,+8 out,+16 H,
     +24 su,+32 nin,+40 B) -> mvnodB (+0 T,+8 X,+24 cw,+40 q,+48 td,+56 inv,+144 LUT,+168 B,+176 nin)
     -> yhatB -> dtoh. mm_host32 does all of this in ONE call today.
  4. three handles through a 28-parameter signature, or load_fn per layer as a workaround
  5. bit-for-bit comparison against dWg + mm_host32 before anything replaces it
WHY IT IS WORTH DOING (unchanged): the fused path measured 0.600 ms vs 13 ms per expert-projection,
i.e. 21x, and round 71 already verified it against the deq path at 8.34e-07 for one expert/token. The
win is real; only the plumbing is missing.
RECOMMENDED SEQUENCE FOR THE NEXT SESSION (in this order, each verified before the next):
  a. extend p6r10's existing FUSED_PATH vs DEQ_PATH comparison from nt=1 to nt=4 using mvnodB +
     xhatB/yhatB (the true production shape -- compacted tokens). No production code touched.
  b. only then edit tail_site: add the three handles to the signature, add a `P6_FUSED_GATE` flag
     branch that computes gv both ways and prints max_abs, with the deq path still authoritative.
  c. flip the flag to use the fused gv, verify the 45-layer digest is unchanged, then extend to up/out.
TRAPS TO CARRY FORWARD: call `mvnod4` (not `exl3mv1s4`) for single-token; `mvnodB4` and `mvnodB_st` in
exl3mv.vyb are dead ends / timing artifacts; verify each device buffer on device before the next stage
(this is what finally cracked bfgemm_p); validate the instrument against a known value before trusting
any number from it.
=== ROUND 102: FUSED PATH VERIFIED AT THE PRODUCTION SHAPE (nt=4, compacted) ===
Extended p6r10's comparison from nt=1 (round 71) to nt=4 with the batched kernels production would
actually use -- xhatB -> mvnodB -> yhatB -- against the existing deq32f + mm_host32 reference:
    P6R10 DEQ_PATH   deq_ms=4  mm_ms=4  total_ms=8
    P6R10 FUSED_PATH xhat_ms=0 mv_ms=1  yhat_ms=0 total_ms=1     (nt=1)
    P6R10 NT4 mv_ms=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.0132789611816406e-06 ref_max=1.67340087890625 of 8192
    P6R10 NT4 fused0=-0.25333678722381592  ref0=-0.25333666801452637
    P6R10 NT4_PASS fused == deq+gemm for 4 compacted tokens
So the fused path is numerically equal to the production path for the compacted multi-token shape, over
all 8192 outputs of a real expert's gate projection, at 1.01e-06 max error.
Timing at nt=4: the fused matvec alone is 1 ms against deq 4 ms + gemm 4 ms = 8 ms for the same result
(the deq cost is per-expert and does not amortise with nt, which is why the win ratio is larger at
small nt -- 21x at nt=1 -- and ~4-8x at nt=4; production's nt averages ~3.6 at B=128).
THE LAST GATE BEFORE WIRING IS NOW CLEARED. What remains is pure plumbing in tail_site, precisely
enumerated in round 101 (three kernel handles, trellis/su/sv/LUT uploads, the f64->f32->xhatB->mvnodB
->yhatB chain, and a flag-gated dual-compute comparison). Nothing about the mathematics is in doubt.
=== ROUND 103: FUSED-GATE VERIFICATION IS WIRED INTO tail_site (flag-gated, default OFF) ===
glm_engine.vyb, immediately after gv/uv are computed by mm_host32, there is now a P6_FUSED_GATE block:
  - loads exl3mv.ptx entries xhatB / yhatB / mvnodB / f16lut
  - builds the f16 LUT locally (the engine has none -- grep found zero references)
  - uploads this expert's suh/svh (g_ge_1/g_ge_2) and the compacted `sub` activations
  - runs f64 -> cast32 -> xhatB(su) -> mvnodB(B=nt) -> yhatB(sv) -> D2H
  - compares against gv element-by-element and prints
      FGATE expert=<e> nt=<nt> count_over_1e-4=<n> max_abs=<m> of <nt*EINTER>
  - frees every temporary (this runs per expert, so a leak here would be fatal)
  - the deq path REMAINS AUTHORITATIVE: FV is never used downstream, and with P6_FUSED_GATE unset the
    block is skipped entirely, so default behaviour is bit-identical to before.
STATUS: compiles clean (no semantic errors from the engine or any module). NOT yet observed running:
  - glm_l3_full.vyb is a stale driver (pre-existing "Unsupported cast i64 -> {ptr,i64,i64}").
  - glm_decode_chain.vyb printed CHAIN_FAIL kernels on lefty. CORRECTION: this was NOT the load_fn
    defect as first stated -- native/build/gemm.ptx (entry "gemm") was simply absent from lefty's
    build dir. Building it from native/kernels/gemm.vyb and rsyncing removed the failure. Lesson
    repeated: check for the missing artifact before blaming the compiler.
So the flag's runtime verification is blocked on that driver/kernel-load issue, NOT on anything in the
edit. The numerical equality it would check is already established independently: p6r10 NT4 verified
the identical chain (xhatB -> mvnodB -> yhatB vs deq32f + mm_host32) at the production shape, all 8192
outputs, max_abs 1.01e-06.
NEXT: get one driver through to tail_site (fix the load_fn resolution, or use glm_serve/glm_prefill
which may not hit the failing entry), then run with P6_FUSED_GATE=1 and read the FGATE lines. Once those
show 0 over 1e-4 across a layer's experts, flip the flag to USE the fused gv, re-check the layer digest
against gold, and then extend to up/down.
=== ROUND 104: SESSION RULES COMMITTED TO THE SKILL ===
The measurement lessons from this session now live in the `vybforge-gpu-model` skill as
references/measurement-discipline.md (the SKILL.md body is at 99,849/100,000 chars and CANNOT take a
pointer to it -- it needs consolidating before anything else is added; skill_view still surfaces the
file via linked_files, so it is discoverable).
Contents: the bulk_dtoh f64-width trap and its garbage signatures; bf16deq vs bf16deq32 and the z>0
transpose; literal bf16 constant packing; the verified reference values for
layers.3 kv_a_proj_with_mqa; the three standing micro-tests (p6r14_tiny / p6r16_real / p6r10 NT4);
verify-each-stage-on-device; the blame order (artifact present -> argument types -> readback width ->
compiler last); time_mono_millis's 1 ms quantisation; ILP being non-universal (bfgemm8 1.67x faster,
mvnodB4 2.6x slower); and plain-store timing variants.

P6_FUSED_GATE=1 ~/vyb glm_decode_chain.vyb on lefty ran the full 1800 s cap and was killed still inside
the shard/index walk: the only output was a stream of
    G9PROBE found=1 off=1269539xx nbytes=256 pathlen=156 path=.../model-000NN-of-00083.safetensors
with offsets that DECREASE monotonically (126953992 -> 126953680 -> 126953368 -> ... -> 126951344)
across many shards. That is the per-layer index walk (glm_decode_chain:2244), not MoE work, and it never
finished. NO FGATE LINE WAS EVER PRINTED, so the in-engine verification remains UNOBSERVED.
This is a DRIVER-SIDE cost, not a problem with the fused-gate block: the block sits inside tail_site,
which runs long after all resolving. The handoff already lists this area as pre-existing trouble
("gx/gxload segfault at shard walk", "decchain monolith stale vs the engine split").
STATE OF THE WIRING (unchanged and safe): the P6_FUSED_GATE block is committed, compiles clean, is
skipped entirely unless the env var is set, and the deq path stays authoritative. The numerical equality
it would report is ALREADY established independently by p6r10 NT4 (xhatB -> mvnodB -> yhatB vs
deq32f + mm_host32, all 8192 outputs, max_abs 1.01e-06).
TO ACTUALLY OBSERVE FGATE, one of these is needed first:
  a. a driver that reaches tail_site without the 45-layer index walk -- a single-layer or single-expert
     path (glm_prefill may be lighter; untested), or a P6_FUSED_ONCE guard plus an early exit
  b. fixing the index walk itself: `native/out/gx_tabs.cache` already exists as a per-layer tab cache,
     but this driver appears to rebuild the tab rather than load the cache
PRIORITY NOTE: (b) is worth doing for its own sake -- every full-chain run pays this cost.
=== ROUND 106: TAB CACHE WIRED INTO glm_decode_chain -- but the driver is the wrong vehicle ===
Added the "GXTAB1" cache load to glm_decode_chain (copied from glm_serve's proven loader) and guarded
the 83-shard walk behind it; also added the find_from helper the file lacked. Two findings:
  1. The cache never hit with the original signature: the stored tag embeds the checkpoint DIR, and
     lefty built it under a different one ("CHAIN TABCACHE miss -> walking shards"). The payload holds
     only (name, shard#, offset, nbytes, dtype) -- NO absolute paths -- so the cache is
     dir-independent; the match is now on the "GXTAB1 " format tag alone, with the payload still
     validated end-to-end (45 entries, exact byte consumption) before acceptance.
  2. Even with that, the driver is still not a quick verification vehicle: its first output is
     "CHAIN phase 1: prefill 20 tokens, 45 layers" -- a full 45-layer prefill -- and the G9PROBE
     stream comes from inside that phase, not from the tab walk alone. Two runs at 700-1800 s never
     reached tail_site, so NO FGATE line yet exists.
CONCLUSION: stop trying to observe the fused gate through this driver. It is a 45-layer prefill
harness; the verification needs a purpose-built single-expert path (or P6_FUSED_ONCE + early exit
immediately after the first expert's FGATE print). The cache fix stays -- it is a real improvement for
every future full-chain run -- but it is not sufficient by itself.
=== ROUND 107: TAB CACHE CONFIRMED WORKING; G9PROBE WAS NEVER THE WALK ===
Two runs side by side prove the round-106 fix:
    before: "CHAIN TABCACHE miss -> walking shards"   then G9PROBE stream
    after : "CHAIN TABCACHE hit native/out/gx_tabs.cache"  then the SAME G9PROBE stream
and the G9PROBE offsets are IDENTICAL across both runs (1646384088, 174881192, 753908392, 70552288,
126952376, 126951992, 126951344, 128533520, ...). So:
  - the cache load works: the shard walk is now skipped on a hit
  - G9PROBE comes from the attention/prefill path, NOT from the tab walk -- my round-105 attribution
    was wrong
  - the remaining runtime is "CHAIN phase 1: prefill 20 tokens, 45 layers", i.e. real model work that
    no cache can remove
Therefore neither 30-minute run could ever have reached tail_site, and the fused-gate verification
needs its own vehicle rather than a 45-layer prefill harness. The cache improvement stays (it removes
the walk for every future full-chain run and now demonstrably hits).
=== ROUND 108: NO DRIVER CURRENTLY REACHES glm_engine.tail_site -- that is why FGATE never prints ===
Chased the verification vehicle properly and found the real reason the FGATE block has never run:
  - glm_decode_chain.vyb defines its OWN local moe_tail_step (14 params) and never imports the
    engine's -- my block is not in that code path at all.
  - glm_prefill.vyb likewise defines its OWN local tail_site (26 params, line 1458).
  - glm_serve.vyb DOES call the engine's tail_site (28 params, line 707) -- and cannot compile:

    glm_engine.vyb declares its own extern "C" block (line ~35) that duplicates the new
    bindings/cuda/cuda_binding.vyb module.
So NO current driver reaches glm_engine.tail_site. The FGATE block is correct and compiles, but nothing
can execute it until one of these is done:
  a. remove glm_engine's duplicate extern block and import cuda_binding instead (the clean fix; touches
     the engine's FFI declarations only, but every engine caller must still compile)
  b. add the FGATE block to glm_decode_chain's LOCAL moe_tail_step too (duplicate logic in a second
     place -- works, but invites drift)
c. fix arity/copy-drift so glm_decode_chain could call the engine's tail_site instead of its own copy
   (the real long-term answer: the driver's copies are why the engine and the driver keep diverging)
ALSO DONE THIS ROUND: VYBFORGE_NLAY -> nlay in glm_decode_chain (1 layer runs in seconds once the tab
cache hits: "CHAIN TABCACHE hit" then "CHAIN_OK"), and the whole native/glm53 module dir synced to lefty
(it only had 2 of 4 files, which is why glm_serve failed on import deq_cache first).
MEASURED FACTS FROM THE FAST RUNS: with the tab cache, a 1-layer and a 4-layer glm_decode_chain both
complete in seconds ("CHAIN_OK"), so the harness is now cheap -- but both use the LOCAL tail_site, so

=== ROUND 109: glm_serve NOW COMPILES (three real defects fixed) -- but fails at runtime pre-layer ===
Removed the duplicate FFI and two related breaks. All three were genuine, and each blocked use of the
engine from any driver:
  1. glm_engine declared its own extern "C" block duplicating bindings/cuda/cuda_binding.vyb, so any
     module importing BOTH died with "Duplicate symbol after splice: 'cuModuleLoadData'". Now the six
     calls the binding carries are IMPORTED (cuMemFree_v2, cuMemAlloc_v2, cuMemcpyHtoD_v2,
     cuCtxSynchronize, cuModuleLoadData, cuModuleGetFunction -- signatures verified identical) and only
     the four the binding LACKS stay declared: cuMemGetInfo_v2, vyb_str_stats, cuda_launch4i,
     malloc_trim. (A blind swap would have broken the engine: the binding has none of those four.)
  2. my P6_FUSED_GATE block used zero_bytes(..), which does not exist in this module -> replaced with a
     wr_pdev zero loop.
  3. had_hoist (glm_engine:1566) lacked share(all) while its twin had_hoist32 had it, so glm_serve's
     call was "Undefined identifier: had_hoist". Added share(all).
VERIFIED: glm_serve.vyb now compiles with zero semantic errors, and on lefty it RUNS:
    SETUP S0walk 560114099 / SETUP S0cache hit / SETUP S1tab 0
    DEQFAIL alloc q rc=700 len=16777216
    CHAIN_FAIL mtp stage deq
rc=700 is a runtime context/allocation failure in the mtp stage, BEFORE any layer runs, so FGATE is
still unobserved. The extern swap is not implicated: the binding's declarations are signature-identical
to the engine's originals. This is the next thing to chase, and it is now the ONLY thing between the
flag and a printed verification.
=== ROUND 110: THE "PASTE THE BLOCK" OPTION IS NOT A PASTE — the driver's copy is older ===
Checked glm_decode_chain's local moe_tail_step (line 3014) before porting the FGATE block into it. It is
the PREVIOUS GENERATION of the MoE tail:
    signature: (streams1, lpre, tab, dir, total, mapbody, gt<GxLoc>, fgemm, fbf, fdeq,
                fhgen, fhad1, fhad2, hdg)     -- 14 params
    uses exl3_deq + fgemm (the f64 path, :3233/:3238/:3243), NOT exl3_deq32f + mm_host32
The engine's tail_site is the f32 generation (28 params). So the FGATE block cannot simply be pasted:
it needs the f32 kernel handles (fdeq32/fdqs/fdqs4/fhad132/fhad232/fcvt32/hdg32/fcast32/fgp/fgr/pb32)
threaded into the driver's function first, i.e. a port to a different kernel set.
Also checked rc=700 on glm_serve: nvidia-smi reports N/A on GB10 (unified memory) and there are no
leftover vyb processes, so it is not resource contention -- it is a context error in an unexercised path.
WHERE THIS LEAVES THE VERIFICATION: both vehicles are blocked, for different and now-precisely-known
reasons (glm_serve: rc=700 in the mtp stage; glm_decode_chain: pre-f32-generation tail). The fused-gate
block itself is correct, compiles, and is default-off. Its numerical equality is proven by p6r10 NT4.
RECOMMENDATION: stop treating in-engine FGATE as the gate. Either (a) port the driver's tail to the f32
path -- which is the same work as making the driver call the engine's tail_site, i.e. the real
consolidation -- or (b) accept p6r10's proof and flip the flag behind a gold-digest check, which would
catch any error the moment it appears. Both are larger than a verification step; neither is a paste.
=== ROUND 111: THE ENGINE IS HEALTHY; THE FLAG IS UNREACHABLE; glm_serve's rc=700 IS PRE-EXISTING ===
1. The extern swap is PROVEN HARMLESS: p6r14_tiny (which imports glm_engine and therefore exercises the
   imported cuMemAlloc_v2 / cuMemcpyHtoD_v2 / cuModuleLoadData / cuModuleGetFunction path) still passes
   on lefty -- "T all8: 36 72 0 0 0 0", "T o[0]=36 o[1]=72". Allocations, uploads and launches all work.
   So rc=700 in glm_serve is specific to that driver's mtp stage, not to the FFI consolidation.
2. BLOCKING FACT for the next decision: glm_engine.tail_site still has NO working caller. glm_serve is

   digest could not be checked. Round 110's recommendation (flip and gate on the digest) is not
   executable until a driver reaches tail_site.
3. rc=700 = CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS at a 16 MB cuMemAlloc_v2 inside deq_bf16, i.e. the context is
   already faulted by something EARLIER in glm_serve's mtp stage. Not resource pressure (no leftover vyb
   processes; nvidia-smi reports N/A on GB10 unified memory).
SO THE DEPENDENCY CHAIN IS: fix glm_serve's mtp-stage fault -> tail_site becomes reachable -> FGATE
prints -> flip the flag -> gold digest confirms. Everything downstream is already correct and committed.
THE ONE REMAINING BLOCKER IS A RUNTIME FAULT IN AN UNEXERCISED glm_serve PATH.
=== ROUND 112: glm_serve IS REACHABLE -- it is an HTTP server, and MTP can be skipped ===
Two things unblocked the engine-path verification:
 1. VYBFORGE_SKIP_MTP=1 now skips mtp_stage_deqs -- the 19-tensor MTP deq stage that faults with rc=700
    BEFORE any layer runs. mdw (its output) is consumed ONLY by mtp_layer_step (speculative decoding),
    so the plain forward does not need it. With the flag, glm_serve.vyb gets past setup:
        SETUP S3stage skipped (VYBFORGE_SKIP_MTP)
        SETUP S3listen 0 / SETUP S3listen 1
 2. glm_serve ALWAYS runs as an HTTP listener (http_listen on VYBFORGE_SERVE_PORT) -- there is no
    one-shot mode, which is why an earlier run appeared to "hang" after setup: it was waiting for a
    request. The route is  POST /v1/generate  {"prompt_ids": [...], "nsteps": N}  (glm_serve.vyb:47,572).
    So the driver is exercised by starting it and POSTing a request, on 127.0.0.1 only (LAN, never the
    Tailnet).
This is the vehicle for the in-engine FGATE verification. Nothing about the fused-gate block was wrong;
it simply needed a caller that runs.
    CORRECTION to the route signature: glm_serve.vyb:572 parses QUERY-STRING params, not JSON --
        POST /v1/generate?ids=785,1234&nsteps=1
    (the header comment at :47 says JSON, but the code reads q.index_of("ids=") / ("nsteps=") off the
    request path). Sending JSON gives "CHAIN_FAIL ids 0" and the request is dropped.
    ALSO: the ids list must contain at least 22 ids ("CHAIN_FAIL ids 2" otherwise). Phase 1 prefills
    ids[0..19] and the two teacher-forced decode steps re-walk ids[20] and ids[21], so the driver needs
    all three groups present even for nsteps=1.
=== ROUND 113: FGATE NOW RUNS IN-ENGINE -- AND IT SAYS THE FUSED PATH DISAGREES ===
The verification vehicle finally works end to end (script: scripts/run_fgate.sh on lefty):
    set VYB_STDLIB, VYBFORGE_GX_DIR; P6_FUSED_GATE=1 VYBFORGE_SKIP_MTP=1 VYBFORGE_SERVE_PORT=18102
    VYBFORGE_NLAYERS=4; start glm_serve; wait for "listening on"; then
    POST /v1/generate?ids=<22 ids>&nsteps=1        (query string, NOT json; >=22 ids required)
It produced real FGATE lines for a real layer, and the driver completed (CHAIN_OK):
    FGATE expert=0  nt=1  count_over_1e-4=2048  max_abs=3.8424 of 2048
    FGATE expert=10 nt=13 count_over_1e-4=26621 max_abs=6.1146 of 26624
    FGATE expert=5  nt=3  count_over_1e-4=6143  max_abs=4.4720 of 6144
EVERY output disagrees, at O(1) magnitude -- this is not a scaling slip, it is a different computation.
DIAGNOSTICS ADDED AND READ (FGATEDBG lines): every input and handle is CORRECT --
    h (hdg32) live, lut live, su buffer live with su_len=8192 (= 2x4096 f16, the right tensor),
    sv_len=4096 (= 2x2048 f16, the right tensor), fused0=-0.878 vs ref0=0.243, sub0=0.486 (a sane
    activation). And hdg32 is literally had_hoist32(fhgen32) -- the same call p6r10 used as hd_pre32.
So the inputs match p6r10's passing test, the handles match, the chain matches -- and the numbers do not.
THE DISCREPANCY IS THEREFORE SUBTLE AND MUST BE BISECTED, NOT REASONED ABOUT. The decisive next probe:
inside the block, ALSO run the fused chain on a SYNTHETIC input (p6r10's xv pattern) against the same
dWg, and compare against a host dot.
    - if the synthetic case PASSES in-engine, the difference is specific to `sub` (its layout or its
      scaling at this point in the layer)
    - if it FAILS, the engine's copy of the chain differs from p6r10's in some detail (grid, param
      slot, freedom nesting) and can be found by direct comparison of the two call sites
=== END ===
=== ROUND 114: SYNTHETIC INPUT FAILS TOO -> IT IS THE CALL SITE, NOT `sub` ===
The discriminator answered cleanly:
    FGATE    expert=0 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=2048 max_abs=3.8424 fused0=-0.8780  ref0=0.2433
    FGATESYN expert=0 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=2047 max_abs=1.3081 fused0=0.07559  ref0=-0.03600
With p6r10's exact synthetic input pattern the engine's chain STILL disagrees, so the problem is not
`sub`, not its layout, not its scaling: the engine's call site differs from p6r10's in some detail I
have not been able to find by inspection.
WHAT HAS BEEN CHECKED AND MATCHES (so do not re-check these):
  mvnodB argv  +0 T=dyr +8 X=dxh +24 cw=EINTER +40 q=arA +48 td=mapdev +56 inv=invbase
               +144 LUT=dLUT +168 B=nt +176 nin=H
  xhatB  argv  +0 dxf +8 dxh +16 hdg32 +24 dsu +32 H +40 nt
  yhatB  argv  +0 dyr +8 dyp +16 hdg32 +24 EINTER +32 dsv +40 nt
  grids        xhatB nt*(H/128), mvnodB (H/64)*(EINTER/128)*8, yhatB nt*(EINTER/128)
  H            hdg32 == had_hoist32(fhgen32) (literally the same call p6r10 uses as hd_pre32)
  params buf   pb32 = 192 bytes, enough for +176
  su/sv        uploaded raw from g_ge_1/g_ge_2, lengths 8192 and 4096 (2x4096 and 2x2048 f16) == correct
  T zeroing    wr_pdev loop over nt*EINTER*4 before the atomic accumulation
WHAT IS STILL UNVERIFIED AND IS THE NEXT THING TO LOOK AT:
  - that `arA`, `mapdev`, `invbase` in tail_site hold what p6r10's did at the SAME moment (arA is the
    trellis arena; if the up/down deqs or the deq itself reposition/advance any of these, the gate's
    fused pass would read the wrong arena while the deq path (which re-uploads) still works)
  - whether the engine's deq leaves `arA` bound to the GATE trellis specifically
  - the single most likely culprit: the deq path may UPLOAD THE TRELLIS INTO arA per projection, so by
    the time my block runs (after up and down), arA holds the DOWN trellis, not the gate's
RECOMMENDED: print arA/mapdev/invbase and a few dWg values, and check whether arA still corresponds to
g_ge_0 at the point the fused chain runs. If the arena is per-projection, capture the gate's arena
handles BEFORE the up/down deqs (or run the verification immediately after dWg is computed).
=== END ===
=== ROUND 115: *** FGATE PASSES IN-ENGINE *** -- the culprit was the shared deq arena ===
    FGATE    expert=0 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.9073486328125e-06 of 2048
             fused0=0.24333177506923676  ref0=0.24333183467388153
    FGATESYN expert=0 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=7.7486038208007812e-07
    FGATE    expert=1 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.2516975402832031e-06 of 2048
ROOT CAUSE: `arA` is the deq's ARENA, not a per-projection buffer. tail_site dequantises gate, then up,
then down, and each deq re-points/re-fills the arena -- so by the time the verification block ran (after
up and down), arA held the DOWN trellis. The fused pass was multiplying gate activations by down weights;
the deq path was unaffected because it re-uploads for each projection. Fix: upload the gate's trellis
(g_ge_0) into our OWN device buffer dQ and pass that as mvnodB's q, exactly as su/sv are already
uploaded from g_ge_1/g_ge_2. That single change took max_abs from 3.84 to 1.9e-06.
LESSON (worth generalising): in tail_site, ANY handle borrowed from the deq's argument list must be
treated as per-call scratch. su, sv and the trellis all had to be uploaded separately; only hdg32 (the
hoisted Hadamard), LUT, mapdev and invbase are genuinely stable across projections.
STATE: the fused gate is now verified against the production path INSIDE the engine, on real compacted
activations and on a synthetic input. Step 2 (flip the flag to USE FV, then gold digest) is unblocked.
=== ROUND 116: *** STEP 2 DONE -- THE FUSED GATE IS LIVE IN THE ENGINE *** ===
With P6_FUSED_GATE=1 and the adopt-condition (count_over_1e-4 == 0), tail_site replaces gv with the fused
result:  if (ffixed == "1" && nb2 == 0) { gv = FV }
A full 4-layer generation through the HTTP endpoint completes and agrees everywhere:
    {"draft_token": 0}                      <- identical to the pre-flip run
    FGATE expert=0  nt=1  count 0  max_abs=2.62e-06 of 2048
    FGATE expert=1  nt=1  count 0  max_abs=1.19e-06 of 2048
    FGATE expert=2  nt=3  count 0  max_abs=1.31e-06 of 6144
    FGATE expert=5  nt=3  count 0  max_abs=1.55e-06 of 6144
    FGATE expert=6  nt=1  count 0  max_abs=1.22e-06 of 2048
So one of the MoE's three projections (gate) is now served straight from the packed EXL3 weights with no
f32 materialisation, verified at ~1-3e-06 against deq+gemm on every expert of a real layer, and the
model still produces its reference output.
SAFETY: the adopt is conditional on a clean comparison, so a regression cannot silently take effect --
it degrades to the deq path. P6_FUSED_USE=0 forces the deq path even with the verification on.
REMAINING (step 3): up_proj (g_ge_3/4/5) and down_proj (g_ge_6/7/8) -- same pattern, but each must
upload ITS OWN trellis into its own dQ (the arena lesson from round 115) and its own su/sv.
=== ROUND 117: UP_PROJ FUSED AND VERIFIED -- 2 of 3 projections now serve from packed weights ===
    FGATEUP expert=0 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.5497207641601562e-06 of 2048
    FGATEUP expert=1 nt=1 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.1622905731201172e-06 of 2048
    FGATEUP expert=2 nt=3 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.9073486328125e-06 of 6144
    FGATEUP expert=5 nt=3 count_over_1e-4=0 max_abs=1.3113021850585938e-06 of 6144
    {"draft_token": 0}   <- run still completes with the same output
Up uses g_ge_3/4/5 (its trellis/suh/svh) uploaded into dQu/dsuU/dsvU -- its OWN buffers, applying the
round-115 arena lesson -- and dWu as the reference. Adopts uv = FU2 only when count_over_1e-4 == 0.
STATE: gate + up fused and verified in-engine; down_proj remains. Down has a REVERSED shape
(act is nt*EINTER, output nt*H, weights [H][EINTER]) so its chain uses nin=EINTER, cw=H, and
g_ge_6/7/8 for trellis/suh/svh.
=== ROUND 118: *** ALL THREE MoE PROJECTIONS FUSED AND VERIFIED -- THE MoE NO LONGER MATERIALISES f32 ***
    {"draft_token": 0}                                     <- same output as the deq-path baseline
    FGATE   (gate) expert=0 nt=1 count 0 max_abs=2.62e-06 of 2048
    FGATEUP (up)   expert=0 nt=1 count 0 max_abs=1.31e-06 of 2048
    FGATEDN (down) expert=0 nt=1 count 0 max_abs=7.75e-07 of 4096
    FGATEUP expert=1 nt=1 count 0 max_abs=9.54e-07 of 2048
    FGATEDN expert=1 nt=1 count 0 max_abs=5.96e-07 of 4096
    FGATEUP expert=2 nt=3 count 0 max_abs=1.91e-06 of 6144
    FGATEDN expert=2 nt=3 count 0 max_abs=6.85e-07 of 12288
Down uses the reversed shape (nin=EINTER, cw=H, g_ge_6/7/8, its own dQD/dsuD/dsvD and its own LUT), and
each projection adopts its fused result only when its own comparison is clean -- so any regression
degrades to the deq path per projection rather than corrupting the layer.
WHAT THIS MEANS: the MoE part of every layer now reads the packed EXL3 weights ONCE and multiplies
directly (xhat -> mvnodB -> yhat), with no f32 dequantisation of gate/up/down at all. That is the
21x-at-nt=1 / 4-8x-at-nt=4 saving measured in this project, now actually in production code instead of a
prototype. The whole MoE wall identified in rounds 94-99 (deq+gemm = 8 ms per expert-projection vs
fused ~1 ms) is addressed at all three call sites.
TO ENABLE: P6_FUSED_GATE=1 in the serving environment. Default is off, so nothing changes until set.
REMAINING WORK (not blockers): measure end-to-end tok/s with the flag on (expect the composed number to
move from ~34.7 toward the 60s); decide whether to make the flag the default after a full 45-layer gold
digest comparison; and root-cause the MTP rc=700 that VYBFORGE_SKIP_MTP currently skips.
=== ROUND 119: THE WIN CANNOT BE MEASURED IN THE VERIFICATION BUILD -- BY CONSTRUCTION ===
Measured SEC P1ffn (the MoE section) for an identical 4-layer / 20-token prefill:
    FG=0 (deq path)                 : 28, 28, 928, 2924
    FG=1 (fused, no synth pass)     : 28, 27, 926, 5956
FG=1 is ~2x SLOWER, and the reason is structural, not a regression: with the flag on, the block still
computes BOTH paths for every projection --
    gv = mm_host32(sub, nt, H, dWg, ...)   AND then  gv = FV      (the fused chain)
    uv = mm_host32(...)                    AND then  uv = FU2
    yv = mm_host32(...)                    AND then  yv = FD
so the verification run does deq + gemm + fused everywhere. That is exactly what a verification build
should do (it is how the equality was proven), but it makes its timing meaningless as a measure of the
fused path's cost.
To get a real number the deq must be SKIPPED, not merely compared against:
    - in fused-only mode, do NOT call exl3_deq32f for gate/up/down (it is ~4 ms per expert-projection,
      the single biggest MoE cost) and do NOT call mm_host32; compute only the fused chain and feed
      that straight to the activation.
    - that is the ~8 ms -> ~1 ms per projection change this whole line of work was for.
    - it is a small structural edit (guard the three dW* deqs and the three mm_host32 calls behind the
      same flag) but it changes control flow in the hottest part of the layer, so it deserves a careful
      pass rather than the tail of a very long session.
Also done: FGATESYN (the synthetic discriminator) is now opt-in via P6_FUSED_SYN=1 -- it answered its
question in round 114 and otherwise just doubles the work again.
STATUS: correctness is established for all three projections; the PERFORMANCE claim for them is
established only from the older standalone probes (0.600 ms vs 13 ms at nt=1; 1 ms vs 8 ms at nt=4).
The in-engine end-to-end number still needs the fused-only mode.
=== ROUND 120: P6_FUSED_ONLY WORKS -- but single-shot timing cannot measure it ===
Added P6_FUSED_ONLY=1: skips the three exl3_deq32f calls AND the three mm_host32 calls, so the layer
computes ONLY the fused chain. Two things had to be adjusted for it to run:
  - the `if (dWg == 0 || dWu == 0 || dWd == 0) { ALL_FAIL deq }` guard now only fires when
    fOnlyG == "0" -- in fused-only mode zero handles are the point, not a failure.
  - the comparison loops are skipped (nothing to compare against), and each projection adopts its
    fused result unconditionally.
It runs clean: no CHAIN_FAIL, the model completes.
BUT THE TIMING IS NOT MEASURABLE THIS WAY. SEC P1ffn across runs:
    FG=0 (deq)                 : 2015, 2024, 2924, 2925, 2018
    FG=1 + ONLY (fused-only)   : 928 (gate only), 3971 (all three)
i.e. the same configuration varies by 1.5x run to run, and the fused mode has come out both 2.2x FASTER
and 2x SLOWER. Cause: every run is a fresh process reading a 175 GB checkpoint cold from disk, so the
MoE section is dominated by I/O, not compute. p6r10's warm in-process measurements (0.600 ms vs 13 ms at
nt=1; 1 ms vs 8 ms at nt=4) remain the only trustworthy performance numbers in this project.
TO MEASURE PROPERLY: one long-lived server process, several WARM requests, and take the steady-state
steps (the script currently makes one request then kills the server). That is a harness change
(scripts/run_fgate.sh: loop N requests, report per-step deltas), not an engine change.
STATE: correctness DONE for all three projections (verified 6e-07..2.6e-06 in-engine); fused-only mode
DONE and runnable; end-to-end warm BENCHMARK still outstanding.
